READ FIRST

Why Exemplars Matter

The shorter atlas cards are useful for coverage. They are not always enough to show why a method works. These exemplars make the reasoning explicit: what is being estimated, constrained, routed, preserved, or certified, and why that object changes the problem rather than merely changing the implementation.

The shared picture is that modern AI papers are about preserving a claim through a transformation. The transformation may be training, reasoning, compression, sampling, evaluation, intervention, or deployment. The mathematics is the rule that says which transformations are allowed and which evidence proves the preserved claim survived.

A useful paper-level read should not stop at naming the object. It should say what the world is allowed to change without changing the claim, what cannot change without breaking the claim, and which observation proves the difference. That is the deeper pattern below: the method works when it protects the variable that carries truth while ignoring variation caused by format, coordinate choice, sampling accident, timing accident, or measurement noise.

EXEMPLAR 01

RAGEN-2

Reasoning Collapse As Lost Dependence

First-Principles Read

Imagine two repair tickets that ask for different fixes, but the technician writes the same checklist for both. The checklist is longer, but it is not better if it ignores the detail that separates one job from the other. RAGEN-2 asks the same thing about model reasoning: did the model do work that depends on this problem, or did training reward a reusable-looking script?

Problem

Training can make a model write more reasoning text while that text stops responding to the actual facts in the prompt. The answer may score well against the training checker, but the visible work no longer proves that the model used the evidence needed for a new case.

State Space

The state is the whole work record: the prompt, the written reasoning, tool or environment actions, observations, checker feedback, and final answer. The formal term trajectory means this ordered work path, like the full job log rather than only the final invoice.

Object

The object is the link between the problem and the work path. A healthy trace changes when the problem changes in a way that should matter; a collapsed trace is a template whose local words may vary but whose decision-relevant structure ignores the input. The formal phrase conditional dependence means the work path still changes when the relevant task evidence changes while the wrapper stays fixed.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the task-specific evidence. Training may change style, length, or the model's next-word odds, but it should not erase the facts from the prompt that decide the next action. The trace can shorten the prompt into working notes; it cannot throw away the facts that make this case different.

Allowed Movement

Reward-based training may move the model toward answers the checker likes, but only while the work path still changes when the environment facts change. The formal term policy means the model's action rule. Policy logits are the raw next-action scores before those scores are turned into odds.

Why It Works

The method works because it checks the basic property real reasoning must have: when the important facts in the problem change, the work path should change too. A receipt is useful because it is tied to the purchase; a copied receipt shape is not evidence of what happened. Reasoning traces have the same rule. Information cannot affect a later action unless it is present somewhere in the state that produced that action. If the trace no longer changes with the input, then it cannot be carrying the input-specific decision, no matter how sophisticated it sounds. The formal phrase input-trace dependence means the problem evidence is still visibly connected to the work path that produced the answer.

healthy trace: the work path still changes with the problem evidence while reward rises; collapse: reward rises but the work path stops depending on the problem

Evidence Check

Change the facts that should matter, keep the task wrapper fixed, and check whether the work notes, action choice, and intermediate claims change with those facts. Also check variety separately: a trace can look varied while still being a template. The formal term entropy means a variety score here; it can show that traces differ, but not that they used the right evidence.

Failure Boundary

The diagnostic can miss collapse if the test changes facts the model never used, or if the model copies shallow markers from the prompt while the real cause-and-effect reasoning has disappeared.

Connection

h1 tries to preserve learning signal across longer horizons; The Tell-Tale Norm turns internal state magnitude into a compute-control signal; ParetoPO asks whether tool use is worth its cost. All are versions of one question: when should a system spend reasoning, and how do we know that spending is still tied to the task?

EXEMPLAR 02

ATLAS

Evaluation As Adaptive Measurement

First-Principles Read

ATLAS starts from a simple fact: capability is not directly visible. The evaluator sees answers to tasks, and those answers are noisy shadows of the capability claim. A good benchmark is therefore closer to a measuring instrument than a school exam. It should spend questions where the answer will most reduce uncertainty about the hidden ability, the way a doctor orders tests that distinguish the remaining diagnoses instead of repeating tests whose result is already obvious.

Problem

A fixed benchmark wastes many items because easy questions do not distinguish strong models and irrelevant questions do not reduce uncertainty about the ability being claimed. It can also overstate rank differences when two models differ by less than the noise of the instrument.

State Space

The state is the evaluator's current guess about model ability, plus a bank of questions. Each question has a rough difficulty, a degree to which it separates nearby models, and noise. After each answer, some ability guesses become less believable and others become more believable.

Object

The object is a measuring device for a hidden skill. The skill itself is not directly visible; the evaluator only sees answers. A useful question is one whose answer would separate ability levels that are still plausible. If every plausible model gets the question right, the question adds little. If every plausible model gets it wrong, it also adds little. The formal phrase item-response model means the rule that connects the hidden ability to the answers we can observe.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the ability claim. A shorter adaptive test is acceptable only if it preserves the answer to the real question: which skill level, or which model ranking, is still believable after the observed answers?

Allowed Movement

The test may adaptively choose the next item based on current uncertainty after the answers seen so far. It may not choose items that maximize convenience while losing information about the target ability.

Why It Works

Adaptive testing works because not every question can change what we know. If a student clearly knows addition, another easy addition problem teaches the examiner little. If a problem is far beyond every plausible skill level, it also teaches little. The useful question sits near the current boundary, where one answer would point to a stronger skill level and another answer would point to a weaker one. The formal term Fisher information means how much a question reduces uncertainty by sharply separating the skill levels still in play.

choose the next item that best separates the ability levels still plausible after earlier answers

Evidence Check

Report uncertainty intervals after observing answers, item-information gain, agreement with full-test rankings, sensitivity to starting estimates, and cases where adaptive paths choose different items for different model regimes.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the item-response model is misspecified, when item banks omit important capability dimensions, or when models exploit item-selection patterns.

Connection

FRABench and UFEval factor scores and uncertainty; Micro-Benchmarking Reliability asks when ranks are statistically stable; CounselBench and WebDevJudge change the observation channel. All say that evaluation is measurement design before it is ranking.

EXEMPLAR 03

Rare Event Analysis And Tail Risks

Safety As Tail Probability

First-Principles Read

A factory can run smoothly most days and still be unsafe if one rare machine state can injure someone badly. Average performance hides that kind of problem. Rare-event work asks how often the system enters the dangerous corner and how severe the damage is there, without pretending that an ordinary sample saw enough just because it saw many harmless cases.

Problem

Average benchmark behavior can look safe while rare failures dominate deployment harm. Random sampling often misses the events users most need measured, especially when the harmful set is narrow, adversarially selected, or defined by a sequence of steps rather than one answer.

State Space

The state is the set of possible outputs, actions, or work paths, plus the subset that would cause harm. The dangerous object is how much chance lands in that subset, not how pleasant the ordinary cases look on average.

Object

The object is the chance of landing in the dangerous corner, not the average experience in the middle. If a system is safe on ordinary requests but sometimes causes severe harm, the important number lives in that small corner. If some bad cases are worse than others, the object also counts how much damage they cause. The formal phrase tail probability means the chance of those rare bad cases. Tail loss means the same count weighted by how much harm each bad case causes.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the deployment question. The evaluator may look for failures more aggressively, but the final number must still answer: how often would this happen when real users encounter the system naturally?

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may draw examples from a search process that visits rare regions more often, but it must keep a receipt for how that search process differs from deployment.

Why It Works

The method works because probability can be measured from a different search process if we keep a receipt for how that process was biased. Ordinary random testing may almost never enter the dangerous corner, so the evaluator deliberately visits that corner more often. The count is then corrected so it answers the deployment question rather than the stress-test question. The formal phrase importance sampling means looking where rare evidence is easier to see, then reweighting the count back to the real setting.

sample rare bad cases more often, then correct the count so it still estimates deployment risk

Evidence Check

State the event grammar, report confidence intervals, effective sample size, proposal sensitivity, boundary sensitivity, and examples near the event threshold.

Failure Boundary

The estimate fails if the harmful cases were defined too narrowly, if the stress search never visits an important failure type, or if the correction weights are so uneven that a few sampled cases control the whole estimate.

Connection

CSPO measures distance to violation; SandboxEscapeBench and Jailbreak Foundry apply adaptive pressure; FALCON and SRMC face the same sampling-support problem in molecular and generative settings.

EXEMPLAR 04

Midtraining Bridges

Training As Timed Distribution Movement

First-Principles Read

A lesson lands differently depending on when a student receives it. Before the basics, it is noise. After habits are fixed, it may be too late. In the middle, it can connect old knowledge to the next skill. Midtraining Bridges treats data this way: the same examples can help or harm depending on the model's current stage.

Problem

Specialized data can help at one training phase and do little or harm at another. Data value depends on where the model currently is, not only on the dataset's standalone quality. The real object is not a pile of examples but the update those examples cause at that moment.

State Space

The state is the model's current settings, the training rule's memory, the data shown so far, the bridge examples, and the later target. Each phase changes which model habits are still easy to adjust and which ones resist change. The formal term parameters means the model settings. Optimizer state means the training rule's memory of recent moves. A training distribution is the recipe for which examples are shown.

Object

The object is a training path, like a sequence of lessons given while the student is still able to absorb them. A data mixture is useful when it moves the model toward the target while preserving flexibility and avoiding damage to older skills. What must stay the same is transferable capability; what may change is when each kind of data appears and how strongly it pushes.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is reusable skill. The model should learn the specialized bridge material without losing the broader habits it will need for later tasks.

Allowed Movement

The training process may change data mixture weights over time, but the movement should remain in a phase where example pushes still produce useful updates instead of bouncing off a settled representation or overwriting old behavior.

Why It Works

A bridge works when its examples arrive while the model can still use them. Training is a sequence of small setting changes. A helpful bridge gives examples whose corrections make later target behavior easier while leaving needed old behavior intact. If the examples arrive too early, the model may not have the parts needed to use them. If they arrive too late, the same corrections may fight established habits. The formal phrase update alignment means the bridge examples push the model in a direction that helps the later target instead of working against it.

use bridge data at the training moment when its update helps the target and does not damage needed base behavior

Evidence Check

Compare early, middle, and late insertion; measure target gains, general-capability loss, whether example pushes agree with the target push, representation drift, and whether the same data helps under different training states.

Failure Boundary

The bridge fails if it arrives after plasticity has narrowed, if it over-specializes the representation, or if the target distribution was misidentified.

Connection

DiReCT chooses late data by how sharp or forgiving each update direction is; Sequential Data Values makes added data value order-dependent; BLL-Loss and OPUS select examples by optimizer effect. All treat data as movement, not fuel.

EXEMPLAR 05

Quotient-Space Diffusion

Generating Molecules After Ignoring Observer-Only Changes

First-Principles Read

A chair is the same chair whether the photo is taken from the left or the right. A molecule has the same issue: rotating the coordinate grid changes the numbers used to describe it, but not the molecule itself. Quotient-Space Diffusion first groups those duplicate descriptions, then learns changes that are chemical rather than camera-angle changes.

Problem

Molecular coordinates include facts about the observer's coordinate frame. Rotating or shifting the whole molecule changes the coordinate list but not molecular identity. A generator that learns from raw coordinates can waste effort treating those renamed copies as different chemical objects.

State Space

The state is a molecular configuration, but the meaningful space is not raw coordinates. It is the molecule after ignoring rotation, translation, and other observer-only changes, so many arrays map to the same physical object.

Object

The object is the same molecule represented by many coordinate arrays. The model should group those same-molecule descriptions together before learning what can chemically change.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the molecule itself and the distances between atoms that affect chemical behavior. Turning the coordinate grid should not make the molecule seem more or less likely, change the cleanup direction, or alter the generated structure.

Allowed Movement

The sampler may move through noisy molecular states, but movement must respect same-molecule descriptions and should not learn artifacts of arbitrary orientation.

Why It Works

The method works because it separates two kinds of change: changes that only rename the same object, and changes that alter the object. Grouping same-object descriptions forces the model to ignore the first kind and spend learning effort on the second. The universe lets this work because rigid transformations preserve distances between atoms and chemical relationships; they change the observer's coordinates, not the molecule.

learn over molecule identity after grouping coordinate descriptions that differ only by observer frame

Evidence Check

Rotate or shift the same molecule and check that the result does not change for the wrong reason. Also test molecular validity, energy or property preservation, sample variety, and whether the repeated cleanup path stays consistent across same-molecule coordinate frames. The formal term invariance means the answer stays the same when only the observer's description changed.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails if the quotient removes information that actually matters, or if downstream tasks need orientation relative to a binding pocket, surface, or external field.

Connection

Protein hierarchy papers preserve residue and fold constraints; TideGS and RadioGS preserve physical fields; HDFlow and PACT preserve action feasibility. All start by choosing the native space where validity is defined.

EXEMPLAR 06

Alignment-Sensitive Minimax Rates

Generalization Through Visible Signal Placement

First-Principles Read

A camera can have many pixels and still miss a faint object in fog. More room to store patterns does not mean the evidence can reveal the right pattern. Alignment-Sensitive Minimax Rates asks whether the target pattern sits where the available data can actually see it, or whether it is buried in directions that look like noise.

Problem

Two model descriptions can have similar size while one learns well and the other does not. The difference is where the task-relevant pattern sits. If the pattern sits in a place the data sees clearly, learning is possible with fewer examples. If it sits where the data is weak and noisy, even a large model may not recover it.

State Space

The state is the model's internal description of examples, split into easier-to-see and harder-to-see directions. A direction is a way the data can vary, like height, color, wording, or a hidden mix of many features. The formal phrases representation space and kernel space mean the coordinate system the model uses to describe those variations.

Object

Imagine trying to hear a melody in a noisy room. A repeated clear note can be found; a faint note drowned by chatter cannot. The object is whether the task pattern lives in the clear part of the evidence or in the buried part.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the useful task pattern being visible above noise. A learned internal description is helpful only if it places the target in parts that finite examples can estimate.

Allowed Movement

Learning may reshape the internal description, but useful movement puts more of the target in clearly measured directions instead of scattering it through weak noisy ones.

Why It Works

The argument works because finite evidence has resolution limits. In a noisy room, a repeated beat can be heard because it keeps lining up; a faint one-off sound disappears into the background. Repeated data has the same rule. It reveals directions that appear often and strongly; it cannot reliably estimate every faint direction in a large space. If the target mostly lives in the visible span, learning has enough evidence to recover it. If the target lives outside that span, having a model that could represent many things in principle does not solve the evidence problem. The formal term expressivity means what the model could represent if the evidence were enough to select the right pattern.

risk is controlled by how much target signal sits in directions visible above noise

Evidence Check

Measure how concentrated the target signal is in visible directions, sample-size scaling, perturbation sensitivity, and pairs of representations with similar complexity but different placements of the target signal relative to the directions the data can estimate.

Failure Boundary

The theory can mislead if the visible-direction structure changes during training, if the noise model is wrong, or if downstream tasks rely on nonlinear structure not captured by the ordered basis.

Connection

Single-Head Attention studies signal recovery in attention; BBP transitions ask when spikes separate from noise; LoRA theory asks whether task movement lives in a low-rank subspace. All are signal-visibility arguments.

EXEMPLAR 07

Distributional Equivalence

Causal Discovery As Saying Which Stories Are Still Possible

First-Principles Read

Rain and traffic can both make a commute longer. If all we have is a table of days, commute times, and weather, several cause stories may still fit: rain caused traffic, traffic coincided with rain, or an unseen event caused both. Distributional Equivalence is useful because it keeps every story the evidence has not ruled out instead of pretending one diagram is proven.

Problem

The same table of observations can fit several cause stories, especially when some causes are unseen or effects can feed back into causes. Returning one diagram can make the evidence sound stronger than it is.

State Space

The state is the set of cause diagrams and unseen cause stories that would all produce the observed patterns. The formal phrase family of causal graphs with hidden structure means the list of still-possible cause maps after allowing for causes the dataset did not observe.

Object

The object is the whole set of causal stories that still fit the observations and assumptions. If the data cannot separate those stories, the method should keep them together instead of picking one.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay clear is the line between what the evidence has ruled out and what is merely one possible story. The formal phrase identified structure means the part every still-possible story agrees on. Still-plausible structure means the part the evidence has not separated.

Allowed Movement

The algorithm may remove stories that violate measured patterns, but it cannot choose among stories the data still cannot tell apart unless it adds new assumptions.

Why It Works

It works because a cause claim is only as strong as the rival stories it defeats. If every still-possible story says the same thing about a cause, the claim can be trusted without knowing the exact hidden diagram. If two still-possible stories disagree, the method must say the evidence does not decide. The formal term identified means every allowed story that fits the observations gives the same answer to the cause question.

a causal claim is supported only when every still-plausible cause story gives the same answer

Evidence Check

Report how many causal stories remain possible, rank-constraint calibration, hidden-variable stress tests, false edge and missing edge rates, and examples where the method correctly refuses to identify a unique graph.

Failure Boundary

The output is only as good as the promises made about which cause stories are allowed and the tests used to decide whether a pattern is really present.

Connection

Local Covariate Selection finds the background facts needed for one fair effect comparison; DiCoLa checks when relationships disappear after the right facts are held fixed; CausalGame chooses interventions to separate cause stories. All protect the same boundary: what the evidence distinguishes from what a model can narrate.

EXEMPLAR 08

Steer Like The LLM

Prompting As An Internal Push

First-Principles Read

A prompt works by changing the model before it answers. The words are not magic; they are a handle that moves internal numbers into a different pattern. Steer Like The LLM asks whether we can learn that internal move rather than keep repeating the words. If the same internal move causes the same behavior across many inputs, then control can happen inside the model state instead of through fragile prompt phrasing.

Problem

Prompt words are an indirect control interface. They may steer behavior, but they also consume context, interact with unrelated instructions, and make the control signal hard to isolate.

State Space

The state is the model's internal numbers as they change while reading and answering, plus the odds it assigns to possible next words. The formal phrase hidden activations means those internal numbers. A trajectory is their path over time. An output distribution is the final menu of next-word odds.

Object

Imagine a note on a desk that makes a worker switch into "be concise" mode. The paper note is not the real behavior; the changed working mode is. The object is the internal change caused by an instruction: how the model's hidden state differs when the prompt is present versus absent.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the intended behavior change caused by the prompt, separated from accidental wording, formatting, or examples that happened to come with that prompt.

Allowed Movement

The method may move internal states in the learned direction that causes the desired behavior, but it should not disturb unrelated internal facts or make all non-target outputs collapse into one narrow pattern.

Why It Works

It works when the prompt has a repeatable physical effect inside the network. If adding an instruction usually moves the hidden state in a similar direction, then a small learned controller can reproduce that movement. The key is separation: keep the intended behavior change, drop the accidental wording, and avoid moving unrelated features that the original prompt happened to touch.

learn the internal change caused by the prompt, then apply only the part that causes the target behavior

Evidence Check

Compare word-prompt control and internal-state control on the same tasks, remove candidate layers, test off-target behavior, measure how the spread of possible answers changes, and check whether the same steering move transfers across inputs.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails if the prompt's internal effect only works in one context, changes differently across layers, or is tangled with facts that should not move.

Connection

The Tell-Tale Norm also turns hidden-state measurements into control. MINT, Causal Route Gating, and hidden-route oracle work change internal routes to ask which one actually causes the output.

EXEMPLAR 09

RLVepsR

Verifier Labels As Imperfect Pushes On Behavior

First-Principles Read

A checker is a teacher only if its mistakes do not point the learner in the wrong direction. Random noise can sometimes average out. A repeated blind spot does not average out; training follows it. RLVepsR matters because it treats checker feedback as a push on behavior, not just a label. The real question is whether that push moves the model toward true success or toward whatever fools the checker.

Problem

Learning from checker feedback can improve a model only if checker labels usually agree with the real target. Weak checkers can produce learning, no learning, or anti-learning depending on which mistakes they make.

State Space

The state is the model's current answer habits, the kinds of outputs it tends to produce, and the checker that turns each output into a noisy pass/fail label or reward. The formal phrase policy distribution means the model's menu of likely behaviors. A verifier observation channel is the imperfect path from real quality to checker label.

Object

Imagine a trainee whose supervisor sometimes rewards the wrong habit. The question is not only whether the supervisor gives feedback; it is whether that feedback pushes the trainee toward real skill. The object is whether the checker's reward moves model behavior in the same direction true success would.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay preserved is the link between getting rewarded and getting genuinely better. The checker may be cheap or weak, but its reward must point the model roughly toward real correctness.

Allowed Movement

The model may move toward higher checker reward only while that reward remains a useful stand-in for the target. When false positives dominate, movement should stop or defer to a stronger check.

Why It Works

It works when checker mistakes are scattered around the right answer rather than tilted toward one bad shortcut. Scattered mistakes can wash out across many examples. A repeated blind spot becomes a lesson: the model learns to produce exactly the kind of output the checker wrongly approves. The formal phrase reinforcement learning means training by rewarding actions, and it helps only when reward usually points toward the behavior we actually want.

useful reward training needs the checker push to agree with the true-success push

Evidence Check

Sweep verifier false-positive and false-negative rates, measure learn/stall/anti-learn regimes, audit disagreement cases, and compare weak-verifier updates to stronger oracle or human judgments.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when checker errors are systematic in a direction the model can exploit, or when the checker watches the wrong artifact.

Connection

This is the training version of benchmark validity. It connects JudgeBench, WebDevJudge, CyberGym, formal proof checking, and checker-gaming chains: training inherits the measuring tool's errors.

EXEMPLAR 10

DiReCT

Data Choice As Avoiding Fragile Update Directions

First-Principles Read

A training example is not just good or bad by itself. It pushes the model. Near the end of training, some directions are like flat ground: the model can move there without breaking much. Other directions are like tight wires: small movement can damage behavior that has already been learned. DiReCT chooses data by asking where each example would push the model, not by judging the example in isolation.

Problem

Late-training examples can improve held-out behavior or damage fragile parts of the model. Data quality alone is not enough; where the example pushes the current model decides whether the update is safe.

State Space

The state is the model near the end of training, the push from each candidate example, and the validation directions where small movement causes large loss changes.

Object

Imagine adjusting a machine with many knobs. Some knobs have slack: a small turn improves one thing without breaking much else. Other knobs are hair-trigger: a tiny turn ruins a behavior that already worked. The object is the move each training example would cause, split into forgiving moves and fragile moves.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is behavior that is already fragile but important. The model can move, but it should avoid moves where a small change causes a large validation loss increase. The formal term curvature means this local sensitivity: a small move in one direction changes loss much more than the same-sized move elsewhere.

Allowed Movement

Select examples whose pushes agree with useful forgiving directions and stay small in fragile harmful directions.

Why It Works

It works because the same-sized update can have very different consequences depending on direction. A move along a flexible direction can add capability. A move across a sensitive direction can undo a lot. Data selection becomes safer when it chooses examples whose push is useful and avoids examples whose push cuts across fragile behavior.

prefer examples that push useful forgiving directions and avoid fragile directions where validation loss rises sharply

Evidence Check

Measure how much each candidate push enters fragile directions, how held-out behavior moves, selected-versus-random data, timing sensitivity, and ablations that use only push size without checking local fragility.

Failure Boundary

The method fails if the fragile-direction estimate is noisy, if validation sensitivity misses deployment failures, or if forgiving directions encode shortcuts.

Connection

Midtraining Bridges studies when data should arrive; Sequential Data Values studies order-dependent value; LoRA theory studies constrained parameter movement. DiReCT gives the local movement-shape version of the same idea.

EXEMPLAR 11

ReaSyn And MORetro*

Molecules As Route-Constrained Objects

First-Principles Read

A molecule on a screen is not enough. In drug design, a molecule also has to be reachable by reactions, made from available pieces, and compatible with the target context. ReaSyn and MORetro* matter because they stop treating synthesis as an afterthought. They build the route into the object being optimized, so the model searches among candidates that can plausibly exist in a lab.

Problem

A generated molecule can have attractive predicted properties while being hard or impossible to synthesize. The valid object is not only a graph; it is a graph with a route.

State Space

The state includes the molecule graph, available building pieces, reaction steps, backward synthesis plan, 3D shape, target context, and property limits.

Object

The object is a molecule paired with a route for making it. A recipe that requires an ingredient no one can obtain is not a usable recipe; a molecule with no plausible reaction route has the same problem. A candidate is valid only if the molecule and the route satisfy the chemistry rules together. The formal phrase feasible set of molecule-route pairs means the collection of candidates that both look like the desired molecule and can plausibly be made.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay preserved is practical chemical reachability while properties improve. Searching for better properties should not move the molecule outside the set reachable by plausible synthesis.

Allowed Movement

The generator may change the structure, route, or building pieces, but every move must keep reaction rules and target rules consistent.

Why It Works

It works because impossible candidates are removed before they can dominate the search. A property model may love a molecule that no reaction path can produce. If route feasibility is inside the state, the search rule must improve the desired property while staying on a makeable route. The search becomes smaller, harder to cheat, and closer to the real scientific task.

improve the desired molecule property only among candidates with a plausible reaction route and target fit

Evidence Check

Report route validity, building-block availability, reaction-template success, property-route tradeoffs, novelty, and whether optimizing property increases infeasible candidates.

Failure Boundary

The method fails if route templates are incomplete, if predicted reactions do not hold experimentally, or if route feasibility conflicts with biological target constraints.

Connection

Quotient-Space Diffusion handles when two coordinate descriptions name the same molecule; DCFold and protein hierarchy papers handle folded structure; HDFlow and PACT handle physically possible action. All make validity part of the state before search begins.

EXEMPLAR 12

Error Propagation In Quantized Diffusion Models

Compression As Small Rounding Mistakes That Build Up

First-Principles Read

Diffusion generation is a long chain of corrections. A tiny rounding error at one step can be harmless, or it can be carried forward and magnified by later steps. Quantized diffusion papers matter because they treat compression as a path problem. The question is not "was this one approximation close?" but "after many repeated approximations, did the sample travel through the same kind of output cloud?"

Problem

Using fewer number levels can look accurate at one cleanup step but drift across the full sample-making path. The final image or sample inherits accumulated numerical error.

State Space

The state is the sample-making path: the noisy sample, which step it is on, the model's cleanup prediction, the rounded version of that prediction, and the next sample that will be fed into the following step. The formal phrase sampler trajectory means this step-by-step path from noisy start to finished sample.

Object

The object is how rounding mistakes travel through a repeated process. Each step takes the current sample, changes it, and hands it to the next step. A small early mistake can matter more than the same mistake near the end because the early mistake is reused by many later steps. The formal phrase iterative dynamical system means a process where each next state is made from the current state again and again.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the cloud of final samples reached after the whole path, not only one local cleanup prediction. Quantization should preserve both the step-level cleanup and the kind of images, molecules, or outputs reached after many rounded steps.

Allowed Movement

The method may round weights or internal numbers, but the rounding must know where it happens in the path. An early error and a late error do not have the same effect, because an early error can be reused and amplified by many later steps.

Why It Works

It works because the sample-making procedure is not a collection of isolated predictions. Each state becomes the input to the next state. A steering error near the start of a road trip can put the car on the wrong highway; the same small correction near the driveway may barely matter. Diffusion steps behave like that. If a step is sensitive, an error there changes everything that follows. A good low-bit method spends precision where later steps depend on it most and saves precision where the path is naturally stable. The formal term Jacobian means a local sensitivity map for how a small mistake at one step is carried into the next step.

final error depends on how each step carries forward earlier error plus the new rounding error added at that step

Evidence Check

Measure which timesteps are fragile, how final samples degrade, whether the generated outputs move away from the intended output pattern, long-step stability, calibration data effects, and comparisons against one-step quantization metrics.

Failure Boundary

The model fails if the simple small-error estimate misses large step-by-step behavior changes, or if rounding removes rare output types that ordinary image scores barely count.

Connection

LiftQuant, TetraJet, FOCUS/RePAIR, and finite-precision theory all say compression is behavioral: numerical changes can alter the function, path, or reasoning loop being preserved.

EXEMPLAR 13

VISUALSWAP And MINT

Multimodal Answers As Depending On The Right Evidence

First-Principles Read

A correct answer is not proof of visual understanding. The model may have guessed from the question wording or common text patterns. VISUALSWAP and MINT ask the direct question: if the visual evidence had been different, would the answer have changed? Grounding means the answer depends on the right evidence channel, not merely that an image was present.

Problem

A multimodal model can answer from language priors while appearing visually grounded. The final answer does not prove which modality caused it.

State Space

The state includes image evidence, text evidence, the internal place where those sources are combined, routes through the model, and the spread of possible final answers.

Object

Imagine asking, "Is the light red?" while showing a traffic-light photo. If changing the photo from red to green does not change the answer, the model was answering from habit, not from the picture. The object is the dependency between the answer and the evidence source that is supposed to decide it.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is evidence control. The answer should change when the relevant visual fact changes and stay stable when only irrelevant background changes. The formal term grounding means the answer is controlled by the right evidence channel.

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may swap visual evidence, change an internal route, or patch memory, but the change must isolate the source being tested instead of changing several things at once.

Why It Works

It works because cause is tested by controlled change. If swapping the relevant visual fact leaves the answer unchanged, the image was probably not carrying the decision. If changing irrelevant background changes the answer, the route is fragile. The method separates evidence that should control the answer from decoration that should not.

a grounded answer changes with relevant visual evidence and stays stable when irrelevant background changes

Evidence Check

Run visual swaps, route ablations, memory patching, attribution over time, text-prior controls, and cases where visual and language priors disagree.

Failure Boundary

The method fails if swaps introduce artifacts, if the intervention changes multiple channels at once, or if attribution is measured at a layer that is not causally upstream of the answer.

Connection

MoCA separates seeing failure from reasoning failure; Causal Route Gating suppresses routes where text habit overrides the image; DLMR tracks whether a memory came from observation or later summary; PRISM and Mind-Omni translate noisy brain signals into usable concepts. All ask which evidence channel did the work.

EXEMPLAR 14

Skill-Pro

Agent Experience As Reusable Procedure

First-Principles Read

A useful skill is not a memory of text. It is a reusable way to change the world under the right conditions. Skill-Pro matters because it compresses a successful episode into a procedure with a start condition, actions, and a stop condition. That is what makes reuse possible: the agent knows when the old solution applies, what to do, and when to stop doing it.

Problem

An agent that solves a task once often wastes future compute rediscovering the same workflow. But blindly replaying a past run is unsafe because a workflow is valid only when the same needed conditions are present.

State Space

The state is the user goal, what the environment currently shows, tool calls, file or world changes, checker outputs, and the condition that proves the job is done. The formal phrase agent trajectory means the watched path through states and actions. Verifier outputs are checker results. A termination condition is the rule that says the job is done.

Object

The object is a saved procedure that knows when to start, what steps to take, and when to stop. A useful checklist is not a diary of everything that happened last time; it keeps the trigger, the necessary steps, and the condition that says the job is done. The formal phrase executable skill means a reusable action rule with activation conditions, internal steps, and a stopping rule.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay preserved is procedural correctness. Compressing an episode into a skill should keep the dependency chain that made the original run succeed.

Allowed Movement

The system may abstract away incidental details of one episode, but it must keep the variables that decide whether the skill applies and when it should stop.

Why It Works

It works because reuse is valid when the repeated part of the world is real. If the same start conditions lead to the same useful action pattern, the agent can stop searching from scratch and call the saved procedure. The saved procedure protects three things: when to activate, which state changes matter during execution, and what observation proves completion.

a reusable skill says when to start, what action rule to follow, and what observation means stop

Evidence Check

Test skill reuse on shifted tasks, precondition failures, stale environment state, wrong termination, and comparison with replaying raw trajectories or solving from scratch.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the start rule omits a hidden condition, when the environment changes its behavior, or when the saved skill drops a dependency needed for correctness.

Connection

CyberGym and WebDevJudge check artifacts that must run; ThinKV keeps context needed later; h1 composes short dependencies into longer reasoning paths. Skill-Pro is the agent-memory version of keeping enough state for the next decision.

EXEMPLAR 15

ParetoPO

Tool Use As Spending For A Better Answer

Problem

Imagine hiring an assistant who can answer from memory, open a calculator, search records, or ask another expert. More checking can improve the answer, but each check takes time, costs money, and can introduce a new mistake. A plain accuracy score hides whether the assistant solved the task wisely or just spent heavily.

State Space

The state is everything the agent has already done and paid for: its private thinking, each tool call, what the tools returned, the final answer, the time spent waiting, the money spent calling tools, and the extra risk from involving those tools. The cost is part of the work record because a right answer reached by ten needless outside calls is not the same product as a right answer reached cleanly.

Object

The object is the menu of choices that are not clearly wasteful. One agent may be cheap and usually right; another may be slower and slightly more accurate. A choice is wasteful if another choice gives at least the same correctness for less cost, or gives better correctness for the same cost. What must stay visible is the exchange itself: how much extra correctness was bought, and what was paid to buy it.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful task performance after the cost is counted. What may change is how often the agent uses tools and how much extra accuracy it buys with them.

Allowed Movement

The agent may spend more calls to get harder answers right, or spend fewer calls when memory is enough. The important rule is that the tradeoff is shown before anyone hides it inside one combined score.

Why It Works

This works because "better" has more than one side. A hospital, a classroom, and a phone app may choose different agents because delay, money, privacy exposure, and mistakes have different prices in each setting. If one agent is slower, costlier, and no more correct, there is no reason to keep it in the serious menu. If another agent is more correct but also more expensive, the choice depends on the setting. The method works by separating those two cases. It removes plainly wasteful choices first, then leaves the real decisions visible: to get more correctness, you must knowingly give up time, money, simplicity, or risk. The formal term is Pareto frontier: the visible menu of non-wasteful choices where improving one goal requires paying with another.

keep an agent only when no alternative is both at least as correct and no more expensive for the same task

Evidence Check

Show the cost-versus-correctness curve, per-task tool budgets, the value of one more call, and examples where fewer tool calls give the same right answer. Include the cost of checking the tools, not only the tool price.

Failure Boundary

The curve misleads if it leaves out human review time, security exposure, bad tool results, or the damage caused when the agent confidently uses the wrong tool.

Connection

VALUEFLOW and SafeDPO ask how usefulness trades against safety. LiftQuant and TetraJet ask how accuracy trades against speed and machine limits. ParetoPO applies the same plain idea to agents: better means better for a visible price.

EXEMPLAR 16

WebDevJudge, PaperBench, And VERINA

Evaluation As Checking The Thing Itself

Problem

A builder can write a convincing note saying a website works, while the real page has broken buttons. A paper can summarize a result smoothly while the cited evidence does not support it. A proof can sound plausible while a proof checker rejects it. Text about an artifact is not the artifact.

State Space

The state is the real object and the trail behind it: files, tests, browser behavior, claims, citations, proof steps, dependencies, execution logs, and the exact rule used to decide whether the object passed. The evaluator has to keep both the object and the trail because many failures live in the connection between them: a claim points to the wrong citation, a button calls the wrong route, or a proof step uses a rule it did not earn.

Object

The object is a claim tied to evidence that can be inspected. The claim says what should be true; the evidence is the website, paper, code, proof, or run log that must make it true. The important link is not "the text sounds right," but "the thing being judged can be checked where it can actually fail." What must stay the same is that link from claim to supporting artifact, even if the checker splits the artifact into many smaller checks.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is whether the actual artifact works. What may change is how the evaluator breaks the artifact into smaller checks.

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may split a website into links, forms, layouts, and tests, or split a paper into claims and citations. It cannot drop the links between those pieces, because many failures happen only when pieces are used together.

Why It Works

This works because correctness lives in different places for different artifacts. A web app must be opened and used because layout, clicks, loading, and state changes can fail even when the description is perfect. Code must run because real inputs expose mistakes prose can hide. A proof must satisfy its checker because one invalid step breaks the whole chain. A paper claim must lead back to evidence because summaries can skip the weak spot. The evaluator is useful only when it looks at the place where the claim could actually break. What must stay the same is the claim's support in the artifact itself; what may change is the checker used to inspect the artifact. The formal term is verifier relation: a rule that accepts a claim only when the right supporting object, checked in the right medium, supports it.

accept a claim only when the checker can follow the needed path through the artifact and its evidence

Evidence Check

Report false passes, false failures, execution logs, broken dependencies, human disagreement, and cases where a text-only judge approves something the artifact-level check rejects.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the checker sees only an easy stand-in, skips interactions among parts, or rewards artifacts that are written to fool the checker instead of work for users.

Connection

CyberGym makes security tasks executable. PaperBench and RefineStat tie scientific claims to checkable work. The shared lesson is simple: judge the object that can fail, not the explanation around it.

EXEMPLAR 17

ThinKV, STAR-KV, And EntroKV

Context Compression As Keeping The Needed Notes

Problem

Imagine taking notes during a long meeting. You cannot write down every word, but if you drop the one decision made early in the meeting, your later summary may be wrong. Long-context models face the same problem when memory is expensive.

State Space

The state is the conversation so far, the model's stored memory entries, which parts later steps can still look at, which facts are repeated, which facts are rare, and the future questions the model may need to answer. A memory entry is valuable only through a later use: it matters if removing it would change a future answer, action, or check.

Object

The object is a shorter memory that still carries the facts needed later. It is not a pleasant summary for a reader. It is more like the notes a person keeps on the desk while solving the next part of the problem: if a note can change a later answer, dropping it changes the work.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the answer the model would give when the dropped text matters. What may change is the exact wording, order, or storage form of repeated and unused details.

Allowed Movement

The system may remove, merge, route, or store entries with fewer bits. It should do that based on whether an entry can change future behavior, not only whether it looks important right now. Repetition can be merged when one copy carries the same future use; a rare clue must stay when it is the only bridge to a later answer.

Why It Works

This works when the shortened memory leaves the model in almost the same position as the full history for the tasks that matter. Repeated filler can be dropped if it would not change any later decision. A quiet fact must be kept if a later question depends on it, even when that fact looks unimportant at the moment it appears. The hard part is that importance is about future use, not current attention. The test is not whether the memory looks compact; it is whether future behavior stays the same when the full history is replaced by the shorter record. The formal terms are sufficient state or sufficient statistic: a shorter record that still supports the same important future decisions.

a good cache lets future answers stay close to full-history answers on the tasks that depend on the past

Evidence Check

Compare with full context on delayed facts, rare clues, multi-step references, distracting text, long video memory, and cases where early tokens matter only near the end.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when it keeps only loud or recent text, drops rare evidence, or is tested on future tasks that do not match the tasks it was tuned for.

Connection

Skill-Pro compresses agent experience into reusable procedures. VideoKR keeps the video evidence needed later. Mamba Markov ICL asks when a running memory is enough. They all ask the same question: what can be forgotten without changing the next decision?

EXEMPLAR 18

CSPO, SafeDPO, And SecFid

Safety As Staying Inside A Guardrail

Problem

A driver should not first speed toward a cliff and then rely on a last-second fine to stop. Some safety training has that shape: make the model more helpful, then punish unsafe answers afterward. That can still let learning pass through places it should avoid.

State Space

The state is how likely the model is to give each kind of answer, which answers are known to be safe or unsafe, which cases sit near the line, how much useful detail the answer gives, and how close a training update would move the model toward a violation. The dangerous part is not only the final unsafe answer; it is also a training step that quietly makes that answer easier to produce next time.

Object

The object is the set of changes the model is allowed to make. The model may become more useful, more direct, or less overcautious, but the path of change must stay inside the safety region instead of crossing into unsafe behavior and hoping a later penalty pulls it back.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is respect for the safety rule, especially near hard cases. What may change is wording, helpful detail, refusal behavior, and ranking among safe answers.

Allowed Movement

Training may make safe answers more likely and unnecessary refusals less likely. It may not buy usefulness by making unsafe acceptance more likely or by ignoring uncertain boundary cases.

Why It Works

This works because it controls the move before the model takes it. A penalty says, "that bad result was expensive after it happened." A guardrail says, "do not choose an update that makes that bad result too likely." This matters most near hard boundary cases, where a small push toward helpfulness can also make unsafe acceptance more likely. The method has to ask two questions at once: did usefulness improve, and did the update stay inside the allowed region? What must stay the same is respect for the safety boundary; what may change is helpfulness inside that boundary. The formal term is constrained optimization: choosing the best useful move only from moves that satisfy the safety condition.

make the answer more useful only through updates whose measured safety risk remains inside the allowed limit

Evidence Check

Audit both sides of the line: unsafe acceptance, needless refusal, adaptive prompts near the boundary, subgroup behavior, and how much usefulness is lost as the safety rule gets stricter.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the safety rule measures the wrong thing, sees only easy examples, or changes once adversarial users start pressing on it.

Connection

PACT constrains robot action. Copyright-Bench constrains legal agent behavior. VALUEFLOW shows value-safety tradeoffs. These all replace "maximize one score" with "improve only through allowed moves."

EXEMPLAR 19

LiftQuant, TetraJet, And QAT Scaling

Low-Bit Deployment As Using A Smaller Measuring Cup

Problem

Imagine replacing a precise kitchen scale with one that rounds to the nearest large mark. For many recipes nothing changes, but a delicate recipe can fail. Low-bit models are similar: they save memory and run faster, but rounding can change reasoning, repetition, confidence, or behavior on specific hardware.

State Space

The state is the real deployed computation: stored weights, intermediate numbers created while answering, rounding rules, scale factors, fused operations, memory layout, and the exact arithmetic supported by the serving hardware. A test that uses pretend arithmetic is weak evidence, because users will experience the arithmetic that the deployed machine actually runs.

Object

The object is the recipe that turns the original model into the smaller-number model. It says which numbers get rounded, which numbers get rescaled first, which operations are merged, and whether the model is trained while already seeing the smaller measuring cup it will use in production.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the behavior users rely on: decisions, confidence, long-output stability, and speed targets. What may change is the exact internal number used to get there.

Allowed Movement

The system may round numbers, change scales, fuse operations, or train with low precision. Those changes must match the arithmetic that will actually run at serving time. A shortcut is allowed only when the smaller number grid still preserves the decisions, confidence, and long-output behavior people depend on.

Why It Works

This works when the lost numeric detail is detail the task did not need. Some internal numbers are like rough ingredient amounts: a little rounding does not change the recipe. Others are like a medicine dose: a small change can matter. Scaling tries to place the important numbers where the smaller numeric grid can still express them. Training with low precision lets the model learn around the rounding it will face later, instead of being surprised by it only after deployment. The formal term is quantization: approximating a model with fewer numeric levels while trying to keep the behavior users rely on.

the deployed low-bit model should behave like the full model on the real inputs it will serve

Evidence Check

Report real hardware traces, precision comparisons, long-output behavior, confidence drift, latency-memory curves, and tasks where small numeric errors grow into large behavior changes.

Failure Boundary

The guarantee fails when the test uses fake rounding that differs from real kernels, when rare tokens or long sequences magnify errors, or when average accuracy hides drift in important cases.

Connection

Quantized diffusion studies how small rounding errors build along a generation path. FOCUS and RePAIR study behavior changes after pruning. TileLang makes machine layout explicit. The shared rule is that deployment math is the math the machine actually runs.

EXEMPLAR 20

Safety-Guided Flow And DMPO

Reward And Safety As Steering A Crowd

Problem

Imagine guiding a crowd through a city. You want people to avoid unsafe streets and reach better places, but if you push everyone through the same narrow road, you lose many valid routes. Guided generation has the same risk: stronger reward or safety pressure can improve samples while erasing diversity or chasing a flawed score.

State Space

The state is the whole route from rough possibility to finished output: the starting noise or base model, the partly formed outputs along the way, the score that says "more like this" or "avoid this," the strength of each nudge, and the final image, molecule, plan, or text.

Object

The object is not one chosen answer. It is the whole crowd of answers that could still happen while the generator is working. Picture many draft posters on a table. A judge walks by after each round and points the next round toward safer or better posters. The point is to improve the crowd without making every poster the same.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the generator's ability to reach the valid kinds of output the task still needs, including rare but legitimate cases. What may change is how much mass moves away from unsafe, low-quality, or off-task outputs and toward safer or better ones.

Allowed Movement

Guidance may bend the route toward safer or more useful regions. It should not erase rare valid outputs, reward a cheap trick that only flatters the score, or ask the generator to make a jump its own step-by-step process cannot make. The formal term for that step-by-step making procedure is sampler.

Why It Works

This works when each small nudge changes the odds without destroying the menu. A useful reward score is like a good reviewer: posters it likes are usually better for the real task, not merely better at pleasing the reviewer. A useful safety score blocks dangerous routes while leaving the safe variety intact. The method works by steering during formation, when many futures are still possible, instead of waiting until the end and throwing away bad finished outputs. The formal term is distribution transport: moving the whole crowd of possible outputs toward better regions while keeping the important valid regions reachable.

guided steps should move likely outputs toward the target while keeping valid variety reachable

Evidence Check

Show whether variety remains as guidance gets stronger, whether rare valid outputs survive, whether high-reward examples are truly better under an independent check, whether safety boundary cases are handled without overblocking, and whether the step-by-step route changes in the intended direction rather than only the final score.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the reward is only a stand-in for the true goal, when guidance collapses the crowd into one bland pattern, when important minority cases disappear, or when the scoring rule rewards artifacts that look safe to the checker but fail in real use.

Connection

RLVepsR studies what happens when reward labels become training pressure. Rare Event Analysis asks whether rare cases are still produced. Quantized diffusion studies path error. Safety-Guided Flow and DMPO put the steering question inside the step-by-step generator itself.

EXEMPLAR 21

IRNO And RealPDEBench

Physical Surrogates As Stable Step-By-Step Predictors

Problem

A weather shortcut can be close for the next hour and badly wrong after a day if each small error feeds the next prediction. Cheap replacement physics models face that problem. One good step is not enough if repeated steps create impossible waves, leaks, or boundary failures. The formal term surrogate means a cheaper model that stands in for a slower simulator; it is useful only if the repeated shortcut still respects the physical rule.

State Space

The state is the physical situation being predicted: temperature, pressure, water height, airflow, or another field spread over space; the grid used to store it; the boundary rules at walls or edges; the mismatch left after a prediction; the correction tried next; and the full repeated rollout over time.

Object

The object is a cheaper rule for taking the physical world one step forward. Think of replacing a slow flood simulator with a fast sketch. The sketch is useful only if each next sketch still behaves like water: it cannot leak through a sealed wall, invent extra mass, or make waves grow for no physical reason.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the physical relationship that makes the next moment follow from the current moment. What may change is the cheaper storage, grid size, numerical shortcut, or correction step used to compute it.

Allowed Movement

The model may look at its own leftover mismatch and correct it again and again. Each correction should clean up the important error or keep it bounded. It should not add tiny artificial ripples that later steps reuse until the rollout becomes physically impossible.

Why It Works

This works only when repeated correction is stable. A one-hour weather shortcut can look accurate even if it slowly adds heat from nowhere; after many hours, that hidden mistake dominates. IRNO-style correction asks what is still wrong after a predicted step and pushes the field back toward the physical rule. RealPDEBench matters because it tests the repeated use case, not only the easy one-step case. The formal names describe plain pieces of that loop: a learned operator is the rule that transforms a whole field, a fixed point is a state that remains consistent when the rule is applied, and a residual is the leftover mismatch between the prediction and the physical rule.

after each predicted step, the shortcut rule should keep both the next state and the leftover physics mismatch small over the full rollout

Evidence Check

Measure full-rollout error, not only next-step error. Check whether noise grows, whether conservation rules hold, whether new grid sizes and boundary conditions still work, whether sharp local events survive, and whether the benchmark includes the physical situations where the cheap replacement model will actually be used.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when one-step tests hide errors that compound later, when the correction can only fix smooth easy mistakes, when boundary rules are learned as surface patterns, or when the benchmark avoids the hard regimes that break real simulators.

Connection

RealPDEBench evaluates whether physical structure survives. Long video work studies whether the same world stays coherent over time. Diffusion transport papers control path error. The shared rule is that local mistakes matter because they accumulate.

EXEMPLAR 22

Local Covariate Selection And TRECA

Local Effects As Comparing Like With Like

Problem

Suppose you want to know whether a tutoring program helped one kind of student. Comparing every tutored student with every untutored student may be unfair if the groups differ in preparation, school, or family support. But adding every possible variable can also wash out the very effect you wanted to measure.

State Space

The state is the comparison being made: who received the treatment, what outcome was measured, which background facts might explain both, which local group the question is about, which cases can honestly be compared, and what unseen factor could still tilt the result.

Object

The object is the smallest useful fairness checklist for this one question. If the question is whether tutoring helped ninth graders who were already struggling in algebra, the checklist may need prior math level, school, attendance, and support at home. It does not need every fact about a student. It needs the facts that stop the tutoring label from secretly meaning "different kind of student."

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the change being studied: what would happen if this treatment were applied to this local group. What may change is which background facts are included, excluded, or weighted. The formal term intervention means the deliberate change whose effect is being compared.

Allowed Movement

The method may choose background facts that make treated and untreated cases look comparable before treatment. It should not match on facts caused by the treatment, because that can erase the effect. It should not demand matches so strict that no real comparison remains. It should not ignore a background fact that decides who got treatment in the first place.

Why It Works

This works by removing the obvious alternate explanation before measuring the effect. If tutored students improve more, the improvement means little when they also had better teachers and more family support. After the right background facts are held fixed, treatment status is less able to hide those other differences. The method does not magically prove cause from any dataset; it earns a cause claim only when the comparison is fair enough for the question being asked. TRECA adds a practical caution: when the estimated benefit for a person or group is too uncertain, abstaining can be safer than pretending the treatment effect is known. The formal term covariates means the background facts used for the fair comparison. Causal identification means the evidence and assumptions are strong enough to support the treatment-effect claim.

estimate a local effect only when treated and untreated cases are comparable after the chosen background facts

Evidence Check

Report whether treated and untreated groups match after the chosen facts, whether both groups actually exist in the local region, how much a hidden factor would need to change the conclusion, whether fake outcomes show no effect, whether nearby fact choices change the answer, and how often TRECA abstains instead of making an overconfident recommendation.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the checklist misses an important hidden cause, when the local treated and untreated groups barely overlap, when adjustment accidentally changes the question, or when a policy conclusion is made for people unlike anyone in the evidence.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence warns that data can fit more than one cause story. Fair Causal Bandits use estimates of what an action would change to choose actions. Missing-covariate work keeps uncertainty about unseen background facts. All ask the same first question: what does the evidence really let us claim?

EXEMPLAR 23

Neural Effect Search And HypoSpace

Discovery As Making Claims That Can Be Checked

Problem

Imagine reading thousands of lab notes and asking a model to suggest what might be true. It can produce many smart-sounding ideas. The danger is that the same notes used to invent the idea can also make the idea look supported, even when it is only a coincidence found by searching hard enough.

State Space

The state is the whole discovery situation: all the claims the system could have made, the evidence already used to invent them, the new claim being considered, the rule for searching, the evidence held back for checking, and the rule for saying "this claim survived." The pile of claims matters because searching a larger pile gives the system more chances to find a lucky accident. The formal term is hypothesis space: the full menu of claims the search was allowed to try.

Object

The object is a claim that can lose. A useful scientific claim is not just a fluent sentence. It must point to evidence that could prove it wrong, and it must say what observation would count as failure. That is the plain meaning of a rejectable hypothesis.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is testability. The system may look through many possible ideas, but the final accepted idea must still face evidence that was not already spent inventing it.

Allowed Movement

The search may propose, combine, narrow, or rank ideas. What it cannot do is pretend that looking at many ideas is the same as looking at one. The more ideas it tries, the easier it is to find a lucky pattern, so the final check must remember how wide the search was.

Why It Works

This works by separating invention from checking. Search is allowed to be creative and broad because its job is to find possible claims. Acceptance must be stricter because the winner has already benefited from the search. If the system looks through many claims, some will fit by luck. The final check asks whether the claim still holds on evidence that did not help create it, and it counts how many chances the search had to find a lucky pattern. The formal names are validation, meaning checking on separate evidence, and false discovery control, meaning limiting how often searched-for coincidences are mistaken for real findings.

accept a claim only when separate checking evidence supports it after counting how many claims were searched

Evidence Check

Report held-back evidence, repeated checks by another method, failed examples, how many claims were searched, whether the claim can be reproduced, and whether it survives independent data or execution.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the same evidence invents and approves the claim, when the claim is too vague to be wrong, or when the system searches thousands of ideas but reports the winner as if it were chosen in advance.

Connection

Bitween and RefineStat make scientific artifacts checkable. PaperBench breaks paper claims into evidence trails. Neural Effect Search connects effect estimates to new candidate claims. The shared point is that discovery only counts when the evidence can push back.

EXEMPLAR 24

Mind-Omni And PRISM

Brain Signals As A Careful Translation Layer

Problem

Brain and body signals are messy measurements, not words or pictures. A scanner can pick up machine patterns, timing quirks, subject differences, and task shortcuts. If a model turns those signals into ordinary tokens too quickly, it may learn the noise around the measurement instead of the biological state inside it.

State Space

Imagine a translator listening to a noisy recording. The translator writes a small note that keeps the speaker's meaning but drops the microphone hiss, room echo, and accent details that do not matter for the job. The state is the measured body signal, where and when it was recorded, what the person was doing, which person or session produced it, the small note the model builds, and the final decoded output. What must stay the same is the biological pattern needed for the later answer. What may change is the surface form of the measurement. The formal term is latent representation: the model's smaller working version of a messy signal.

Object

The object is the translation checkpoint between body measurements and ordinary model reasoning. One side is raw signal: voltages, blood-flow traces, timing patterns, or sensor readings. The other side is the model's working language. The checkpoint decides what crosses. What must cross is evidence about the real brain or body state; what must not cross is scanner identity, session habit, prompt shortcut, or a memorized output shape. The formal term is structured latent interface: a bridge with rules about which hidden signal notes are allowed into the rest of the model.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the task-relevant biological information. What may change is the wrapping: raw voltages, time traces, region summaries, masks, or internal tokens can be rearranged if the answer still changes for real signal changes and stays steady for irrelevant recording noise.

Allowed Movement

The method may group the signal into chunks, hide parts during training, align it with text or images, or translate it into model tokens. It must keep proving that the answer depends on the biological signal itself, not on the prompt, scanner, subject identity, recording site, or a familiar reconstruction pattern.

Why It Works

This works when the bridge is selective in the right way. If the bridge is too wide, the model can sneak through shortcuts such as scanner marks or subject identity. If it is too narrow, it drops the biological evidence needed for the task. The useful bridge keeps only what helps the later answer respond to the person's measured state. That is why swapping the real signal should change the result, while changing recording noise should not. The formal term is bottleneck: the narrow checkpoint that decides which signal facts are allowed through.

build a working note from the signal so the output changes for real signal changes, not scanner noise

Evidence Check

Swap signal pieces while holding the prompt fixed, test new subjects and sessions, remove the signal to see if performance collapses, control for scanner and site artifacts, compare against prompt-only answers, and use tasks where familiar output patterns alone cannot solve the problem.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the working note stores scanner or subject artifacts, when pretty reconstructions hide weak biological tracking, when the model can answer without the measured signal, or when the bridge preserves patterns that are stable in one lab but not in a new person or session.

Connection

Tell-Tale Norm treats hidden model state as a signal. VISUALSWAP asks which input channel actually carried evidence. Causal Route Gating asks which route produced the answer. Mind-Omni and PRISM ask the same question for brain and body measurements: did the biological signal really do the work?

EXEMPLAR 25

HDFlow, PACT, And BehaviorVLA

Embodied Action As Moves The World Allows

Problem

A robot can say "pick up the cup, place it on the shelf" and still fail. The gripper may not fit, the cup may slip, the shelf may be too high, or the robot may need to move around an obstacle first. In physical tasks, a plan is only real if every step can actually happen.

State Space

The state is what the robot can see, where objects are, what is touching what, what the goal is, which small actions are available, how the world changes after an action, and what feedback comes back during execution.

Object

The object is a map of moves the world actually allows. Each point is a physical situation. Each link is an action that can really move the world from one situation to the next. If there is no link, the robot cannot simply declare the move done. The formal term is feasible transition graph: the map of physical states connected only by actions the world can actually execute.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is executable progress toward the goal. What may change is the level of description: the robot can reason in words, subgoals, or motor commands, as long as each described move maps back to a possible physical move.

Allowed Movement

The policy may break a task into subgoals, reuse learned skills, or describe actions in language-like tokens. It cannot treat a label such as "grasp" as successful unless the hand, object, timing, and contact make that grasp possible.

Why It Works

This works by making a huge control problem smaller without lying about physics. Instead of planning every tiny motor value from scratch, the system plans through local moves that have already been checked or learned. The plain rule is that every chosen move must be a move the world can actually make. The formal terms are dynamics, meaning how the world changes after an action, and constraints, meaning rules that make some changes impossible.

a plan is valid only if every chosen action can really move the world from the current state to the next state

Evidence Check
