The state is the current world: actors, roads or objects, layout, which places connect to which other places, facts the camera cannot directly see, what the camera sees now, the action taken, the next world state, and the rendered image. The model needs both visible facts and hidden facts, because the camera may not show every car, lane, object, or connection that affects what can happen next. The formal terms are topology, meaning how places connect, and latent variable, meaning a hidden fact the model uses even when it is not directly shown.

Object

Imagine a toy town on a table. If you push the blue car left, the town after the push is not just any pretty picture of a road; it is the same town after that specific push. The object is the "after this action, what can the world become?" rule. What must stay the same is the identity of the world: the same cars, lanes, blocked roads, hidden objects, and physical rules. What may change is the next position, speed, view, and visible image caused by the chosen action. The formal term is action-conditioned transition: the rule for how a chosen action changes the world state.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is lawful continuity. The future should still come from the same underlying world, not from a fresh video clip that merely looks plausible. A left turn may change the lane position; it should not silently move an unseen car, erase a wall, or swap the road layout.

Allowed Movement

The generator may store the scene as pixels, lists of objects, a road graph, or hidden numbers. Those storage choices may change. The non-negotiable part is that action-changing facts remain present: which objects exist, where they can move, which spaces connect, and which futures are impossible.

Why It Works

The method works by separating the hidden table from the camera picture. First the model updates the table: after this action, these objects can move in these ways. Then it draws the camera view of the updated table. That separation helps because the rule for what can happen lives with cars, roads, objects, and connections, while the picture is only one view of them. What must stay the same is the underlying world identity and action law. What may change is the rendered view and next positions caused by the action. The formal term is transition kernel: the rule that gives the possible next worlds after a current world and action.

update world state from current state plus action, then render the new state

Evidence Check

Run tests where the same state gets different actions, edit road or object connections, check actor interactions, run long rollouts, inspect hidden-state consistency, and compare against video continuation that produces plausible frames while ignoring actions.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when hidden state leaves out variables the action changes, when realistic rendering hides impossible physics, when the model redraws a fresh plausible scene instead of updating the same world, or when the checker cannot inspect the state where the error happens.

Connection

HDFlow and PACT restrict physical action steps. Stable Video Infinity repairs long generated paths. VideoKR asks whether visual evidence is retained. FlashWorld and VectorWorld put those ideas inside an interactive simulator.

EXEMPLAR 52

Depth Anything 3

Vision As Recovering How Far Things Are

Problem

A model can name a chair, a door, or a person and still misunderstand where they are in space. For walking, grasping, reconstructing a room, or changing a camera view, the missing fact is often distance, not object category.

State Space

The state is the image, the camera's internal settings, the line from the camera through each pixel, the estimated distance at each pixel, the 3D point in the scene, ambiguity about scale, hidden surfaces, and the downstream spatial task. Camera intrinsics are the camera settings needed to turn pixels into viewing rays.

Object

The object is a map from flat pixels to 3D space. A ray is the line of possible locations behind one pixel. Depth picks how far along that line the real surface is.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is spatial layout: what is near, what is far, what blocks what, and what space is reachable. What may change is the internal representation used to store that layout.

Allowed Movement

The model may infer depth from shadows, perspective, texture, and learned regularities. It must stay consistent with camera geometry and cannot replace measured spatial structure with guesses based only on familiar object sizes.

Why It Works

This works because depth adds the coordinate lost when the 3D world was projected into a 2D image. A pixel gives a direction. Depth gives a distance. Together they locate a scene point, making occlusion, relative position, and reachable space computable.

pixel plus camera ray plus depth gives a 3D scene point

Evidence Check

Measure real distance, near-far ordering, scale transfer, camera changes, object boundaries, cross-scene generalization, and cases where object recognition succeeds but spatial layout fails.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when it guesses common object size instead of scene geometry, when camera settings are wrong, or when a task needs uncertainty over several possible depths rather than one confident estimate.

Connection

Quotient diffusion chooses the right geometric space. FlashWorld maintains world state. Embodied benchmarks measure real state changes. Depth Anything 3 supplies a missing coordinate that many physical and multimodal systems need.

EXEMPLAR 53

VibeVoice, TTSDS2, And Hibiki-Zero

Speech As Sound Over Time, Not Just Text

Problem

Speech carries words, but also timing, speaker identity, emotion, rhythm, pauses, and audio quality. A transcript can be correct while the spoken result is late, flat, unnatural, or impossible to understand.

State Space

The state is the meaning, the sound pattern, the speaker information, tone and rhythm, the partial audio heard so far, the allowed delay, the generated waveform, and the speech-quality score. Prosody means tone, stress, rhythm, and timing. A waveform is the actual audio signal.

Object

Imagine reading a joke from a transcript and then hearing someone tell it. The same words can land differently if the pause, voice, speed, or emphasis changes. The object is the running bundle that keeps those things together while speech is being made or translated: what has already been said, what still needs to be said, who is speaking, how fast they are speaking, and how the audio should sound. The formal term is acoustic-temporal latent state: hidden speech information that carries sound and time, not text alone.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the listener-facing message. The words, speaker identity, emotional tone, timing, and understandability must remain tied together. What may change is the language, codec, generated waveform, or scoring method.

Allowed Movement

The system may translate, synthesize, stream, clean up, or judge speech. It should not flatten speech into finished text too early, because text throws away the pauses, stress, rhythm, turn timing, and sound defects that decide whether the result works for a listener.

Why It Works

This works because speech is a moving signal, not a sentence printed on a page. A streaming system has to decide what can safely be spoken now and what must wait for more context. The hidden running bundle gives it memory of the sound already emitted and room to carry tone and timing forward, so the next piece of audio fits the previous piece instead of restarting from text each time.

speech output should depend on meaning, voice, prosody, and latency, not transcript text alone

Evidence Check

Measure intelligibility, speaker match, prosody, naturalness, delay, turn timing, artifact-level speech ratings, and cases where transcript accuracy disagrees with audio quality.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when speech is text-correct but late, flat, unstable, speaker-inconsistent, or judged through text-only proxies that cannot hear the error.

Connection

Mind-Omni creates an interface for biological signals. VideoKR preserves visual evidence over time. WebDevJudge checks artifacts in their native medium. VibeVoice, TTSDS2, and Hibiki-Zero say speech must also be generated and judged in its own medium.

EXEMPLAR 54

CausalGame

Causal Learning As Choosing The Test That Breaks The Tie

Problem

Watching the world can leave several cause-and-effect stories equally plausible. If a system can run an experiment, it should not merely describe causality. It should choose the action that separates the competing stories.

State Space

The remaining doubt is like a list of suspects that have not yet been ruled out. The working record includes possible cause stories, current observations, available deliberate changes or measurements, their costs, current uncertainty, and the cause-and-effect question being answered. After each observation, some stories become less likely and some remain plausible. The formal term is posterior uncertainty: the remaining doubt after updating the possible stories with the evidence already seen.

Object

The object is a rule for choosing the next test. If two cause stories both explain the old data, the next action should make them predict different results. The action is not an answer in words; it is the deliberate change or measurement that should make rival stories disagree. The formal term is experiment policy: the rule for choosing that next test.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is cause-and-effect knowledge that the evidence can actually support. Each chosen action should either rule out some possible stories or improve the estimate of the effect we care about.

Allowed Movement

The agent may deliberately change something, observe, query, or stop. What may change is the chosen experiment and the next observation. What must stay the same is the goal: reducing doubt about the cause-and-effect question. It should choose actions because they distinguish possible worlds, not because they lead to fluent explanations. The formal term is intervention: a deliberate change made to test what follows.

Why It Works

This works when different cause stories predict different outcomes under the same deliberate change. If one story says fertilizer caused plant growth and another says extra sunlight did, a good test changes one while holding the other steady. The best experiment is the one where the stories disagree the most for the least cost. The value of the experiment is how much doubt it is expected to remove once the result is seen. The formal term is information gain: the amount of remaining doubt a test is expected to remove.

choose the experiment expected to separate competing causal stories most for its cost

Evidence Check

Measure uncertainty reduction, experiment-choice quality, hidden-cause traps, cost-sensitive choices, recovery of the real cause rule on held-out cases, and whether the agent separates correlation from controllable effect rather than only describing a plausible story.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the deliberate changes are unrealistic, hidden causes are missing from the environment, costs are ignored, or the agent memorizes benchmark-specific experiments instead of learning which evidence separates cause stories.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence reports what passive data cannot distinguish. Local Covariate Selection identifies target effects under assumptions. Neural Effect Search proposes claims. CausalGame adds the active step: choose evidence that can break the tie.

EXEMPLAR 55

Softmax Turing And HATSolver

Expressivity As Showing What Computation The Architecture Can Carry

Problem

Listing a model's parts does not tell us what it can compute. A pile of calculator buttons matters only if the buttons can be arranged into the needed procedure. Neural architectures need the same test: can their operations actually carry the target computation?

State Space

The state is the working desk inside the model: tokens or symbols, which pieces each token reads, internal memory-like numbers, smaller subproblems, update rules, numeric precision limits, and final decisions. Attention weights are the model's learned "which pieces should this piece read" scores.

Object

The object is the procedure the model parts can actually carry out. A calculator with many buttons is useful only when those buttons can be pressed in an order that performs long division, stores a carry, or solves a subproblem. Neural architectures need the same proof: can these operations store information, update memory, solve smaller expressions, simulate steps, or combine algebraic pieces in the required order? The formal term is expressivity: what a model architecture can represent or compute in principle.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the algorithmic relation: the right sequence of internal transformations from input to output. Different weights can implement it, but the computation being claimed must survive.

Allowed Movement

The argument may simplify depth, width, numeric precision, or input type. What must stay the same is the needed input-to-output procedure. What may change is the idealized model size or resource setting, but the proof has to say those resources clearly because a computation that works only with impossible precision or huge size may not explain real models.

Why It Works

The claim works when each architecture operation can be matched to a defined step in the needed procedure. Softmax attention can be used to read from memory or choose next states under a construction. Hierarchical attention helps when the task itself breaks into smaller expressions that can be solved and recombined. The key is a step-by-step map: this layer stores this fact, this operation reads it, this later step uses it. The label "expressive" is only earned after that map exists.

the architecture expresses a task when its layers can implement the task's needed steps

Evidence Check

Separate "can be constructed" from "can be learned," state how resources scale, test finite precision, remove hierarchy or attention parts, and check whether trained models use the constructed procedure rather than a shortcut.

Failure Boundary

The claim fails when possible computation is mistaken for learnable computation, required resources are unrealistic, or the task does not actually have the hierarchy the architecture assumes.

Connection

Transformer Circuits gives concrete algorithm maps. Language Generation in the Limit warns about evidence needed to learn them. Finite Test Certification asks what evidence supports a deployed claim. Softmax Turing and HATSolver fill the capability-in-principle side of that chain.

EXEMPLAR 56

Structure Learning CI And DiCoLa

Graph Discovery As Asking Which Links The Data Can Actually Support

Problem

Finding a causal graph can look like drawing arrows. The harder question is whether the data really supports each arrow or missing arrow. Finite, noisy data can make weak links look absent, and hidden causes can make unrelated variables move together.

State Space

The state is the observed facts, possible hidden facts, candidate graph family, facts held fixed during tests, statistical tests, divide-and-conquer strategy, sample size, and the returned graph or set of graphs. CI means conditional independence: two variables stop being related after we hold a chosen set of other variables fixed.

Object

Imagine noticing that ice cream sales and sunburns rise together. If you hold the weather fixed and compare only equally hot days, the link may disappear; heat was the shared background cause. The object is this kind of "do these still move together after we make the right background facts the same?" evidence. What must stay the same is honesty about what the data can prove. What may change is the graph the method narrows down to. When several arrow drawings fit the same evidence, the data has not earned a single drawing.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is honesty about what the evidence proves. Scaling the algorithm should not turn weak tests into false certainty about arrows.

Allowed Movement

The method may split the graph, restrict possible graph shapes, or handle hidden facts. Each claimed edge or missing edge must still be tied to checked evidence about whether relationships remain after other facts are held fixed.

Why It Works

This works because causal structure leaves shadows in the data. If one variable affects another only through a third, the first and second can stop being related after the third is held fixed. Divide-and-conquer helps only if the split keeps the held-fixed relationship facts needed to rebuild the graph family. The key test asks whether two facts still move together after the relevant background facts are fixed. The formal term is conditional independence: two facts stop moving together after the right background facts are held fixed.

use supported held-fixed relationship tests to narrow the set of cause graphs still consistent with the data

Evidence Check

Report whether held-fixed relationship tests are calibrated, sensitivity to sample size, power on weak links, false and missing edges, hidden-variable stress, split-and-recombine error, and remaining doubt when one graph is not identified.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when independence tests are too weak, hidden variables break assumptions, decomposition cuts needed links, or a single graph is reported even though several graphs fit the same evidence.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence protects against overclaiming. Local Covariate Selection uses graph assumptions for target effects. CausalGame adds active interventions. Structure Learning CI and DiCoLa define the passive evidence layer underneath.

EXEMPLAR 57

RefineStat, MedAgentGym, WZ-LLM, And VERINA

Scientific Work As Claims That Must Run Or Check

Problem

A generated paper section, code analysis, or proof can sound convincing while being wrong. Scientific work becomes useful only when the claim enters an environment that can expose bad statistics, failed code, invalid proof steps, or an unfinished workflow.

State Space

The state is the generated artifact plus the place it must survive: dataset or proof context, execution environment, checker, diagnostics, repair action, task goal, and final accepted or rejected claim.

Object

Imagine a lab report with a beautiful conclusion, but the spreadsheet formula is broken. The report is not acceptable just because the paragraph sounds right. The object is the set of outputs that pass the right kind of inspection. Code has to run. A statistical fix has to answer the stated data question. A proof step has to be accepted by the proof system. A medical workflow has to reach a valid completed state. The formal term is admissible artifact: an output allowed by the relevant checker.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is checkable scientific validity. The model may propose code, analyses, priors, or proof steps, but acceptance must depend on evidence that can reject them.

Allowed Movement

The generator may repair priors, rerun code, change analysis steps, or search proof states. What may change is the proposed artifact or repair path. What must stay the same is that the checker can see the failure mode relevant to the scientific claim.

Why It Works

This works by splitting proposal from acceptance. The generator can suggest a repair, proof step, analysis, or workflow action. The environment then checks the thing in the place where failure would actually show up. That matters because scientific language can hide errors that execution, diagnostics, proof rules, or workflow state can reveal. The formal term is domain constraints: the rules native to the scientific setting that decide whether the artifact is allowed.

accept a scientific artifact only when the relevant checker passes the claim it is supposed to support

Evidence Check

Report execution success, statistical diagnostic repair, proof-kernel acceptance, biomedical workflow completion, false-positive checks, repair paths, and places where the checker is narrower than the final scientific conclusion.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the checker validates syntax but not meaning, sandbox tasks are toy versions of real practice, or a verified component is treated as proof of an unsupported end-to-end claim.

Connection

WebDevJudge and PaperBench evaluate native artifacts. Neural Effect Search proposes claims that can be rejected. CausalGame chooses evidence. RefineStat, MedAgentGym, WZ-LLM, and VERINA make scientific generation accountable to a failure channel.

EXEMPLAR 58

MAPF Via MMOT And Schrodinger Bridges

Multi-Agent Planning As Moving Everyone Together Without Collisions

Problem

If every robot, vehicle, or character plans its own shortest path, the group can collide, block hallways, swap identities, or waste space. The real problem is not one route. It is coordinated movement through shared space and time.

State Space

The state is the group timetable: where agents start, where they need to end, whether identities matter, positions at each time, obstacles, motion rules, collision rules, path costs, and any smoothing used in the solver.

Object

Imagine moving a class through a narrow hallway. Giving each person their own shortest route can create jams. The object is the full timetable of where everyone is at each moment, so the group gets from all starts to all goals without two bodies needing the same space at the same time. What must stay the same is the no-collision, valid-motion rule. What may change is which person takes which route, how waits are inserted, and whether the solver first thinks in terms of a crowd flow before assigning exact people. The formal term is constrained transport plan: a movement table that sends everyone from starts to goals while obeying motion and collision rules.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is feasible coordinated motion. Each agent should reach a valid target while preserving no-collision rules and global consistency.

Allowed Movement

The planner may temporarily think of the agents as a moving crowd rather than as isolated individuals. It may soften the search so many possible group routes can be considered before choosing exact paths. What must stay the same is that the softened plan can be turned back into real routes for real agents. The formal term is Schrodinger bridge: a way to choose likely paths between a starting cloud and an ending cloud while staying close to a reference movement rule.

Why It Works

This works because conflict is handled before routes are finalized. If everyone plans alone, collisions are discovered late and repairs can create new collisions. If the solver plans the whole moving crowd at once, it can rule out shared-space conflicts while choosing the paths. What must stay the same is real no-collision movement for named agents. What may change is whether the solver first reasons about a crowd-like flow before assigning exact bodies back to routes.

choose joint paths from starts to goals while satisfying motion rules and no-collision constraints

Evidence Check

Report collision rate, path cost, scaling with agent count, identity consistency, obstacle changes, start-goal perturbations, whether softened flows become valid individual routes, and comparison with independent planning plus repair.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when relaxed group movement cannot be assigned back to real agents, constraints are too expensive to enforce, or smoothing hides rare but catastrophic collisions.

Connection

Fair Optimal Transport constrains allocation matches. Safety-Guided Flow moves samples under constraints. HDFlow and PACT restrict embodied actions. MAPF via MMOT and Schrodinger bridges makes transport a physical planning object.

EXEMPLAR 59

Binary RAR

Factuality As No Claim Contradicts The Evidence

Problem

An answer can contain nine true sentences and one serious false one. If the score averages all ten, the answer may still look good. In high-trust retrieval, one unsupported contradiction can make the whole answer unsafe to use.

State Space

The state is the user question, the retrieved evidence, the answer's individual claims, how each claim connects to evidence, contradictions, missing support, the reward signal, and how training changes the model.

Object

The object is a pass/fail rule for the whole answer. A binary rule means there are only two outcomes: pass or fail. The rule asks whether the answer is allowed at all, not how many nice sentences surround the bad one. The formal term is admissibility predicate: the yes-or-no rule that decides whether an answer is allowed.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is trust in the evidence link. The model may become more fluent or complete, but it should not hide a local factual error inside many safe sentences.

Allowed Movement

The model may change wording, retrieve different evidence, abstain, or split claims more carefully. It may not get more reward by adding unsupported claims whose errors are diluted inside an average score.

Why It Works

This works because the reward no longer gives partial credit for an answer with a contradiction. The model is trained to stay inside the set of answers where every factual claim is supported and none conflict with evidence. In plain terms: one serious contradiction fails the answer.

pass only if every factual claim is supported by the retrieved evidence and not contradicted by it

Evidence Check

Evaluate contradiction rate, unsupported claims, abstention behavior, completeness, retrieval changes, adversarial evidence conflicts, and whether gains persist when evidence is replaced by harder near-contradictions.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the claim extractor misses implied claims, retrieved evidence is incomplete or wrong, or strict pass/fail scoring makes the model over-abstain instead of using evidence better.

Connection

Omni-Reward and JudgeBench study reward as measurement. WebDevJudge checks real artifacts. CRAMF retrieves exact proof-library items. Binary RAR is the factuality version of the same principle: the checker must see the evidence relation the reward claims to measure.

EXEMPLAR 60

Conformal Policy Control

Policy Improvement As Staying Near Where Safety Was Checked

Problem

A new policy can get more reward by moving into behavior the safety data never covered. The question is not only "is this policy better?" It is also "has it moved so far from the checked behavior that our safety evidence no longer applies?"

State Space

The state is the road-test record: data from a reference policy, a candidate improved policy, a score for how unusual the candidate looks, a held-out calibration set, the tolerated risk level, how action patterns shift, and the final deploy-or-reject decision.

Object

Imagine approving a new driver only for roads like the ones used in the road test. A faster route is not automatically acceptable if it takes the driver onto icy mountain roads that were never checked. The object is the allowed neighborhood around behavior with evidence behind it. The method uses examples held back from training to draw a practical line for "too unlike what was checked." The formal terms are conformal threshold, meaning that held-out-data cutoff, and risk set, meaning the accepted region.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is a finite-evidence guardrail around unsafe deviation. Policy improvement is allowed, but only while the candidate remains close enough to behavior for which the calibration data is relevant.

Allowed Movement

The optimized policy may differ from the reference policy. What may change is action choice when improvement stays within the checked neighborhood. What must stay the same is the relevance of the safety evidence. The deviation score measures how unlike the checked behavior this candidate looks. The formal term is nonconformity: how unusual the candidate looks compared with checked behavior.

Why It Works

This works by turning limited safe-behavior data into a practical cutoff. The method does not need to understand every future failure perfectly; it asks a narrower question first: does this candidate look more unusual than the checked examples allowed? The cutoff has teeth only when the held-out examples and future cases are comparable, like test papers shuffled from the same stack. The formal term is exchangeability: the condition that lets the held-out examples stand in for future cases.

accept the improved policy only when its deviation from checked behavior stays below the calibrated cutoff

Evidence Check

Report violation rate, calibration coverage, sensitivity to reference-policy quality, shift tests, risk-level sweeps, whether the unusualness score tracks real safety variables, and cases where unconstrained reward optimization would leave the accepted region.

Failure Boundary

The guarantee weakens when calibration data and deployment are not comparable, when the deviation score misses the real safety variable, or when the reference policy is unsafe in rare states.

Connection

CSPO restricts movement near safety boundaries. SafeDPO shapes preference movement. Rare Event Analysis studies low-probability failures. Finite Test Certification asks what limited evidence proves. Conformal Policy Control makes policy improvement a finite-evidence constrained move.

EXEMPLAR 61

FedPissa, SmartFed, FlatLand, And FedARC

Federated Adaptation As Sharing What Is Common And Keeping What Is Local

Problem

Federated clients are not copies of one another. A phone, hospital, language community, or device may have different data, privacy limits, tasks, and compute. Averaging everyone together can erase local needs, while training everyone alone wastes shared signal.

State Space

The state is the global model, each client's local data pattern, small adapter modules, shared reference points, communication budget, private parameters, shared parameters, and each client's utility. Low-rank adapters are small trainable pieces that change the model through a limited number of direction patterns. Anchors are shared reference examples or features that help clients line up meaning.

Object

Imagine several teachers improving the same textbook from different classrooms. A clearer explanation of fractions may help everyone. A note about one classroom's local exam should not be averaged into every copy. The object is the split between improvements that mean the same thing across clients and changes that belong to one client. What must stay the same is useful shared learning. What may change is each client's private adapter, local route, or amount of communication. The formal term is decomposition of update space: separating the update into shared directions, private directions, and resource-limited routes.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful common structure without client collapse. Shared updates should improve what transfers across clients while preserving local factors that averaging would damage.

Allowed Movement

Clients may share anchors, selected adapter components, rank-wise experts, or a shared model spine. Aggregation should avoid mixing updates that mean different things for different clients.

Why It Works

This works when the system first asks, "Are these two changes about the same thing?" Shared anchors act like common test pages that let clients line up meanings. Selective averaging then shares only the pieces that point in a common useful direction. Local pieces stay local because mixing them would turn one client's special need into everyone else's unwanted change. The deep idea is simple: average only things that are measured in the same meaning-system.

share only the update parts that align across clients; keep incompatible local parts private or personalized

Evidence Check

Measure global accuracy, per-client utility, personalization gap, communication cost, signs that private information is exposed, update interference, anchor stability, and performance when client data shifts or modalities are missing.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when anchors do not represent shared meaning, small adapter pieces store private artifacts, routing favors large clients, or personalization hides degradation for smaller clients.

Connection

RAIN-Merging and LoRA theory restrict update movement. Common Corpus makes data sources explicit. VALUEFLOW asks who benefits from aggregation. Federated adaptation asks the systems version: what can be shared without destroying what should stay local?

EXEMPLAR 62

CRAMF

Formal Retrieval As Finding The Exact Math Library Names

Problem

A proof assistant does not accept "the usual theorem about this idea." It needs the exact definition, theorem name, and symbol used in its library. A model can understand the informal idea but still fail because it chooses the wrong formal object.

State Space

The state is the library lookup problem: the informal statement, library definitions and theorem names, retrieved candidates, reranking scores, proof context, generated formal statement, and checker feedback. Mathlib is the large Lean mathematics library.

Object

Imagine trying to cite a law in court. Saying "the rule about contracts" is not enough; the court needs the exact section name. A proof assistant is similarly strict. The object is a narrowed menu of exact library names that may express the informal idea. What must stay the same is the intended meaning. What may change is the route from ordinary wording to the exact symbol the checker recognizes. The formal terms are checker-known symbol, meaning a library name the proof assistant recognizes, and retrieval-conditioned, meaning generation is guided by retrieved candidates instead of guessing from scratch.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the idea the proof is trying to state. The system may change ordinary wording into exact checker symbols, but it must not swap in a nearby library name that means something different.

Allowed Movement

The system may expand the query, retrieve definitions, rerank candidates, and condition generation on them. It cannot treat nearby English words as interchangeable if the formal library treats them as different objects.

Why It Works

The method works by shrinking the search before proof generation. Without retrieval, the model must guess from a huge set of exact library names. Retrieval supplies plausible checker-known symbols, and the proof checker can reject wrong ones. The hard part is the number of choices: fewer plausible names makes the search less scattered, the way finding a book is easier with the right shelf than with the whole library unsorted. What must stay the same is the intended proof idea. What may change is the route from ordinary wording to the exact checker-known symbol. The formal term is entropy: a count of how spread out the remaining choices are.

map an informal concept to likely checker-known symbols, then generate using symbols the checker can check

Evidence Check

Measure whether required definitions are retrieved, reranking precision, proof-checker acceptance, proof repair rate, near-miss definition errors, and performance when the needed concept is rare or buried deep in the library.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when retrieval misses the decisive object, the informal statement is underspecified, several library translations are valid but incompatible, or the proof checker accepts syntax that still misses the intended theorem meaning.

Connection

VERINA and WZ-LLM rely on formal checking. WebDevJudge and PaperBench evaluate native artifacts. Binary RAR ties claims to retrieved evidence. CRAMF supplies the formal-math infrastructure layer: before a proof can be checked, the system must choose the right objects to speak in.

EXEMPLAR 63

Consequence-Based Utility

Verification Through What The Claim Lets You Do Next

Problem

Some claims are hard to check directly. A theorem, method, or scientific explanation may be plausible, but the ideal checker may be too slow, unavailable, or beyond what a human can inspect.

State Space

The state is the candidate claim, downstream tasks the claim should affect, weaker available checkers, checking cost, prior evidence, and the decision that depends on whether the claim is useful or true.

Object

Imagine a mechanic who cannot open the engine today but can turn the key, listen to the sound, and check whether the lights dim. Those consequences do not prove everything, but they are not random either. The object is a planned link from a hard-to-check claim to easier signs that should change if the claim is doing real work. What must stay the same is the connection between claim and consequence. What may change is which downstream task, prediction, proof step, or decision is used as the visible test. The formal term is consequence map: the planned link from a hard claim to easier checks it should affect.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is relevance. The downstream consequence must stay tied to the original claim, not become a separate easy stand-in score that can be improved while the claim itself remains unsupported.

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may use consequence checks instead of direct proof, but only if the consequence was defined before selecting the claim and is informative under stated assumptions.

Why It Works

This works when the consequence was chosen before seeing the claim and false lookalike claims do not improve it the same way. A hard claim earns more trust when it helps with a pre-set task that depends on the claim being right: better predictions, solved related cases, shorter later proofs, or different decisions that matched false claims cannot produce. It is weaker than direct proof, but stronger than judging whether the claim sounds impressive.

trust a hard claim more when pre-defined checkable consequences improve in ways false claims do not

Evidence Check

Define consequence tasks before scoring, compare against plausible false claims, report downstream effect size, test held-out consequences, and audit whether the consequence can be gamed without the original claim being valid.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when consequences only measure impressiveness, are chosen after seeing the claim, or can be produced by a false claim through a shortcut.

Connection

HypoSpace evaluates coverage of possible explanations. RefineStat and VERINA use executable checkers. Benchmarking at the Edge of Comprehension models bounded human verification. Consequence-Based Utility covers the middle case where direct proof is missing but implications can still be tested.

EXEMPLAR 64

GraphGlue, Embedding Translation, And Diffract

Representation Transfer As Making Two Internal Languages Compatible

Problem

Two models or graph domains may both encode useful structure but use different internal coordinates. If we connect them without translation, nearby things in one system may not stay nearby in the other, and a useful direction may point somewhere wrong.

State Space

The state is multiple internal representation spaces, local domain structure, maps between embeddings, attention-head updates, confidence estimates, shared tasks, and transfer loss. An embedding is a vector representation of an item inside a model: a list of numbers used as its internal address and description.

Object

Imagine two subway maps of the same city: one drawn geographically and one drawn for easy reading. A good translation does not preserve paper distances; it preserves which stops connect and which trips are short for riders. The object is a translation between two internal maps. What must stay the same is the relationship the task uses: neighbors, directions, graph links, or useful update effects. What may change is the coordinate system used to store those relationships. The formal term is manifold: a space that may bend globally but still has local neighborhoods where "near" and "direction" make sense.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is relational meaning. If two items are related, near, or directionally different in a task-relevant way, that relation should survive transfer.

Allowed Movement

The method may align local coordinate charts, translate embeddings, rewind low-value attention-head updates, or assign confidence to transfer. It must preserve the relations used by the target task.

Why It Works

This works when the translation preserves the relationships that later decisions rely on. Matching average locations can look successful while breaking local neighborhoods, graph edges, or useful directions. Local maps help because the same global space can twist: a translation that works in one region may fail elsewhere. Confidence scores matter because they warn when the system is leaving the region where the learned translation was checked.

translate representations so task-relevant relationships remain true after the map

Evidence Check

Measure neighborhood preservation, cross-domain transfer, confidence outside the training domain, error after chained translations, attention-head removal or rewind effects, and cases where global alignment hides local distortion.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when no shared structure exists, alignment preserves easy geometry but loses the target variable, or confidence scores miss rare regions where translation errors compound.

Connection

FedARC uses anchors for federated alignment. Alignment-Sensitive Minimax Rates asks whether signal sits in recoverable directions. RAIN-Merging restricts adaptation subspaces. GraphGlue, Embedding Translation, and Diffract ask which coordinates can move across systems without changing meaning.

EXEMPLAR 65

MetamerGen

Human Perception As Different Images That Feel The Same

Problem

Two images can differ in pixels while a person reports seeing the same scene. This happens because human vision does not preserve every pixel equally: central fixation, peripheral gist, attention, and task all matter.

State Space

The state is the image, where the person looks, central and peripheral visual information, the internal percept, the generated image, human same-different judgment, and model perceptual score. Foveal means central high-detail vision; peripheral means outside the center of gaze.

Object

The object is the set of physically different images that a person judges as the same under a viewing condition. The plain rule is: different images can belong in one group when they count as the same for human perception.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the human scene percept, not the exact pixels or a generic image-model score. The generator should change details people do not use while preserving what they do use.

Allowed Movement

The model may change the image through a hidden generation process, but the change is valid only if it stays inside the tested human same-percept group. A metamer is one of these physically different but perceptually matching stimuli.

Why It Works

This works by turning a theory of perception into a generator constraint. Instead of asking only whether an image looks realistic, it asks which changes humans fail to notice. That makes generation a probe of what information human vision actually preserves.

two images are metamers when humans judge the same percept under the viewing condition even though the pixels differ

Evidence Check

Use same-different human judgments, fixation-conditioned tests, peripheral-detail changes, model-human disagreement analysis, and controls showing ordinary image similarity is not enough to explain acceptance.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when generated images exploit task artifacts, participant judgments are noisy or biased, or the model preserves low-level statistics without preserving the intended scene percept.

Connection

PRISM and Mind-Omni build interfaces to human signals. DAVE and Visual Attribution Streaming ask which visual evidence caused an answer. Depth Anything 3 recovers geometric variables. MetamerGen adds the human-perception object: what visual information can change without changing experience?

EXEMPLAR 66

Fair Causal Bandits

Exploration As Learning Without Taking Unfair Actions

Problem

A system that learns by trying actions can cause unfair harm while it is still learning. It is not enough to check fairness after learning is done. Some exploratory actions may already be illegitimate.

State Space

The state is the context, action, reward, cause assumptions, protected attribute, possible deliberate change to that attribute, uncertain fairness rule, confidence set, regret, and history of violations. A bandit is a repeated decision problem where the system learns rewards by trying actions.

Object

Imagine a lender trying loan offers while still learning which offers repay. The lender is not allowed to experiment by giving unfair offers to some people and calling the harm "data collection." The object is an exploration rule that chooses only actions the current evidence can defend. The fairness question asks what would have happened to the same case if a sensitive condition had been different. It is a same-person, changed-condition question, not a comparison between two unrelated people. The formal term is counterfactual: the changed-condition version of the same case used to ask whether the action would still be fair.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is legitimacy of action, not only total reward. The learner should improve reward while bounding the chance or size of unfairness under the protected change being considered.

Allowed Movement

The policy may try uncertain actions only when the current evidence range and cause assumptions say the action is plausibly fair, or when the algorithm explicitly accounts for the violation risk.

Why It Works

This works by treating each experiment as an action taken on a real case, not as harmless observation. The learner may prefer actions that teach it more, but those actions still have to pass the current fairness test. As evidence improves, the allowed set can expand or shrink. The point is to learn rewards without spending unfair treatment as the price of learning.

try high-reward actions only when the estimated unfairness under the protected change stays below the allowed risk

Evidence Check

Report reward regret and fairness violations together, test wrong causal graphs, vary protected-attribute interventions, inspect conservative or abstention regions, and compare with methods that audit fairness only after learning.

Failure Boundary

The guarantee fails when the causal graph omits key paths, sensitive-attribute interventions are not well defined, confidence intervals are miscalibrated, or fairness constraints depend on facts the system cannot observe.

Connection

TRECA abstains when treatment response is uncertain. Fair Optimal Transport restricts matches. VALUEFLOW exposes who loses under aggregation. Conformal Policy Control bounds policy movement from safe behavior. Fair Causal Bandits bring the same principle into exploration: valid learning must respect the constraint while information is incomplete.

EXEMPLAR 67

CreDRO, CRC, And MIRA

Uncertainty As The Set Of Actions Still Justified

Problem

A model can give a well-calibrated confidence number and still not tell a doctor, operator, or classifier what to do. The useful question is not only "how unsure are we?" It is "which actions are still safe or justified given what we do not know?"

State Space

The state is the input, possible labels or possible output distributions, the set of remaining plausible answers, the set of allowed predictions, the cost of wrong actions, abstention, calibration data, and possible deployment shift.

Object

Imagine a doctor choosing between treatment, another test, or waiting. A single confidence number may hide the important fact: two diagnoses are still plausible, and they recommend different actions. The object is the set of still-plausible answers shaped by the decision it must support. What must stay the same is action validity under the remaining possibilities. What may change is the size and shape of the set as evidence arrives. The formal terms are credal set, meaning a collection of possible probability models, conformal set, meaning a finite-data prediction set chosen from held-out examples, and regional mass agreement, meaning a check that probability is placed correctly in the parts of the answer space where the decision is sensitive.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is action validity. The uncertainty set should keep enough alternatives to avoid unsafe overconfidence, but remove enough impossible alternatives that a useful action remains.

Allowed Movement

The method may widen, shrink, or reshape the remaining possibilities when evidence changes decision risk. The set can grow when the situation is unfamiliar and shrink when evidence rules out alternatives. Coverage, meaning how often the true answer is included, is not enough if the set is so broad that every action becomes equally bad.

Why It Works

This works because the uncertainty is built for the decision that will consume it. If every plausible answer in the set leads to the same safe action, the system can act. If plausible answers disagree in a way that changes the action, the system should abstain, ask for more evidence, or choose an action that still works across the plausible answers.

act only when every plausible answer in the uncertainty set makes the action acceptably safe

Evidence Check

Report how often the set includes the right answer, set size, value of abstention, damage under cautious decisions, behavior outside the training mix, probability placement in decision-sensitive regions, and cases where ordinary confidence is calibrated but recommends the wrong action.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the uncertainty set leaves out the real shift, is so broad that no useful decision remains, or is calibrated on data whose costs do not match deployment.

Connection

LIMSSR keeps missing information explicit. Rare Event Analysis samples tail uncertainty. Conformal Policy Control bounds policy movement. Fair Causal Bandits constrain exploration. CreDRO, CRC, and MIRA make uncertainty useful by tying it to the decision.

EXEMPLAR 68

Floating-Point Neural Networks

Expressivity As What The Machine Can Actually Store And Compute

Problem

A theorem may say a network can represent a function using perfect real numbers. Real hardware does not use perfect real numbers. It stores rounded numbers with limited digits, so some delicate constructions disappear when the model is actually run.

State Space

The state is the network depth and width, activation function, number format, storable weights, rounding rule, intermediate layer values, and the target function. Floating point is the common machine format for approximate real numbers. A mantissa is the stored significant digits of such a number.

Object

Imagine a recipe that says to add exactly 0.0000000000003 teaspoons of salt. On paper the instruction is precise; in a real kitchen the spoon cannot measure it. The object is the set of input-output rules a network can carry when every number is stored with limited machine digits. What must stay the same is the claimed computation after rounding happens at each step. What may change is depth, width, number format, and allowed approximation. The formal term is expressivity under finite precision: what a network can represent when its numbers are the rounded numbers real machines can store.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the claimed computation after every operation is performed in the deployed number system. The proof should survive rounding, overflow, underflow, and limited precision.

Allowed Movement

The proof may change depth, width, precision, or representation. It must count which values can actually be stored and how every operation transforms them.

Why It Works

This works by moving the theorem onto the machine that runs the model. Perfect-real-number proofs can rely on tiny gaps, exact thresholds, or cancellations that need unlimited digits. Real machines round after each operation, so a gap that looked decisive on paper can vanish or flip. Floating-point analysis asks whether the same behavior remains when every layer uses those rounded values.

the implemented network must approximate the target after every operation uses the finite number format

Evidence Check

State precision assumptions, test different formats and accumulation orders, compare ideal constructions to implemented networks, and report input-output rules that work with perfect numbers but disappear after machine rounding.

Failure Boundary

The result can still miss deployment if hardware routines round differently, mixed precision changes intermediate states, or the theorem covers possible computation but not learning from finite data.

Connection

LiftQuant and TetraJet study low-bit approximation. Quantized diffusion follows accumulated numerical error. Softmax Turing asks what attention can compute in principle. Floating-Point Neural Networks adds the deployment caveat: in principle is not enough unless the arithmetic carries the computation.

EXEMPLAR 69

Data Market Pricing And Bayesian Truthful Valuation

Data Value As Paying For Information That Actually Helps

Problem

Data is not valuable just because there is a lot of it. Sellers can duplicate examples, hide useful data, exaggerate labels, coordinate with each other, or sell evidence that overlaps with someone else's. A pricing rule that ignores behavior may pay for noise instead of useful information.

State Space

The state is the data sellers, what each privately knows, reusable data assets, buyer value, model improvement from one more source, seller reports, payments, strategic actions, and the final market outcome.

Object

The object is a market rule for information. It has to answer three plain questions: what did this seller add that the buyer did not already have, what could the seller gain by lying, and can the same data be reused by others without being used up? The payment rule must make useful honesty more attractive than exaggeration or duplicate selling. The formal terms are mechanism, meaning the report-to-payment rule, incentive-compatible, meaning honest useful contribution is the sensible strategy, and non-rival, meaning data can be reused without being consumed.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is true extra evidence value. The market should pay for data that changes downstream usefulness, not duplicated volume or metadata that is easy to manipulate.

Allowed Movement

The rule may price, rank, screen, or combine data sources. It must account for seller incentives and for the fact that information can be copied and reused. A payment rule that ignores overlap pays twice for the same evidence; a rule that ignores strategy rewards whatever is easiest to exaggerate.

Why It Works

This works by changing the game around data contribution. If payment is tied to the real improvement a source adds after other data are considered, and if honest reporting is rewarded, sellers have less reason to distort what they provide. The relevant value is not total dataset size; it is the extra usefulness this source adds on top of what is already available. The formal term is marginal value: the added usefulness from this source after the other sources are already counted.

pay for the extra buyer value caused by a truthful useful contribution, not for raw volume

Evidence Check

Test truthful-report stability, duplicated or correlated sources, collusion, distribution shift, buyer-specific utility, and whether measured data value predicts real downstream gain after other data are included.

Failure Boundary

The market rule fails when real sellers can act outside the model, buyer utility is misspecified, data sources are secretly correlated, or payments reward a stand-in benchmark instead of buyer value.

Connection

Sequential Data Values studies conditional contribution. Common Corpus makes source history auditable. Accuracy Auctions price prediction value. VALUEFLOW exposes value aggregation. Data Market Pricing and Bayesian Truthful Valuation make the incentive layer explicit.

EXEMPLAR 70

CompSLOT, M-CBE, And Falling Trees

Interpretability As Making The Prediction Go Through Inspectable Parts

Problem

An explanation can be a caption written after the model has already decided. That is weak. In high-stakes or continually learning systems, the explanation should shape the path by which the prediction is made.

State Space

The state is the input features, learned concepts or object slots, the map from concepts to task output, expert expressions, tree or rule structure, task stream, prediction, and whether the explanation stays stable under shift.

Object

Imagine a mechanic saying the car failed because the battery was weak and the starter clicked. That explanation is useful only if those checks actually affected the diagnosis. The object is a predictor whose middle steps can be inspected before the final answer: color, shape, symptom, object, rule branch, or expert formula. What must stay the same is the path from those exposed parts to the decision. What may change is how the parts are learned, grouped, or routed. Concepts are named properties. Slots are reusable containers for parts or objects. A concept bottleneck means the model must pass through those concept variables before predicting, so the explanation is on the path to the answer instead of written afterward.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the link between the exposed middle step and the final decision. If the model says "wheel present" mattered, changing that named part should change the prediction in the claimed way. That is functional interpretability: the visible explanation actually carries part of the computation.

Allowed Movement

The model may learn concepts, combine slots, route through expert formulas, or grow tree branches. The prediction should remain expressible through the stated inspectable structure.

Why It Works

This works when the exposed middle step is not decorative. If the model says "wheel present" mattered, then changing the wheel evidence should change the output in the claimed direction. Slots help when stable objects recur across scenes. Expert formulas help when a domain expert can state how named parts should combine. Trees help when decisions need ordered rule branches. The common test is whether the visible explanation carries some of the actual work.

the explanation is real when changing the named concept changes the prediction as claimed

Evidence Check

Intervene on concepts, test concept stability under shift, measure continual retention, compare explanation sparsity with predictive loss, and show that changing a named concept changes the prediction in the expected direction.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when concepts are correlated stand-ins, humans misread exposed variables, flexible experts hide uninterpretable interactions, or the bottleneck removes information needed for the task.

Connection

MoCA routes multimodal failure to perception or reasoning. Neural Effect Search turns features into testable hypotheses. MetamerGen tests human perceptual equivalence. CompSLOT, M-CBE, and Falling Trees make interpretability a model constraint instead of a caption.

EXEMPLAR 71

Benchmarking At The Edge Of Comprehension

Evaluation As Checking Only What The Checker Can Actually See

Problem

For hard outputs, a person may not be able to verify the whole answer. They may only check a few local claims, contradictions, or consequences. A benchmark that assumes unlimited human checking is measuring an imaginary process.

State Space

The state is the model output, smaller local claims, human checker capacity, adversarial challenges, checking budget, remaining uncertainty, and benchmark score.

Object

Imagine inspecting a long bridge with only one hour. You cannot tap every bolt, so the inspection plan must say which bolts are checked and what those checks justify. The object is a checking process with limited inspectors. What must stay the same is the difference between "we checked this part" and "the whole answer is true." What may change is which subclaims are sampled, challenged, or deferred. The formal term is verification game: the model chooses what to show, the checker chooses what to inspect, and the rules decide what evidence counts.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is evidence for correctness under limited checking. The benchmark should distinguish a claim that was checked from one that merely sounded coherent.

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may sample subclaims, ask local questions, use adversarial probes, or defer uncertain judgments. It cannot turn local checks into a global truth claim unless it states the assumption connecting parts to the whole.

Why It Works

This works by matching the score to the checker's actual view. If humans can inspect only local pieces, the score should say what those local checks imply, not pretend the whole artifact was directly verified.

score correctness through checked local claims plus stated assumptions about how those checks cover the whole answer

Evidence Check

Report checker agreement, local-claim coverage, adversarially selected failures, uncertainty intervals, and examples where fluent global answers pass surface review but fail local checks.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when local checks miss global inconsistency, humans cannot judge sampled claims reliably, or the model learns to hide hard errors between inspected regions.

Connection

Consequence-Based Utility checks downstream implications. Finite Test Certification bounds finite evidence. WebDevJudge checks native artifacts. Benchmarking at the Edge of Comprehension adds the human-verifier limit: evaluation has to model what the checker can actually see.

EXEMPLAR 72

HSD And Global Resolution

Fast Decoding As Accepting Draft Words Without Changing The Model Being Sampled

Problem

A smaller draft model can suggest words quickly, and the larger target model can check them. This can speed generation. But if accepted drafts change which outputs are likely, the system is no longer sampling from the target model.

State Space

The state is the target model's next-token probabilities, draft proposals, several possible continuations, the accept-or-reject rule, leftover probability after rejection, latency budget, and deviation from target behavior.

Object

Imagine an assistant filling in a form quickly while a supervisor checks the entries. Speed is useful only if the final form is the one the supervisor would have produced under the official rule. The object is the accept-or-reject rule tying cheap draft words to the expensive target model's word odds. What must stay the same is the final chance of each possible next word. What may change is how much cheap draft work is used to get there. The formal term is coupling: a rule that keeps two generation processes linked so their outputs can be compared.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is output fidelity. The generated sequence should be distributed like direct target-model decoding, or differ only by a controlled amount.

Allowed Movement

The algorithm may propose multiple drafts, reorder checks, solve allocation subproblems, and reuse cheap model work. Every accepted token must still be corrected against the target probabilities.

Why It Works

This works when acceptance pays back every shortcut. If the small model suggests a common word too often, the checker accepts that word less often. If a draft word is rejected, the leftover chance has to be given to the right alternatives. In plain terms, the cheap path can suggest guesses, but the final lottery still has to behave like the target model's lottery. The formal term is change-of-measure correction: the accounting step that turns samples from one rule into samples that match another rule.

accept draft tokens only with a rule that keeps final output probabilities equal or close to the target model

Evidence Check

Measure speedup, acceptance rate, divergence from target decoding, rare-token behavior, long-sequence drift, multi-draft interactions, and downstream quality under fixed target-model settings.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when small local deviations accumulate, drafts miss rare important tokens, or implementation shortcuts break the acceptance rule.

Connection

Quantized diffusion tracks accumulated sampler error. Safety-Guided Flow changes paths deliberately. ThunderAgent speculates over actions. HSD and Global Resolution are the decoding version: approximate computation is allowed only when the target distribution survives.

EXEMPLAR 73

Secret Memory Lower Bounds, WGM Domain Discovery, And High-Dimensional Gaussian Mechanism

Privacy As Learning Population Facts Without Exposing One Person

Problem

Privacy is not just "add noise." The real question is what can still be discovered when individual people must remain protected, memory is limited, and the system may not even know all possible items ahead of time.

State Space

The state is user-level data, unknown item domain, private summaries, memory budget, noise rule, attacker model, utility target, and the population-level set being discovered.

Object

Imagine publishing a neighborhood count, not a list of who bought which medicine. The count should reveal a population fact, while one person's record should not be recognizable in the release. The object is the protected channel from private records to public summary. What must stay the same is the population signal supported by many people. What may change is the exact released summary because deliberate randomness is added. The formal term is randomized map: a rule that turns private records into a public summary while deliberately adding randomness.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is population signal under individual protection. The method should reveal what many users support without making one user's rare item or path identifiable.

Allowed Movement

The algorithm may keep sketches, add noise, search for missing item types, or change the noise shape. It must respect two limits at once: outsiders should not be able to tell whether one person was included, and the machine may have only limited memory while data streams by.

Why It Works

This works by limiting how much one person can move the public answer. If adding or removing one user barely changes what outsiders see, that user's record is harder to detect. But protection costs something: the same randomness and compression that hide individuals can also hide rare population facts. The papers ask where that tradeoff breaks: how much memory is unavoidable, how unknown item lists can still be found, and what noise shape preserves useful many-person patterns. The formal terms are differential privacy, meaning the one-person-change guarantee, lower bound, meaning proof that some private tasks need at least a certain amount of memory, domain discovery, meaning finding unknown items, and Gaussian mechanism, meaning adding bell-shaped noise.

release population information while making any one user's contribution hard to detect within the memory budget

Evidence Check

Report privacy settings, memory needs as the problem grows, usefulness on rare items, recovery of previously unknown items, finite-regime noise comparisons, attacks using outside information, and whether downstream artifacts leak information outside the private release rule.

Failure Boundary

The guarantee fails when deployed artifacts store data outside the private channel, side information changes the attack, or the utility target requires information privacy intentionally removed.

Connection

Gaussian Certified Unlearning defines deletion as indistinguishability. Common Corpus audits source history. Catch-22 frames watermarking as evidence testing. Secret Memory, WGM, and high-dimensional mechanisms state the resource side: privacy changes what can be discovered and stored.

EXEMPLAR 74

Learning-Augmented Paging And Matroid Algorithms

Advice And Oracle Calls As Help That Still Has A Price

Problem

An algorithm can look strong if predictions are treated as free and always helpful, or if a special query is treated as constant cost. In real use, predictions can be wrong, and a query can become expensive when the object being queried is large.

State Space

The state is the online request sequence, machine-learned advice, how much the algorithm trusts that advice, worst-case fallback, helper-query access model, query size, algorithm decision, and realized cost. The formal term is oracle: an abstract helper that answers a specific kind of query.

Object

Imagine keeping a small desk cache while someone predicts which files you will need next. Good predictions save trips. Bad predictions clutter the desk. Asking an expert helper may also cost time. The object is a guarantee that counts every kind of help: learned advice, wrong advice, special queries, and fallback behavior. What must stay the same is fallback protection: bad predictions or costly helper calls should not make performance collapse. What may change is how strongly the algorithm trusts advice or how often it asks the helper.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is fallback protection. Good predictions may improve performance, but bad predictions should not destroy worst-case protection, and oracle calls should not hide real work.

Allowed Movement

The algorithm may use learned advice or helper queries. It must budget how much it trusts advice and count each helper call in the units the deployed system actually pays.

Why It Works

This works when help is explicit rather than magical. The proof says: if predictions are good, here is the saved cost; if predictions are bad, here is the fallback loss; if the algorithm asks a helper, here is the real work behind that answer. For matroid-style problems, the helper is often checking whether a chosen set is still allowed, like asking whether adding one more course keeps a schedule valid. The formal term is matroid: a rule system for which collections of choices can coexist and how one valid collection can be exchanged for another.

use advice when it helps, fall back safely when it is wrong, and count each query at its real cost

Evidence Check

Measure performance across prediction error, trust budgets, adversarial request sequences, natural helper-query sizes, and cases where treating helper calls as free reverses the practical algorithm ranking.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when prediction errors concentrate on hard cases, the trust budget is tuned after seeing results, or the real helper has side costs missing from the model.

Connection

ParetoPO budgets tool calls. ATLAS spends evaluation questions by information. Benchmarking at the Edge models verifier limits. TileLang counts memory movement. Learning-Augmented Paging and Matroid Algorithms add the theory version: every helpful external resource needs a price in the guarantee.

EXEMPLAR 75

Distribution Transformer And Gaussian Mixture Distances

Updating Beliefs Without Flattening Them

Problem

Imagine sorting reports about an unknown machine fault. Before opening the reports, you may have several live stories: maybe the battery is weak, maybe a sensor is loose, maybe two small problems are interacting. New evidence should make some stories stronger and others weaker. It should not force all uncertainty into one neat answer just because a formula or model wants a single center.

State Space

The state is the set of possible stories before the evidence, how much trust each story gets, the new observations, the possibility that some observations are dirty or misleading, the revised set of stories after seeing the evidence, and the decision that will be made from that revised belief.

Object

The object is a belief-updating machine. Start with a table full of possible stories, each with a note saying how believable it is. New evidence should not merely pick one story. It should move the notes: this story now fits better, this one fits worse, this one is still possible but needs a wider margin. What must stay the same is the set of live possibilities and their relative support; what may change is how much trust each possibility receives after evidence. The formal terms are probability distribution, meaning the table of belief weights, and mixture of Gaussians, meaning a table whose possible stories form several rounded piles rather than one pile. Distribution Transformer learns how evidence moves the whole table. Gaussian Mixture Distances supplies the measuring tape for asking whether two such tables are close or meaningfully different.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the separate possibilities. If three explanations were genuinely different before the evidence, the update has to keep track of which explanation gained support, which lost support, and which remains uncertain. The method is not allowed to hide several live explanations inside one overconfident average.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the weight, location, spread, and interaction of the possible explanations. A story can become more likely, less likely, broader, narrower, or connected to another story. It should disappear only when the evidence really rules it out.

Why It Works

It works because the update is forced to respect two jobs at once. First, each possible story must be checked against the new evidence. Second, the stories must still be compared with each other, because evidence that weakens one story may strengthen another nearby story or may split one rough story into two sharper ones. The method helps only if it keeps that bookkeeping visible. The distance measure is the audit: it asks whether the changed belief still has the right separate piles, the right spread inside each pile, and the right amount of trust on each pile. The formal term is posterior: the revised belief after evidence.

belief before evidence -> observe data -> belief after evidence, while keeping separate possibilities and measuring how much the belief cloud moved

Evidence Check

Check whether the final belief gives honest confidence, recovers the right separate explanations, assigns sensible weights, keeps similar conclusions when a few samples are wrong, corrupted, or from the wrong source, makes good downstream decisions, and keeps two possible answers separate when the evidence does not justify choosing one.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the allowed family of belief shapes is too limited, when the labels for the separate possibilities stop meaning anything, or when the learned updater has only practiced on clean simulated evidence and then meets messier real evidence.

Connection

CreDRO and CRC keep uncertainty as sets of defensible actions. Mind-Omni builds a translation layer for hidden biological signals. Alignment-Sensitive Rates ask what signal can be found with finite evidence. Distribution Transformer and Gaussian Mixture Distances give the belief version: learning is not only choosing an answer, but moving uncertainty in a way that keeps its shape meaningful.

EXEMPLAR 76

Auxiliary MCMC, Path-Dependent DAI, And p-less

Drawing Examples Without Losing The Rules

Problem

Suppose you want a fair picture of a city by visiting neighborhoods. If you only visit the easy streets, your picture is biased. If you take shortcuts without recording them, you cannot tell whether the final map is fair. Sampling has the same problem: a generated example can look reasonable even when the route used to get it distorted what was supposed to be sampled.

State Space

The state is the thing we want to sample from, the rule for suggesting the next move, any extra helper variables, the path already taken, the rule for accepting or rejecting a move, the cutoff for which choices are allowed, the amount of exploration, and the variety of samples produced.

Object

The object is a path-aware drawing rule. Imagine drawing names from a jar, except every draw changes which names are easier to reach next. To be fair, the method cannot only show the final name. It also has to remember how that name became reachable. A one-step-at-a-time sampler is called a Markov chain. Estimating an answer from many such draws is called Monte Carlo. Auxiliary MCMC adds helper facts so hard moves become easier. Path-Dependent DAI keeps the construction route as part of the sample. p-less changes how much of the low-probability tail remains open instead of using one fixed cutoff everywhere.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the intended mix of outcomes. Exploration is helpful only if the final pile of samples still represents the target source, or if the method clearly says it is sampling from a changed source.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the route. The sampler can add helper variables, remember earlier steps, raise or lower cutoffs, or take bigger exploratory moves. But every such move has to be paid for in the accounting: the method must know how that route changes the chance of ending at the sample.

Why It Works

It works because a sample has two meanings: what it is, and how likely the method was to produce it. If two routes can land on the same example but one route is much easier, the final pile will be skewed unless that route advantage is corrected. Helper variables can open doors into regions that ordinary moves rarely reach. Path memory prevents the sampler from pretending all arrivals were equally natural. Adaptive cutoffs keep extra choices alive when the model is unsure and narrow the choice set when the next step is already clear. The plain rule is: explore freely, but keep the receipt for how each sample was made.

valid sampling = proposed example + route information + correction rule, so the final sample pile still matches the intended source

Evidence Check

Check whether samples match the intended source, how often proposed moves are accepted, how many genuinely different examples are produced, whether quality and diversity trade off cleanly, what happens when path memory is removed, and whether rare but valid outcomes remain reachable.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when helper variables add noise without reaching new regions, when the needed history is too large to track reliably, or when an adaptive cutoff removes rare words or actions that matter for reasoning, creativity, or safety.

Connection

HSD and Global Resolution accept draft tokens only when they preserve the intended generation rule. Safety-Guided Flow changes generation paths on purpose. MAPF moves many agents along paths without collisions. Auxiliary MCMC, Path-Dependent DAI, and p-less make the shared point: the route is part of the evidence, not decoration.

EXEMPLAR 77

Generative Filtering And Linear Recurrent Memory

Remembering Just Enough To Predict What Comes Next

Problem

Think of tracking a delivery truck when you only see it at a few intersections. The latest sighting is not enough; you also need what earlier sightings imply about its hidden route.
