A model in a partly observed world has the same problem. It must remember enough of the past to know what might happen next, without carrying every raw detail forever.

State Space

The state is the stream of observations, the hidden situation those observations only partly reveal, a cloud of possible hidden situations or a compact memory, the rule for how the situation changes, the rule for what can be observed, the action or prediction being made, and the updated belief after each new observation.

Object

The object is a compact memory that is good enough for the future. Picture a dispatcher tracking a truck from scattered sightings. The dispatcher does not need every camera frame forever; they need the current set of live route guesses, how likely each one is, and what the next sighting should look like under each guess. A memory with exactly that needed information is called a sufficient belief state. Generative Filtering learns how to push the live guesses forward when time passes. Linear Recurrent Memory asks when a simple running note can carry the same useful information without storing the whole past.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is uncertainty that matters later. The memory can forget the color of a truck if that never changes the route estimate, but it cannot forget a rare turn that would make the next correct action different.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the form of the memory. The method can use many particles, a learned simulator, or a simple recurrent vector. The form is flexible; the job is not. It must keep the hidden possibilities that affect future observations or decisions.

Why It Works

It works only when forgotten details truly cannot change the next decision. If two different histories lead to the same useful memory, then every future test the task cares about should treat those histories the same. That is the deep reason compression is allowed. Generative Filtering helps by carrying several hidden guesses forward instead of committing too early. Linear Recurrent Memory helps when the world has a repeatable rule that can be tracked by updating one running note at each step. The formal term is filtering: repeatedly revising hidden-state guesses as new observations arrive.

good memory at any moment means future decisions depend on the remembered belief, not on every raw observation before it

Evidence Check

Compare the compact memory to using the full history, test whether hidden states are recovered, insert delayed clues, measure whether the set of possible states collapses too early, remove memory dimensions, and test actions when the current observation alone is ambiguous.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the memory drops a rare hidden possibility, when the learned simulator changes how uncertainty should evolve, or when the recurrent memory predicts common cases well while forgetting the hidden case that changes the decision.

Connection

ThinKV and EntroKV keep only context that will matter later. Mamba Markov ICL uses recurrence to infer the source of a sequence. VideoKR keeps temporal evidence. Generative Filtering and Linear Recurrent Memory give the control version: memory matters when it carries the hidden state needed for the next move.

EXEMPLAR 78

Unpaired Causal IV And Evolutionary Selection

Using Natural Differences To Learn What Caused What

Problem

Suppose one city changes bus schedules and another changes school hours, but you do not have perfect person-by-person records. You still want to know which change affected commute time. The danger is that the cities may differ in many other ways. Causal work starts by asking which differences are useful clues and which differences are distractions.

State Space

The state is the environment where data came from, samples that may not be paired person-by-person, the exposure or treatment, the outcome, the process that selected which cases appear, hidden causes that were not measured, assumptions about which changes are allowed to act as clues, and the causal effect being estimated.

Object

The object is useful natural variation. Think of a school lottery: winning the lottery may change whether a child attends a program, while the lottery number itself should not directly change the child's later test score. That outside nudge can help separate cause from background differences. The method is useful only when the nudge changes the thing we care about and leaves the other routes alone. That kind of nudge is called an instrumental variable. In evolutionary selection, the outside nudge is repeated survival or copying: traits that keep lasting under a rule reveal what the rule favored.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the causal question: what would change if we changed the treatment itself? The environment label or selection process is useful only if it helps isolate that question, not if it merely describes where the data came from.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the environment, the mix of samples, or the selection pressure over time. But the method must say exactly what is assumed: the outside change must affect the outcome through the intended path, it must actually move the treatment, and the underlying relation must stay stable enough to compare.

Why It Works

It works by finding a difference that behaves like a clean lever. A clean lever does not prove causality by magic; it earns trust by passing three plain tests. It must actually move the possible cause. It must not directly move the outcome through a side door. And the groups being compared must be close enough that the lever is the main relevant difference. When those tests hold, rival stories are forced to give the same answer about the target effect. The formal term is identification: the data plus assumptions leave only one answer to the causal question.

causal answer is credible when the useful outside change moves the cause, leaves other paths alone, and forces compatible stories to agree

Evidence Check

Check whether the outside lever is strong, whether it might affect the outcome directly, whether the result holds in new environments, whether simulated selection recovers the known effect, whether negative controls stay unchanged, and whether the answer changes when pairing assumptions are weakened.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when environments directly affect outcomes, when selection changes the mechanism over time, when unpaired samples come from mismatched populations, or when an unmeasured cause can explain the same pattern.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence warns that different causal stories can fit the same observations. Local Covariate Selection asks which facts are needed for one fair comparison. CausalGame gathers active evidence. Unpaired Causal IV and Evolutionary Selection show the passive-data version: natural structure helps only when its assumptions are explicit enough to rule out rival stories.

EXEMPLAR 79

FlowGuard, DeceptionDecoded, And DOUBT

Checking Which Clue Made The Model Unsafe

Problem

A picture alone can be harmless. A sentence alone can be harmless. Together they can give a model enough context to produce a dangerous instruction, a false accusation, or a misleading explanation. Safety therefore cannot only ask whether the image is safe and whether the text is safe. It has to ask what happened when the clues were combined.

State Space

The state is the visual clue, the text clue, the model's combined understanding, the answer from image alone, the answer from text alone, the answer from both, uncertainty about objects, uncertainty about relationships between objects, and pressure from attacks or misinformation.

Object

The object is a map of evidence routes: which clue pushed the answer, and how much. If a label on a bottle, a nearby caption, and a user's question jointly create a risky request, the checker needs to know which part supplied which meaning. FlowGuard separates danger that came from the image, the text, their shared meaning, their interaction, or one channel drowning out the other. DeceptionDecoded asks whether the model relied on real source evidence or a cheap surface cue. DOUBT separates two kinds of uncertainty: not knowing what object is present, and knowing the objects but not knowing their relationship.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is grounded support. If an answer claims something, the system should still be able to point to the image, text, or combination that supports it. If the dangerous meaning appears only after fusion, that route should be visible before the model turns it into a fluent answer.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the response strategy. The system may combine clues, refuse, ask for clarification, warn, or route the case to another checker. But it must keep enough route information to say which clue changed the decision and whether that change was justified.

Why It Works

It works because a safety failure often starts before the final answer is written. The model first builds meaning from pieces of evidence. If changing only the image flips the answer, the image route carried the decision. If changing only the text flips it, the text route carried it. If neither route alone is enough but together they create the risky meaning, the interaction is the problem. If the answer follows layout, style, or an attack pattern instead of grounded evidence, the route is not trustworthy. The formal term is information decomposition: splitting one decision into the evidence channels that carried it.

risk is located by changing one clue at a time and asking which route actually caused the unsafe answer

Evidence Check

Run image-only, text-only, and fused tests; swap the image while keeping the text; swap the text while keeping the image; test misleading cues; separate object mistakes from relationship mistakes; and check whether the warning signal appears before the final answer becomes unsafe.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when attacks imitate normal image-text dependence, when route scores are trained only on familiar jailbreaks, or when the model uses a hidden meaning that the route map does not represent.

Connection

MoCA, VGS, Causal Route Gating, VISUALSWAP, and MINT ask whether visual evidence actually caused an answer. FlowGuard, DeceptionDecoded, and DOUBT add the safety question: did the route that caused the answer have real support, and did it stay safe under pressure?

EXEMPLAR 80

Information Flow UQ, DLMR, RFCMH, WETR, And AGREE

Keeping Receipts For Every Claim

Problem

A research assistant may answer from a retrieved article, a stored memory, a weak label, a chart, a previous summary, or its own guess. If all of those sources are dumped into one context, the assistant can start treating a guess as if it were evidence. The mistake then persists because the system has lost the receipt for where the claim came from.

State Space

The state is retrieved passages, context tokens, visual or multimodal observations, memory writes, weak labels, trust weights, generated claims, which internal layers used which evidence, and the confidence attached to the final answer.

Object

Imagine writing a report where every sentence has a margin note: "seen in photo," "quoted from article," "inferred from two sources," "remembered from earlier," "weak hint," or "unsupported." The object is that margin-note system for model claims. The arrows say which source helped create which claim. The trust level says whether that source deserves full weight, partial weight, or almost none. What must stay the same is source identity after retrieval, memory, compression, and reasoning. What may change is how the source is stored or combined. Information Flow UQ traces which context pieces actually influenced a retrieval-augmented answer. DLMR keeps multimodal observations separate from derived memory. RFCMH repairs unsupported multimodal generations by checking consistency. WETR and AGREE adjust weak labels according to how reliable they appear to be.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is source identity. A claim should still be marked as observed, retrieved, inferred, weakly labeled, corrected, or unsupported after the model compresses and uses it.

Allowed Movement

What may change is how evidence is stored and combined. The model may retrieve, summarize, compress, fuse modalities, repair a claim, or write memory. It may not erase the difference between a primary observation, a source quote, a model inference, and a weak hint.

Why It Works

It works because a claim is only as strong as the evidence still attached to it. If removing a passage does not weaken the claim, that passage was probably decorative context rather than support. If memory cannot point back to what was seen or read, it may be a stored guess. If a weak label comes from a process that often mistakes similar cases, it should count less. If an answer is repaired, the repair should identify the source that made the corrected claim valid. The formal terms are provenance, meaning source tracking, and uncertainty quantification, meaning honest confidence rather than confidence produced by smooth wording.

accept a claim when enough reliable sources point to it and their source labels survive retrieval, memory, and compression

Evidence Check

Remove retrieved passages, trace which layers used which tokens, audit memory writes, separate observation from summary, perturb weak labels, test cross-modal disagreement, and check whether confidence drops when real support is removed.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when contribution scores confuse correlation with support, when reliability weights silence rare but true evidence, or when the memory system writes unsupported conclusions into the place reserved for observations.

Connection

ThinKV asks what context must be kept. VideoKR and Visual Attribution Streaming ask which video evidence caused an answer. Common Corpus and Hubble preserve dataset source records. Information Flow UQ, DLMR, RFCMH, WETR, and AGREE connect those ideas into one rule: memory helps only when it remains evidence with receipts.

EXEMPLAR 81

DR Tulu

Teaching A Research Agent While The Checklist Improves

Problem

When a person begins a hard research task, the first checklist is usually incomplete. After reading sources, they learn that a good answer must mention a caveat, compare a missing baseline, or reject an early assumption. A research agent has the same issue: if training uses only the first checklist, it can learn to satisfy an outdated measure of quality.

State Space

The state is the user question, search path, discovered sources, temporary hypotheses, checklist criteria, agent actions, answer draft, checker feedback, and changes to the reward over time.

Object

Imagine training a junior researcher while also rewriting the grading checklist as the project teaches you what matters. One learning process improves the researcher: where to search, which sources to trust, when to revise, and when to stop. The other improves the checklist: what counts as enough evidence, which caveats are required, and which comparisons cannot be skipped. What must stay the same is the outside research obligation. What may change is the checklist when new evidence proves the old checklist was incomplete. The formal terms are policy, meaning the agent's action rule, reward, meaning the checklist score, and reinforcement learning, meaning changing behavior because of that score.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the real research obligation: answer the question using the best evidence found so far. The checklist can change, but it should move toward that obligation rather than toward whatever the current agent happens to produce easily.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the checklist, the required evidence, the subclaims, and the agent's search strategy. Those changes are allowed only when grounded in sources or verified failures, not in style preferences or self-confirming drafts.

Why It Works

It works when checklist changes are treated like claims that need evidence. The agent searches; new sources reveal that the old checklist missed a necessary caveat or comparison; the checklist is revised; then the next training step gives a sharper signal. The method fails in spirit if the checklist changes merely because the agent found an easy style that scores well. Then the agent and judge can agree with each other while both move away from the outside facts. The formal term is co-evolving rubric and policy: the checklist and action rule change together, so every checklist movement needs an audit trail.

agent improves when the score changes because new evidence demands it, not because the agent learned to flatter the score

Evidence Check

Compare fixed-checklist and evolving-checklist training, inspect each checklist revision, verify that new criteria came from external evidence, test on held-out research questions, and measure whether answers improve on facts or caveats not visible at the first checklist step.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when checklist updates reward length, source count, confidence, or self-confirming claims instead of evidence quality. It also fails when the agent learns to write material that bends future checklists in its favor.

Connection

RLVepsR studies what happens when checker feedback is noisy. Omni-Reward and JudgeBench treat reward models as imperfect measuring channels. ATLAS changes evaluation questions as uncertainty changes. DR Tulu is the research-agent version: the measuring rule may move, but the reason it moved must be visible.

EXEMPLAR 82

SceneSmith, MomaGraph, EgoTactile, And TimeRewarder

Giving A Robot The Facts That Make An Action Possible

Problem

A photo can show a cup on a table, but it may not show whether the cup is slippery, whether the handle faces the robot, whether there is room to move, or whether the last attempt actually worked. A robot fails when its data describes the scene in words or pixels but omits the facts that decide whether an action can happen.

State Space

The state is the room or scene, object identities, where objects are, how they relate, what actions each object allows, hand or body pose, touch and pressure, human demonstration video, evidence of success, and the rule for how the world changes after an action.

Object

The object is a usable action state: a working description that contains the facts needed to choose and verify a physical move. A robot does not only need the word "cup." It needs where the cup is, whether it can be grasped, what blocks the path, what touching it feels like, and what counts as success after the action. SceneSmith creates indoor scenes for practice. MomaGraph stores objects and what they can do to each other as a graph. EgoTactile adds contact facts that sight misses. TimeRewarder turns human videos into evidence about whether an action actually succeeded.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is action-relevant reality. The representation has to keep the variables that decide whether the robot can grasp, push, open, place, or avoid something, and whether the result matched the goal.

Allowed Movement

What may change is how the state is built. The pipeline can generate scenes, simplify them into object-relation graphs, add touch sensors, or infer success from video. But it must keep the physical agreement between state, action, contact, and outcome.

Why It Works

It works because the learning signal is moved closer to the physical event that matters. Pixels can show that a hand is near a handle, but they may not show whether the fingers made contact, whether the handle turned, or whether the door opened far enough. Scene generation helps only if the scenes obey the physical rules the robot will later face. Graphs help only if their links match real ways objects constrain or enable each other. Touch helps because contact is often the missing fact between a planned action and a real outcome. Video-derived reward helps when it marks success over time instead of rewarding a single nice-looking frame. The formal term is partially observed control problem: acting while important state facts are hidden.

usable robot data = scene facts + object relations + touch/contact + success evidence for state -> action -> next state

Evidence Check

Test stale graphs, slipping contact, impossible generated scenes, wrong success labels from video, transfer from simulation to real robots, cases where language planning succeeds but execution fails, and whether changing affordance or contact facts changes the policy's action.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when generated scenes break physics, graphs omit a relation that matters for the task, touch signals are delayed or miscalibrated, or video reward learns visual style instead of real success.

Connection

SAW-Bench and RoboMME measure embodied memory and reasoning. HDFlow, PACT, JoSE, and RodriNet constrain physical moves. BehaviorVLA and DreamDojo connect practice to deployment. SceneSmith, MomaGraph, EgoTactile, and TimeRewarder state the prerequisite: the policy needs a view of the world where action validity is visible.

EXEMPLAR 83

FlashVID, InfoTok, VideoFlexTok, OmniFit, And LST

Keeping The Few Moments That Can Change The Answer

Problem

Imagine skimming a long security video to answer one question: did anyone pick up the bag? Most frames show nothing changing, but one half-second matters. If the summary deletes that moment, the summary is cheap but useless. Video, speech, and mixed media give models the same problem: there is too much input to keep everything, but the small part that matters may be brief.

State Space

The state is the raw video frames, sounds or speech, text, repeated information inside each medium, the token budget, the latency budget, the kept summary, the later question, and the error caused by throwing information away.

Object

The object is a smaller record that can still answer the real question. Think of making notes from a long meeting when you do not yet know which question your boss will ask. You can drop repeated small talk, but you must keep any moment that could change an answer: who agreed, who objected, when a decision changed, or which slide contradicted the speaker. FlashVID merges repeated visual evidence. InfoTok and VideoFlexTok spend video tokens on moments that carry more answer-changing information. OmniFit decides how to divide limited tokens across media and model layers. LST turns long speech into compact internal chunks while still allowing new audio to arrive. The formal term is an approximate sufficient statistic: a short record that is not the whole stream, but still keeps almost all the evidence needed for the task.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is answer-relevant evidence. If a sound, gesture, frame, or cross-media clue would change the answer, the compressed state must keep it or keep a reliable sign of it.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the storage form. The system may merge repeated frames, drop easy parts, pool nearby tokens, patch speech into chunks, or move budget between media. Each move should be judged by the uncertainty it creates, not by token savings alone.

Why It Works

It works when the summary makes the discarded material unable to change the answer. That is the core test. If the kept record leads to the same answer as the full stream, the discarded frames or words were extra for that task. If a removed half-second would change the answer, the method did not become efficient; it threw away evidence. The hard part is that importance depends on the later question. A quiet frame can be useless for "what color is the car?" and essential for "did the driver look down before turning?" Streaming adds another burden: the model must keep unresolved clues that may only become meaningful after later speech, sound, or video arrives.

a compact stream is valid when answering from it is nearly the same as answering from the full stream under the token budget

Evidence Check

Try many token budgets, rare events, late questions, before-after order questions, conflicts between sound and image, latency-sensitive speech, and removals of tokens that the method claimed were low value.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when it cannot know what future question will be asked, when a short decisive moment looks locally ordinary, or when it assumes two media say the same thing even though only one of them contains the hidden fact.

Connection

ThinKV, STAR-KV, and EntroKV keep the text context needed later. Generative Filtering keeps a hidden belief state. DLMR keeps source labels in memory. FlashVID, InfoTok, VideoFlexTok, OmniFit, and LST extend the same principle to many media: efficiency is correct only when the kept summary can still answer the task.

EXEMPLAR 84

NextStep-1, EditVerse, LatentLM, And UALM

Editing Media While Keeping It Usable For The Next Step

Problem

Suppose you ask an editor to change a video so the car is red, then later ask them to make the same car turn left and add matching engine sound. The first edit must leave behind a usable object for the second edit. A generator that treats text, image, video, and audio as one flat string can lose the details that make later edits precise.

State Space

The state is the instruction, a shared editing language, the medium's own hidden state for image, audio, or video, the current artifact, the part to edit, the parts that must not change, the decoder that turns hidden state into media, and the context for the next instruction.

Object

Imagine editing a shared project file rather than taking a screenshot after every change. A useful media editor needs two layers. One layer says what the user wants: keep the same car, make it red, preserve the street, add matching sound. The other layer stores the artifact in a form that can actually be edited again: image layout, audio timing, video motion, or a compact media state. What must stay the same is controllable identity across steps. What may change is the requested part and the file format used inside the model. NextStep-1 treats creation and editing as predicting the next usable step. EditVerse puts text, image, and video actions into one editing sequence. LatentLM keeps a compressed working version of the media: not the final pixels or sound, but a short internal list that can be rebuilt into media and edited again. The formal name is a VAE latent vector, where an autoencoder is the compressor-and-reconstructor that teaches the model what such a reusable short list should contain. UALM links audio understanding, audio generation, text reasoning, and mixed-media reasoning through one interface.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is controllable artifact identity: the same object, layout, timing, sound structure, edit target, and enough internal state to keep editing later.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the requested part of the artifact and the format used to represent it. The model can translate between media, produce native image or audio states, or edit an existing artifact. It must separate the coordinates that should change from the facts that should survive.

Why It Works

It works when control and validity are kept separate but connected. The instruction layer carries what should change and what must stay fixed. The media layer carries what makes the artifact real enough to render or play: image positions, audio timing, waveform shape, video identity, and motion. If the model stores only the final pixels or sound, later edits have to guess what the object was. If it stores only an abstract instruction, the output may lose detail. The useful middle ground is an editable state that remembers both the user's control facts and the medium's own rules. The formal phrase is a stateful cross-modal protocol: a step-by-step rule for carrying one editable media state into the next, even when the work moves between text, image, video, and audio.

current artifact + instruction -> shared control -> next native media state, while unchanged facts stay unchanged

Evidence Check

Test repeated edits, identity preservation, image validity, audio timing, video consistency, agreement across media, reconstruction quality, and whether later instructions can still point to objects or events created earlier.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the shared code is too abstract to reconstruct details, too low-level to follow instructions, or too tangled to edit one part without corrupting another.

Connection

VibeVoice and Hibiki-Zero keep speech in sound-time form. FlashWorld and VectorWorld keep world transitions. TideGS and RadioGS keep field coordinates. OmniVerifier checks generated artifacts. NextStep-1, EditVerse, LatentLM, and UALM define the shared interface that lets those native states join one controlled workflow.

EXEMPLAR 85

EmotionThinker, EEmo-Logic, Active Mind Avatars, And MetaphorVU

Reading Social Meaning From More Than One Surface Clue

Problem

A person can say "great" and mean delight, sarcasm, resignation, or politeness. The words alone are not enough. Tone, face, body, context, culture, and what just happened can all change the meaning. A model that jumps from one word to an emotion label can be confident for the wrong reason.

State Space

The state is voice cues, face or body expression, language, metaphorical phrase, social setting, event history, cultural frame, the person's internal appraisal, intended meaning, and the response or answer.

Object

The object is the hidden situation that would make the social clues fit together. If someone says "great" after bad news, the word alone points one way, but timing, tone, facial expression, and shared context may point another way. The model is trying to infer the unseen appraisal: what the person likely thinks just happened, how they feel about it, and what response would respect that uncertainty. EmotionThinker grounds emotion reasoning in sound and context. EEmo-Logic rewards reasoning for emotion questions. Active Mind Avatars model body-based social expression. MetaphorVU tests figurative meaning as a vision-language problem rather than literal caption matching. The formal term is a latent social state: the meaning that is not directly visible, but can be inferred when words, tone, expression, history, and setting are read together.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is an explanation that fits the human situation. The model should account for the cues and support the response without reducing meaning to a single word, facial expression, or stereotype.

Allowed Movement

What may change is which cue receives weight. The model can combine sound, face, text, gesture, and context. It should keep uncertainty when cues conflict and should not replace missing cultural or situational evidence with a generic guess.

Why It Works

It works by refusing to treat a surface cue as the answer. A strained voice, a sad phrase, an ironic image, or a tense gesture is evidence, not the emotion itself. The method earns trust when it can explain why those clues belong together: what happened, what the person may believe about it, which cue is decisive, and which cues remain ambiguous. Training helps only if it rewards that chain of support, not just the final emotion label. When cues disagree, the right output may be uncertainty rather than a forced category.

infer hidden social state from cues and context, then answer while keeping uncertainty when the cues disagree

Evidence Check

Use examples where cues conflict, sarcasm changes the meaning, metaphors are perturbed, culture shifts the interpretation, sound disagrees with text, expression disagrees with context, and the same literal phrase means different things in different situations.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when benchmarks reward stereotypes, when labels force many different appraisals into one category, or when the model explains social meaning from the easiest cue instead of the decisive one.

Connection

Mind-Omni and PRISM build interfaces for human signals. WETR weights token evidence for emotion. MetamerGen tests when different images feel the same to people. EmotionThinker, EEmo-Logic, Active Mind Avatars, and MetaphorVU add the social layer: grounding can mean inferring a person's hidden situation, not only recognizing objects.

EXEMPLAR 86

JoSE, RodriNet, PACT, And HDFlow

Moving Only In Ways The Body Can Actually Do

Problem

A robot can understand "pick up the cup" and still send a command that twists the wrist the wrong way, clips through the table, or loses grip. The task idea is not enough. The action must live inside what the body and contact with the world allow.

State Space

The state is body pose, joint chain, motor commands, compact action variables, high-level subgoal, low-level motion path, trusted old behavior, correction update, contact, and the physical result after movement.

Object

The object is the smaller set of moves that this body can actually do from this situation. A robot arm cannot choose any command from a giant list. Its joints bend only certain ways, rotations have rules, contact can block movement, and balance can make a move unsafe. JoSE searches a compact set of behavior handles instead of the whole raw command list. RodriNet builds lawful rotation and body-chain structure into prediction. PACT keeps corrections close to trusted behavior. HDFlow separates the high-level goal path from the low-level motion path. The formal term is a constrained action manifold: the reachable action surface inside the much larger list of possible command numbers.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the intended physical effect. The robot should still move the world toward the goal while respecting body geometry, contact, and the range of behavior already known to be stable.

Allowed Movement

What may change is how the goal is split into smaller steps, which compact behavior handles are used, and how the learned action rule is corrected. The method may not move into commands the body cannot execute or updates that erase useful old skills.

Why It Works

It works because most raw commands are irrelevant or impossible before the task even begins. Learning from the whole command list wastes experience rediscovering body limits, rotation rules, and contact failures. Better handles remove impossible directions before learning starts, so practice is spent choosing among moves the body can plausibly execute. Separating intent from motion lets the model plan "what should happen" while a lower layer handles "which body movement can cause it." Bounding corrections matters when feedback is weak because a small-looking change in a model can create a large real-world movement after contact and leverage amplify it.

choose a move from the allowed action surface, keep the update near trusted behavior, and check that the world transition satisfies the goal

Evidence Check

Measure body-rule violations, coverage of allowed actions, subgoal correctness, recovery after perturbations, loss after correction, transfer to new bodies, and cases where the language plan is good but physical execution fails.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the allowed surface excludes a needed recovery move, when the body model is wrong, or when a small policy update creates a large state change because contact is unstable.

Connection

SceneSmith and MomaGraph build environment state. EgoTactile measures hidden contact. BehaviorVLA studies reusable embodied behavior. MAPF plans paths under constraints. JoSE, RodriNet, PACT, and HDFlow give the action-side complement: after the state is visible, movement still has to stay physically possible.

EXEMPLAR 87

MetaEmbed, TACO, COMPACT, And LiME

Putting Data In A Shape That Keeps Its Rules

Problem

A spreadsheet is not just a long sentence. Rows, columns, keys, units, and missing fields all matter. If a model flattens the table until those relationships are blurred, it may answer from nearby words instead of from the table rule. The same problem appears when compressing context or adapting a model: the convenient representation can hide the structure the task needs.

State Space

The state is the structured input, row and column keys, field types, relation edges, serialization order, kept context, compact adaptation coordinates, task error, and the facts that must survive the representation.

Object

The object is a safer way to place structured data inside the model so its rules still work. A coordinate is just where something is placed in the model's internal format, like putting each column of a spreadsheet in a place where it can still be found. The useful placements are the ones that still let the model look up a key, compare values, join records, keep a rare field, or adjust a model in a direction that changes the target behavior. MetaEmbed and TACO keep table and record operations visible. COMPACT keeps the parts of context that matter instead of treating every placement equally. LiME restricts adaptation to a small set of behavior-changing handles.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the task's rulebook: looking up the right record, comparing the right values, adding or counting the right fields, joining by keys, checking types, preserving source relations, and keeping the model changes that actually change the target behavior.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the encoding. The method may embed, serialize, compress, or adapt. It must not destroy the operations that define the problem just because the new representation is easier for the transformer to process.

Why It Works

It works when the new placement preserves the actions the task will ask for. If a question depends on joining two tables by a key, the representation must keep the key relationship, not just nearby words. If a column has units, the representation must keep the unit so numbers are compared correctly. If a model update only needs a few behavior-changing handles, the compact update space must include those handles. Efficiency helps only after irrelevant placements are removed; it hurts when useful structure is removed. The formal phrase is a structure-preserving coordinate map: a way to change the representation while keeping the operations that matter.

a representation is valid when the same lookup, join, comparison, and useful update remain possible after encoding

Evidence Check

Test row and column swaps, unit changes, key joins, aggregation, missing fields, serialization changes, context-compression budgets, and whether compact adaptation loses behavior outside the calibration set.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the chosen coordinates preserve layout but not meaning, when correlations replace keys, when compression removes rare fields, or when a small adapter hides a domain-specific direction the task needs.

Connection

GraphGlue and embedding translation preserve compatibility between representation spaces. CRAMF keeps formal symbols queryable. ThinKV keeps sufficient context. MetaEmbed, TACO, COMPACT, and LiME make the preprocessing lesson explicit: choosing coordinates is part of the reasoning, not a formatting detail.

EXEMPLAR 88

VIST3A, TideGS, And RadioGS

Making Pictures And Signals Come From One Stable Scene

Problem

A generated room can look fine from one camera angle and fall apart from another. A radio or field signal can match one sensor and fail at a nearby sensor. The issue is that each observation may be made separately, without a stable scene or field underneath it.

State Space

The state is camera position, 3D appearance, geometry, small local blobs used to represent the scene or field, position, size, opacity, signal strength, sensor view, the rendering or measurement rule, and the observed artifact.

Object

The object is a hidden scene or field that can produce many observations. A real room is not rebuilt from scratch every time you move the camera. The same table, wall, and lamp should explain all views. A radio field works similarly: a nearby sensor should see a related signal because both sensors are measuring one underlying field. VIST3A ties generated appearance to viewpoint and 3D geometry. TideGS and RadioGS use many small rounded pieces to approximate continuous scenes, fields, or signals that sensors can measure. The formal name for those rounded pieces is Gaussian primitives: small smooth blobs whose position, size, and strength can be adjusted so many blobs together approximate the scene or signal.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the identity of the underlying scene or signal across views. Changing the camera, time, or sensor should change the measurement, not invent a different object or a different physical rule.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the view, edit, rendering, or reconstruction. But when the constraint is geometric or physical, the change has to happen in the scene or field state. Moving pixels directly is valid only when those pixels are the visible result of a consistent hidden state change.

Why It Works

It works because the representation stores what caused the observations, not just the observations themselves. A scene state lets the model move the camera while keeping object identity, position, and shape fixed. A field state lets the model predict what a different sensor should measure because both sensors are tied to the same hidden field. The check has to happen before the final picture or signal is produced, because that is where the shared physical object lives. The formal phrase is a renderable native state: an internal object that can be turned into pictures, radio readings, or other measurements by a known rendering or sensing rule.

one hidden scene or field should explain many views through the rendering or sensor rule

Evidence Check

Run multi-view consistency tests, new-view rendering, field reconstruction with held-out sensors, local edit tests, occlusion cases, boundary cases, and regimes where the local bell-shaped pieces are too weak to represent the signal.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the chosen pieces cannot represent sharp breaks or long-range effects, when camera or sensor calibration is wrong, or when a plausible picture hides the fact that no single scene explains all views.

Connection

Quotient-Space Diffusion removes irrelevant symmetries. Depth Anything 3 recovers coordinate structure. FlashWorld and VectorWorld maintain transition state. VIST3A, TideGS, and RadioGS apply the same rule to media and fields: generate in the space where validity is defined.

EXEMPLAR 89

OmniVerifier, DAVE, VC-STaR, And HALO

Checking The Part Of The Artifact Where Failure Actually Lives

Problem

A written judge can say an image is fine while missing a broken hand, wrong label, or impossible layout. A self-correction loop can improve the wording while leaving the bad step unchanged. A policy update can look better in logs while making agents unstable together. The checker fails when it watches the wrong part of the system.

State Space

The state is the produced artifact, visual evidence, explanation signal, correction path, learned behavior update, many-agent state, the part shown to the verifier, the constraint being checked, and the accept or reject decision.

Object

The object is a checker aimed at the part where the failure can actually live. If the failure is a malformed image, the checker must inspect the image, not only a caption. If the failure is a false explanation, the checker must test whether the explanation still points to the same evidence when irrelevant details change. If the failure is a broken reasoning path, the repair must fix the step where the error entered. If the failure is unstable behavior, the update must be checked in the behavior state. OmniVerifier checks generated visual artifacts directly. DAVE separates stable visual explanations from artifacts caused by the model architecture. VC-STaR uses corrected solution paths for self-training. HALO limits how far a learned behavior or group of agents can be updated when a large change could make them unstable.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is satisfaction of the real constraint in the native place where it can fail: image regions, evidence maps, reasoning paths, or behavior updates, not only final text.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the artifact, explanation, correction path, or learned behavior rule. But the checker has to inspect the state component whose correctness is being claimed.

Why It Works

It works when the verifier sees the right view of the system: the part that carries the claim. A text-only judge can miss a visual defect because the defect never appears as text. A final-answer judge can miss a reasoning bug because the final sentence hides the bad step. A log-score judge can miss unstable control because the bad state appears only during interaction. The checker becomes meaningful when its view includes the variable that would make the claim false. The formal phrase is projected constraint checking: checking the right slice of a larger system instead of checking an easier surface that may hide the failure.

accept only when the checker sees the failure-bearing part and that part satisfies the claimed constraint

Evidence Check

Compare native and text-only judging, change irrelevant image features, inspect corrected paths, test whether the verifier can be gamed, repeat optimization against the checker, and measure whether accepted updates stay stable in deployment-like interactions.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the verifier becomes another weak score, when the inspected slice omits the failure variable, or when repeated optimization finds artifacts that satisfy the checker but not the user-facing constraint.

Connection

WebDevJudge and PaperBench judge native artifacts. RLVepsR treats verifier labels as noisy learning signals. Binary RAR checks factual contradictions. FlowGuard checks cross-modal risk. OmniVerifier, DAVE, VC-STaR, and HALO unify those cases: verification only means something when it sees the object that can fail.

EXEMPLAR 90

BehaviorVLA, DreamDojo, SCALE, XR-1, Latent Action Supervision, And ScaleMoE

Proving A Learned Behavior Still Works When The Setting Changes

Problem

A robot can learn to open one drawer in one simulator and still fail on a different drawer, robot arm, lighting condition, or feedback loop. A benchmark score is weak evidence if it only proves the behavior worked in the exact world where it was practiced.

State Space

The state is what the robot observes, the robot body, the hidden short name for an action, the choice of specialist controller, simulated world, real rollout, self-check signal, deployment correction, task mix, and transfer result.

Object

The object is a behavior pattern plus the conditions under which it transfers. The useful behavior is not "move motor 7 by this exact amount." It is a rule like: from this kind of visual and language situation, choose an action that causes the intended world change. BehaviorVLA seeks reusable vision-language-action behavior with less data. DreamDojo expands practice through generated worlds. SCALE uses model signals to adjust behavior during deployment. XR-1 tests whether behavior works across bodies. Latent Action Supervision checks whether the model's hidden short names for actions match real task structure: for example, whether actions grouped together really cause similar world changes. ScaleMoE routes control through specialized components, like sending grasping, navigation, or manipulation cases to different learned specialists.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the useful effect of the behavior: the same kind of state change should happen across new objects, bodies, or environments, and the system should expose when that transfer assumption is no longer justified.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the practice world, the chosen specialist, the hidden short name for an action, or the correction made during deployment. The behavior must remain tied to what the robot observes and to real physical transition evidence.

Why It Works

It works when the behavior is represented at the right level of detail. If it is too close to one robot's motors, it will not transfer to another body. If it is too abstract, it becomes a wish like "open the drawer" without the contact, timing, and force needed to make the drawer move. Generated worlds help only when they vary the same facts deployment will vary. Specialist routing helps only when specialists divide real control situations, not superficial scene styles. Hidden action codes help only if they group actions by what they do in the world. Self-correction helps only when the model's uncertainty is tied to real action risk.

transfer is credible when the same behavior causes the intended state change across tested tasks, bodies, and worlds

Evidence Check

Measure real rollouts across bodies, synthetic-to-real gaps, hidden-action ablations, expert-routing failures, distribution shifts, self-correction under uncertainty, and whether learned behaviors preserve transition effects rather than memorizing demonstrations.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when simulated practice misses contact or timing, when a hidden action code hides body-specific constraints, when expert routing follows superficial features, or when self-evaluation stays confident outside training support.

Connection

SceneSmith constructs environments. MomaGraph and EgoTactile expose affordance and contact. JoSE and RodriNet constrain action geometry. MAPF plans under path constraints. BehaviorVLA, DreamDojo, SCALE, XR-1, Latent Action Supervision, and ScaleMoE ask whether those pieces become behavior that survives deployment shift.

EXEMPLAR 91

FRABench, UFEval, And Micro-Benchmarking Reliability

Breaking A Score Into What It Actually Measured

Problem

A restaurant can have a high average rating while the food is good, the service is slow, and the allergy handling is unsafe. A model leaderboard has the same problem. One number can hide whether the model improved image quality, followed instructions, stayed safe, or only won by a tiny gap that might disappear on another set of questions.

State Space

The state is the model, the pool of test items, the separate rubric dimensions, judge outputs, scores for each aspect, item difficulty, noise from using only a finite test set, gaps between models, and the final ranking claim.

Object

The object is a measuring report with parts and error bars. A useful report does not only say "4.3 stars." It says which part was measured, how many examples supported that number, and how much the number might move if the test used another reasonable sample. FRABench and UFEval split evaluation into aspect-level scores. Micro-Benchmarking Reliability asks whether the model order survives resampling and small test-set noise. The formal phrase is a factored measurement model with finite-sample uncertainty: a score split into real parts, plus an honest statement that a limited test set can make the score wiggle.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the real capability claim. If the claim is "better at instruction following," the aggregation must not hide that under visual quality. If the claim is "model A beats model B," the report must keep uncertainty about whether that ordering is stable.

Allowed Movement

What may change is how items are weighted, grouped, sampled, or aggregated. The evaluator can combine scores, but it must keep the component scores and the doubt needed to justify the conclusion.

Why It Works

It works because ability is not one thing, and a test item is not the whole world. One image question may test visual detail. Another may test instruction following. A third may mostly test whether the judge likes verbose answers. Splitting the score shows which ability actually moved. Error bars ask a different plain question: if we drew another similar batch of test items, would the claimed gap probably remain? The formal name is a bootstrap interval: repeatedly making many pretend test sets by resampling the existing results, then seeing how much the reported score moves.

say A beats B only when the measured component gap is larger than the uncertainty from finite items and judges

Evidence Check

Report reliability for each aspect, judge agreement, uncertainty intervals, sensitivity to item count, ranking reversal rates, and examples where the average score hides a severe component failure.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the rubric leaves out the real failure type, when judges cannot separate the aspects reliably, or when the items are stable but too narrow for the deployment claim.

Connection

ATLAS spends questions where they reduce doubt most. Finite Test Certification limits what a benchmark can prove. WebDevJudge and OmniVerifier change what the evaluator can observe. FRABench, UFEval, and Micro-Benchmarking Reliability complete the measurement picture: a score must say what it measured and how much evidence supports it.

EXEMPLAR 92

Weak-Strong Verification And RLVepsR

Knowing When A Cheap Checker Is Not Enough

Problem

Imagine a factory where a quick camera check catches most defects, but a few expensive failures need a human inspector. Checking everything by hand is too slow. Trusting the cheap check everywhere is unsafe. Verification for AI has the same tradeoff, and the stakes rise when checker scores are used to train the model.

State Space

The state is the task, candidate answer, cheap checker score, strong checker result, cost of checking, confidence threshold, defer decision, model update, and whether the answer truly succeeded.

Object

The object is a checking system with known mistakes and known costs. A cheap checker is like a quick scale at the airport: useful when the bag is clearly under the limit, risky when it is right at the boundary. A stronger checker costs more, so it should be used where it can change the decision or prevent harm. Weak-Strong Verification decides when to accept, reject, or ask the stronger checker. RLVepsR studies what happens when checker labels become training rewards even though some labels are wrong. The formal phrase is a noisy measurement channel: an imperfect instrument that gives a useful signal but is not the same thing as truth.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is connection between measured success and real success. Strong checking should be spent where it changes the decision or prevents the model from learning from a bad signal.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the route: accept, reject, defer, change thresholds, or train from labels. The system must keep track of false positives, false negatives, and whether those errors become worse after the model optimizes against the checker.

Why It Works

It works when uncertainty decides who checks the answer. A cheap score far from the boundary can save cost because a stronger checker is unlikely to change the call. A cheap score near the boundary should buy stronger evidence because one wrong call can flip the outcome. For training, the question is sharper: when the checker is wrong, what direction does it push the model? If mistakes are rare and balanced, learning may still improve. If mistakes reward the wrong surface pattern, training teaches the model to exploit the checker rather than solve the task.

use the strong checker when its expected decision value is worth the cost; train only when checker feedback points toward real success

Evidence Check

Calibrate cheap scores, report defer curves, sweep the strong-checker budget, measure false positives and false negatives, test checker gaming after optimization, and compare learning, stalling, and damage under controlled noise.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when cheap-checker confidence is wrong on hard cases, when the strong checker is not actually stronger for the relevant failure, or when the trained model moves into cases where old checker calibration no longer applies.

Connection

DR Tulu changes rubrics as evidence changes. Omni-Reward and JudgeBench treat reward as measurement. Binary RAR checks factual contradictions. OmniVerifier checks native artifacts. Weak-Strong Verification and RLVepsR state the core rule: a verifier is an instrument with mistakes, costs, and feedback effects.

EXEMPLAR 93

Reward Redistribution For CVaR MDPs, UP-OCP, And ROCP

Designing For The Bad Cases That Average Scores Hide

Problem

A delivery system can be fast on average and still unacceptable if one percent of packages contain medicine that arrives dangerously late. AI decisions have the same issue. The average can look good while rare sequences, uncovered cases, or high-loss deployment states remain unsafe.

State Space

The state is the sequence of actions, reward at each step, final loss, the worst slice of outcomes, past calibration data, prediction set, online update, desired coverage level, and the downstream harm from a wrong decision.

Object

The object is a risk rule tied to action. The rule asks three plain questions: what happens in the bad slice of cases, which earlier choices helped create those bad endings, and whether the current prediction range is wide enough to act safely. Reward Redistribution for CVaR MDPs sends blame from rare bad endings back to earlier choices that helped cause them. CVaR means looking at the worst part of the loss pile instead of averaging it away. An MDP is a step-by-step decision problem where each action changes the next situation. UP-OCP controls prediction intervals as data arrives over time. ROCP-style robust or conformal prediction keeps several plausible outcomes alive so the decision can hedge instead of pretending one forecast is certain.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is safety under bad but plausible outcomes. The method should preserve coverage, tail control, or a loss bound exactly where the downstream action is sensitive.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the reward timing, the width of prediction sets, the calibration rule, or the conservative decision. The risk statement must stay tied to the real harm variable and to the order in which data arrives.

Why It Works

It works by turning uncertainty into a limit on what action is allowed. Looking at the worst cases forces training to care about harm that the average would bury. Sending blame back through the sequence helps the learner see that the bad ending may have been prepared by an early choice, not only the final step. Online coverage keeps asking whether the prediction range still contains the truth often enough as new cases arrive. Robust prediction keeps multiple plausible futures alive when evidence is weak. Conformal prediction is the formal name for using past calibration errors to choose ranges that should contain the true answer often enough; the plain goal is to make the range wide when the evidence is shaky and narrow when the evidence is reliable.

choose the action that controls worst-case or coverage loss, not only the average loss

Evidence Check

Report tail-loss curves, coverage over time, calibration under shift, interval width versus usefulness, stress cases near decision boundaries, and whether redistributed reward points to the early actions that actually create tail harm.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the bad event is defined wrong, when prediction sets leave out the real shift, when conservative sets are too broad to act on, or when redistributed rewards point to proxies rather than causes of tail improvement.

Connection

Rare Event Analysis estimates low-probability harmful mass. Conformal Policy Control limits policy improvement using finite evidence. CreDRO, CRC, and MIRA make uncertainty decision-shaped. CVaR MDPs, UP-OCP, and ROCP give the operational form: uncertainty matters when it changes the action safely.

EXEMPLAR 94

LR Decay, BLL-Loss, LALP, Sequential Data Values, And NASH

Valuing Data By What It Can Still Teach Right Now

Problem

A math hint can be useful before a student understands the topic and useless after they already solved it. A duplicate worksheet adds little after the first one. Training data works the same way: an example is not valuable in isolation. Its value depends on the model's current state and what other examples have already taught.

State Space

The state is the current model, optimizer memory, learning rate, the direction an example would push the model, reasoning trace, local step validity, selected data subset, utility pieces, redundancy, and the extra contribution of adding one more item.

Object

The object is credit for useful change along a training path. An example earns credit only if the current model can still learn something from it that the selected data has not already taught. Timing matters: the same example can be powerful early, redundant later, or harmful if it pushes the model in the wrong direction. LR Decay studies how timing and step size change data value. BLL-Loss and LALP give credit to reasoning traces by local validity or outcome. Sequential Data Values measures what an item adds after previous selections. NASH breaks utility into parts so contribution estimates are less noisy. The formal term is marginal contribution: the extra value one more item adds after the already-chosen items have done their teaching.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful model movement. Data should be valued by the change it can still cause now and whether that change supports the intended capability.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the example weight, timing, trace reward, or selected set. The credit must stay tied to how the model moves, not just metadata, source prestige, or copying a teacher's exact words.

Why It Works

It works because an example affects training only through the update it causes now. A high learning rate can make the same example powerful; a late small step can make it nearly inert. An example can push toward the target, away from it, or sideways into a lesson already covered by other data. For reasoning traces, the important question is not whether the wording matches a reference exactly. It is whether each step is allowed by the previous step and whether the whole path still reaches the right conclusion. Data value is therefore a moving property of the model, the timing, and the surrounding selected set.

data value depends on the current model, step size, useful update direction, and what the selected set already covers

Evidence Check

Move the same data to different training times, measure whether updates point toward the target, test alternate valid traces, audit local reasoning credit, report redundancy and complementarity, and remove utility components to see which ones mattered.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when useful movement is measured on a narrow validation slice, when local trace credit rewards plausible but globally wrong steps, or when value changes after the model moves but the selector keeps using stale estimates.

Connection

Midtraining Bridges times distribution movement. DiReCT chooses data by directions that help and do not damage. OPUS and HOBIT select examples by optimizer effect. Common Corpus keeps source records. LR Decay, BLL-Loss, LALP, Sequential Data Values, and NASH explain the data principle underneath: data matters through the movement it produces at that moment.

EXEMPLAR 95

BRCD And Production Agent Studies

Finding The Fix, Not Just The Loudest Symptom

Problem

When a website goes down, many dashboards turn red at once. The service with the biggest spike may only be reacting to another service that failed first. Operators need to know which change would actually stop the failure, even when logs are incomplete.

State Space

The state is the service dependency map, partial causal structure, traces before failure, observed failure, anomaly scores, candidate causes, possible directed cause stories, repair outcomes, and risk that the incident comes back.

Object

The object is a ranked list of repair points with remaining doubt attached. Each candidate repair is judged by a plain counter-question: if we fixed this point, how likely is the failure to stop? BRCD keeps several possible cause maps that fit the logs instead of betting everything on one story, then asks which repair would most change the failure across those maps. Production agent studies add workflow facts: tool calls, handoffs, monitors, hidden state, and when to escalate to a human. The formal term is posterior: the uncertainty you have after the traces have updated your earlier guesses.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is repair value. A diagnosis should keep uncertainty over possible mechanisms while identifying actions that would reduce the failure probability.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the ranking, the sampled cause story, the aggregation across stories, or the escalation path. The method should not collapse uncertainty into one confident story unless the traces actually distinguish that story.

Why It Works

It works because cause is about what would change after a fix, not which dashboard shouted loudest. Symptoms say where the system looked abnormal. Causes say where a forced repair would alter the bad outcome. Keeping several possible cause maps is important when logs are incomplete, because the same symptom pattern can come from different mechanisms. A good diagnosis therefore ranks actions, not stories: it asks which next check or repair would most reduce the chance of the incident continuing or returning.

rank a suspected cause by how likely the failure would change if that point were fixed, given traces and possible mechanisms

Evidence Check

Compare anomaly rank to successful fixes, audit missing trace variables, test whether proposed fixes reduce recurrence, report uncertainty over alternative causes, and measure whether agent diagnoses choose useful next checks rather than plausible stories.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the true cause is not traced, when possible cause graphs share the same symptoms but imply different repairs, or when no intervention feedback arrives to correct the diagnosis.

Connection

CausalGame chooses evidence to identify mechanisms. Local Covariate Selection targets one effect. Distributional Equivalence names what passive data cannot distinguish. BRCD and production studies bring those ideas to operations: the output that matters is the ranked repair action.

EXEMPLAR 96

DISCO, Fair Posthoc Control, And Accuracy Auctions

Treating Predictions As Rules That Affect People

Problem

A loan model is not just guessing a label. Its output changes who gets money, who pays more, and who has a chance to improve. A high average accuracy can still hide shortcuts, unfair group effects, or the fact that being correct matters more for some users than others.

State Space

The state is input features, shortcut variables, protected or social groups, assumptions about what would happen under a different decision, a control knob after training, private value of correctness, prediction, decision rule, and welfare or harm.

Object

The object is a decision rule with constraints. It should say which evidence is allowed to influence the decision, which shortcut routes are forbidden, how different groups are affected, and whose benefit is counted. DISCO discourages shortcut paths by checking whether unwanted variables still carry information after allowed facts are held fixed. Fair Posthoc Control keeps a visible fairness-accuracy tradeoff after training instead of hiding it in one score. Accuracy Auctions treat correctness as worth different amounts to different people and design reporting rules around truthful value. The formal term policy simply means the rule that chooses what action to take.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is legitimate decision value: useful prediction under stated limits about which variables may drive the decision, how groups are affected, and whose accuracy matters most.

Allowed Movement

What may change is weighting, after-training adjustment, auction design, penalties for unwanted shortcuts, or choosing not to decide on uncertain cases. The system must expose the tradeoff instead of hiding social constraints inside one average score.

Why It Works

It works by treating the model output as an allocation rule. The prediction changes what people receive, so the route from evidence to decision matters. Removing a shortcut blocks an unwanted route, such as a protected fact leaking through a proxy. A fairness tradeoff curve shows what is gained and lost when the decision rule is moved toward one fairness goal. An auction uses people's private values so accuracy is spent where being correct matters most, but only if the reporting rule makes honesty the best move. The formal phrase is a constrained decision rule: an action rule that is allowed to be useful only while obeying stated causal, fairness, and incentive limits.

choose the decision rule that creates value while obeying shortcut, fairness, incentive, and uncertainty constraints

Evidence Check

Run shortcut interventions, sweep subgroup tradeoffs, test assumptions about what would happen under protected changes, check truthful reporting, compare welfare, and find cases where average accuracy rises while constrained value falls.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when shortcut variables are hidden, fairness variables are mismeasured, people act strategically outside the mechanism, or post-training controls cannot repair representation choices made during training.

Connection

VALUEFLOW exposes value aggregation. SafeDPO exposes helpfulness-safety frontiers. Fair Causal Bandits constrain exploration. Data Market Pricing handles strategic information. DISCO, Fair Posthoc Control, and Accuracy Auctions add the decision-rule layer: model outputs have social consequences once they allocate decisions.

EXEMPLAR 97

Causal Route Gating, Divergent Interventions, And Activation Oracles

Changing A Model Internally Without Creating A Fake Situation

Problem

If you test a car by replacing one part with a random object, the car's failure may tell you nothing about how the original part worked. Internal model tests have the same danger. Changing hidden activations can reveal a causal route, or it can create an unnatural state the model would never use on its own.

State Space

The state is the hidden activation vector, attention route, visual or text evidence path, normal range of activations, internal patch, question asked to an oracle probe, output change, and distance from states the model naturally visits.

Object

Imagine testing a radio by turning one knob while leaving the rest of the radio assembled. If you rip out half the circuit, the result may not tell you what the knob normally did. The object is an internal cause test that changes one suspected route while keeping the model close to a normal working state. What must stay the same is a believable computation. What may change is the route, gate, patch, or probe being tested. Causal Route Gating estimates how much the answer came through the visual route or the text-prior route, then suppresses paths that rely too much on prior text habits. Divergent Interventions warns when a patch creates an unnatural hidden state. Activation Oracles expose information stored in hidden activations through probes that can be queried in language. The formal phrase is on-manifold: the changed internal state still looks like the kind of state the model naturally uses, rather than a broken artificial condition.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is a valid answer to the cause question. If the experiment claims "this route mattered," the output change should come from that route changing, not from the model being shoved into a broken internal condition.

Allowed Movement

What may change is a patch, gate, steering direction, or internal query. The experiment must stay local enough that the rest of the model is still doing its ordinary job, and close enough to natural hidden states that the conclusion remains about real computation.

Why It Works

It works when the internal change is close to a clean experiment. A clean experiment changes one suspected route while leaving the rest of the machine in a believable condition. If blocking a text-prior route reduces hallucination while visual evidence remains available, that route was actionable. If an oracle can recover hidden information, the state really carried that signal. But the result is credible only when the patched state is still one the model could reasonably occupy and the output change is specific to the route being tested. Otherwise the experiment may only prove that broken internal states produce strange outputs.

an internal effect is credible when the output changes after a local patch and the patched state remains near normal model states

Evidence Check

Measure distance from natural activations, route specificity, patch locality, visual-text swaps, oracle calibration, evasion under pressure, and whether interventions transfer across prompts without unnatural artifacts.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when patches create unnatural activations, when probes become scores the model learns to game instead of honest checks, when route estimates are only correlated with the cause, or when oracle answers expose information without enabling reliable control.

Connection

MINT and VISUALSWAP ask whether visual evidence causes outputs. Steer Like the LLM turns prompts into activation directions. FlowGuard checks cross-modal safety. Causal Route Gating, Divergent Interventions, and Activation Oracles name the internal standard: interventions must stay near the model's own computation.

EXEMPLAR 98

Jacobian Spectra, BBP Transitions, And FTRL Lower Bounds

Seeing Which Learning Directions Are Actually Reachable

Problem

A radio signal can be real but too faint to separate from static. A route can exist on a map but take too long to reach. Learning has the same limits. A model may be able to represent the answer in principle, while the signal is buried in noise or the update path is too slow to reach the useful state.

State Space

The state is the pattern of strong and weak directions in the representation, local sensitivity of the model, planted signal, noise cloud, optimizer path, game shape, missed-opportunity score, distance from the current state to the target state, and the set of possible stable outcomes.

Object

Imagine tuning a radio. Some directions on the dial make the station clearer; other directions only change the static. The object is the learning direction that the algorithm can actually see and follow. A local sensitivity table asks: if this input or parameter changes a little, which outputs change a lot and which barely move? What must stay the same is recoverable progress with finite data and time. What may change is the representation, optimizer, or game rule being studied. The formal names come after that plain object. A Jacobian is the local sensitivity table. Spectra are the ordered list of strong and weak response directions. A BBP transition is the point where a hidden signal becomes strong enough to stand apart from random noise. An FTRL lower bound is a proof that one learning rule can look good by average mistake count while its current choice still stays far from the stable outcome.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is recoverable progress: a signal, direction, or path that the algorithm can follow with finite data and time.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the representation, optimizer, or game assumption. The theory must keep separate two claims: "a solution exists" and "this learning process can actually reach it."

Why It Works

It works because learning is directional. A signal that sticks out beyond the noise can become a feature. A signal buried inside the noise may be real but unusable with the available data and update rule. A network that is sensitive along one direction can learn changes there more easily than along a direction it barely reacts to. In games, updates can reduce average regret, meaning the running average of missed opportunity improves, while the actual current play remains far from the desired stable behavior. The formal term phase transition means a sharp change from hidden signal to visible signal as signal strength crosses a threshold.

progress needs a signal direction above noise and an update path that gets closer to the target in usable time

Evidence Check

Track strong and weak directions, emergence of outlier signal, noise thresholds, whether updates move with the recovered directions, distance of the current choice to the stable outcome, and hard games where regret improves but practical convergence does not.

Failure Boundary

The result fails as guidance when the random-noise or game model does not match training, when visible directions are not task-relevant, or when average-regret guarantees are mistaken for a guarantee about the final current behavior.

Connection

Alignment-Sensitive Minimax Rates studies target signal in directions the model has learned clearly. Single-Head Attention studies recovery of repeated signal directions from noise. Adam Degeneracy changes the distance rule used by the update method. Language Generation in the Limit separates possibility from reachable evidence. Jacobian spectra, BBP transitions, and FTRL bounds add the same caution from another angle: capability is the part of structure that learning can actually reach.

EXEMPLAR 99

CounselBench, Seizure-Semiology-Suite, And MADQA

Checking The Skill Experts Actually Care About

Problem

A counseling answer can sound warm while missing a risk boundary. A medical video answer can describe movement while missing the event a clinician cares about. A document answer can be wrong because the needed evidence was never retrieved, not because the final reasoning was bad. In expert domains, fluency is often the wrong thing to measure.

State Space

The state is the domain artifact, expert checklist, safety boundary, time-based evidence, document evidence, human baseline, answer with ideal access, agent search path, retrieved support, and final score.

Object

The object is a score broken into the parts an expert would actually inspect. A counseling answer can be kind in tone and still miss the safety move. A seizure-video answer can describe motion and still miss the medically important moment. A document answer can reason well from the wrong source because the right source was never found. CounselBench, Seizure-Semiology-Suite, and MADQA split the score so each hidden failure remains visible. The formal phrase is expert measurement decomposition: splitting the score by the expert-visible pieces instead of hiding safety, timing, evidence access, and interpretation inside one average.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is domain-valid support. The evaluation should preserve the difference between an answer that merely sounds right and one supported by the right expert rule, time window, or document evidence.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the scoring setup: human experts, ideal evidence access, separate scores, or time labels. The benchmark must keep the domain failure visible instead of reducing every miss to one generic accuracy number.

Why It Works

It works because expert tasks have rules that fluent text can hide.
