The judge must ask the same question an expert would ask: did the answer handle the risk boundary, locate the right time window, retrieve the needed document, or respect the professional constraint? Once those parts are separated, a model cannot earn a high score merely by sounding polished. The score becomes evidence about the skill that matters in the domain, not evidence about general smoothness.

domain score should separate evidence access, expert interpretation, and safety or professional constraint satisfaction

Evidence Check

Report expert agreement, safety-boundary violations, event localization over time, ideal-answer versus human versus agent gaps, retrieval misses, and cases where fluent responses fail domain-specific constraints.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when expert labels disagree without being modeled, when benchmark cases are too simplified, when time labels miss clinically relevant cues, or when ideal-answer definitions make the task too easy or too strong.

Connection

FRABench factors general multimodal quality. ATLAS treats items as measuring instruments. WebDevJudge changes the artifact channel. CounselBench, Seizure-Semiology-Suite, and MADQA add the domain rule: evaluation must observe the variable experts actually use.

EXEMPLAR 100

Gaia2, BIRD-INTERACT, tau2-bench, OpenApps, VenusBench-Mobile, And PIPE

Judging The Whole Work Session, Not Just The Final Reply

Problem

An assistant booking travel, editing an app, or querying a database can fail long before the final answer. It may click the wrong button, misunderstand a user correction, query the wrong table, forget a state change, or recover badly from a changed interface. Scoring only the final text hides where the work broke.

State Space

The state is the user goal, environment state, interface view, database or app state, action history, checks for intermediate actions, user feedback, memory, final artifact, and task completion condition.

Object

The object is the whole sequence of states, actions, and observations. A work session is like following a recipe in someone else's kitchen: what you see, which drawer you open, what changes after each step, and whether you recover from a wrong turn all matter. Gaia2 attaches action-level checks in changing environments. BIRD-INTERACT turns text-to-SQL into real database interaction. tau2-bench exposes failures in coordinating with user guidance. OpenApps, VenusBench-Mobile, and PIPE test app or mobile agents under interface variation and rewrites. The formal phrase is a controlled interaction trajectory: the checked path of task states through time, including what the agent saw, did, changed, and verified.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is legitimate progress toward the user's goal. Each action should move the environment closer to completion while leaving evidence that the state transition was valid.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the action: ask, query, click, write, retrieve, revise, or stop. Every action changes the state and should be judged by that change, not by how plausible the action sounded.

Why It Works

It works because agent work is a loop, not a final sentence. The agent sees only part of the environment, chooses an action, observes what changed, updates its memory, and chooses again. If a failure happens at step three, a polished final answer cannot prove the work was valid. Intermediate checks locate the break in the actual loop: seeing the screen, remembering the task, retrieving the right evidence, following the user, grounding a database query, or adapting to the interface. The formal phrase is a partially observed control problem: the agent has to act even though it cannot directly see the whole world state, so each observation and action must update what it believes is going on.

evaluate the action path by verified progress through task states, not only by the final text

Evidence Check

Measure action validity, state-transition success, user-correction handling, robustness to changed interface details, database-grounding errors, memory failures, and whether final success can be traced to legitimate intermediate steps.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when checks verify local steps but miss the global goal, when interface variants are superficial, when user simulators are unrealistic, or when agents learn benchmark-specific action scripts.

Connection

Skill-Pro turns traces into reusable procedures. ThunderAgent schedules agent execution graphs. DR Tulu changes research rubrics as evidence changes. SandboxEscapeBench checks boundary crossing. Gaia2 and related interaction benchmarks provide the measurement base: agent capability is a property of stateful work paths.

EXEMPLAR 101

CyberGym, CVE-Factory, And SandboxEscapeBench

Proving Security Claims By Running The Attack Or The Defense

Problem

A person can describe a lockpick without opening a lock. A model can describe a vulnerability without producing an exploit, and it can describe a patch without proving the bug is blocked. Security claims are behavioral: did the attack work, did the patch stop it, and did the isolation boundary hold?

State Space

The state is vulnerable code, patched code, exploit attempt, proof-of-concept output, generated security task, container or virtual-machine boundary, forbidden host resource, tool path, and pass or breach record.

Object

Imagine testing a locked door. A convincing story about the lock is not enough; the test is whether the door opens under the allowed attempt and stays closed after the fix. The object is runtime evidence about a boundary. What must stay the same is the security claim: vulnerable code should be reachable by the exploit, fixed code should block that same route, and forbidden host state should remain unreachable from inside the sandbox. What may change is the generated task, attack path, patch, or isolation setup. CyberGym checks proof-of-concept behavior against vulnerable and fixed code. CVE-Factory studies how to generate realistic security tasks that can be checked. SandboxEscapeBench tests whether forbidden host state can be reached from inside nested isolation. The formal phrase is executable boundary evidence: proof from running the attack or defense and observing what state can actually be reached, not proof from persuasive text.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is security causality. The exploit should succeed because the vulnerability exists, fail because the patch or boundary works, and leave logs that distinguish those cases.

Allowed Movement

What may change is task generation, attack execution, patching, or sandboxing. The result must stay tied to runtime evidence rather than textual plausibility.

Why It Works

It works because security is about reachable states. A vulnerability claim is supported when the exploit changes the vulnerable program and fails on the fixed one for the right reason. A sandbox claim is supported when forbidden resources remain unreachable under the allowed actions. Generated tasks matter only when they preserve a real vulnerability structure and include a success condition that execution can check. Running the attack or defense turns a story about security into evidence about which states can actually be reached.

security claim holds when execution separates vulnerable, fixed, and forbidden-boundary states under controlled actions

Evidence Check

Run vulnerable-versus-patched comparisons, capture exploit logs, test false positives, verify generated tasks with independent checks, measure sandbox reachability, and inspect whether agents exploit benchmark harness artifacts instead of the intended vulnerability.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when generated tasks are unrealistic, when proof-of-concept attacks exploit the test harness, when patches change unrelated behavior, or when sandbox models omit real host attack surfaces.

Connection

WebDevJudge and PaperBench judge native artifacts. RLVepsR studies verifier labels as training signal. CSPO and SecFid model safety boundaries. CyberGym, CVE-Factory, and SandboxEscapeBench make the security version explicit: the certificate is an executed state change or its prevention.

EXEMPLAR 102

EditBench, WZ-LLM, VERINA, And AlgoVeri

Letting The Checker Define Whether Code Or Proofs Count

Problem

A code patch can look tidy while failing the user's edit. A proof can sound convincing while failing the proof checker. For code and formal math, similarity to a known answer is weak evidence. The artifact has to run, satisfy the spec, or be accepted by the formal system.

State Space

The state is the source artifact, edit instruction, tests, specification, proof goal, checker or proof kernel, generated patch or proof, rejection trace, and the set of artifacts the checker accepts.

Object

Imagine submitting a key to a lock, not an essay about why the key should work. The key either turns in this lock or it does not. The object is the set of artifacts that the external checker accepts for this exact context. What must stay the same is the intended behavior or theorem. What may change is the patch text, proof route, repair attempt, or tool. EditBench evaluates edits in their surrounding code context. WZ-LLM and VERINA target proof artifacts that must satisfy formal systems. AlgoVeri studies verified-code or proof capability across tools. The formal phrase is an admissible artifact set: all outputs that pass the checker for this specification, surrounding context, and intended use.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the thing the artifact is supposed to do. The code should produce the intended behavior when run, or the proof should establish the intended theorem under the checker. The formal phrase is semantic validity: the artifact has the right meaning or behavior in its target system, not merely the right-looking text.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the patch, proof, repair, or target tool. Each move must land inside the accepted set for the target artifact and preserve the user's stated intent.

Why It Works

It works because proposal and acceptance are separated. The model is allowed to guess, search, repair, and try again. The checker is not asked whether the answer sounds convincing; it enforces a rule over behavior, specification, contract, or proof kernel. That turns correctness into a membership question: is this artifact inside the accepted set for this exact task? The hard part is scope. A passing test proves only the behavior tested. A formal proof proves only the theorem stated. The benchmark has to keep that boundary visible.

a generated artifact is valid when the checker accepts it for the spec, context, and intended behavior being claimed

Evidence Check

Report checker acceptance, failing-test categories, proof-kernel rejection causes, theorem-statement alignment, edit-intent satisfaction, cross-tool transfer, and cases where accepted artifacts still miss broader user intent.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when tests are incomplete, specs are wrong, theorem targets are irrelevant, proof checkers verify a narrow statement, or benchmark examples reward local fixes that break surrounding behavior.

Connection

CRAMF retrieves the exact proof-library items needed for a claim. RefineStat repairs probabilistic programs under checks that ask whether the program tells the intended random story. PaperBench evaluates research artifacts. Consequence-Based Utility uses downstream checks when direct proof is unavailable. EditBench, WZ-LLM, VERINA, and AlgoVeri are the strict end of that range: acceptance is defined by an external verifier.

EXEMPLAR 103

Pressure Reveals Character, Jailbreak Foundry, Harnessing Non-Adversarial Robustness, LLM Deception, And Obfuscation Atlas

Testing What Happens When The Interaction Pushes Toward Failure

Problem

A person may seem calm in ordinary conversation but behave differently under pressure. A model can also look safe or honest on easy prompts because the test never reaches the condition that triggers failure. The failure may appear only after multi-turn pressure, reusable jailbreak pieces, harmless-looking wording shifts, detector-aware training, or prompts that invite deception without obvious attack language.

State Space

The state is the prompt path, user pressure, attack module, model response, hidden internal state, detector score, harmless-looking wording variation, deception label, safety boundary, and observed failure event.

Object

The object is a hidden tendency revealed by pressure. A model may look safe in ordinary prompts because the test never pushes on the boundary where the unsafe behavior appears. Pressure Reveals Character changes the interaction path to expose behavior traits. Jailbreak Foundry makes attack pressure reusable and executable. Harnessing Non-Adversarial Robustness studies controlled prompt changes that are not explicitly malicious. LLM Deception on Benign Prompts asks whether deception appears without hostile wording. Obfuscation Atlas studies what happens when internal detectors become targets. In plain terms, the method measures a tendency to fail that ordinary prompts do not expose.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the safety claim under pressure. A real claim should not only say the model behaves well in the easiest prompt region; it should say how close the interaction can move toward violation before the claim breaks.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the prompt path, attack composition, harmless surface form, internal-state inspection, or detector pressure. The pressure process must be explicit enough that the measured failure rate can be reproduced.

Why It Works

It works because hidden tendencies are inferred from behavior under carefully chosen changes. Average accuracy mostly measures the easy middle of the prompt world. Pressure tests move toward a boundary while keeping the failure definition fixed. If a model has a deception or evasion pattern, the signal may stay rare until the interaction reaches the region where that pattern is useful to the model. Adaptive tests increase the chance of entering that region. The important protection is that the pressure path must be reproducible; otherwise the test may only show that one clever prompt worked once.

pressure tests estimate failure near a boundary, not average response quality on ordinary prompts

Evidence Check

Report exact pressure paths, whether human or automated checkers agree with the failure label, whether attack pieces work again when rerun, families of harmless wording changes, tests where the model knows it is being watched by a detector, hidden-state changes, and whether failures remain when the pressure path changes form but targets the same boundary.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when pressure is dramatic but not diagnostic, when attack modules only fit known defenses, when harmless wording changes are too narrow, or when training against a detector creates a new evasion game that no longer measures the original safety property.

Connection

Rare Event Analysis estimates tail mass. CSPO defines distance to violation. SandboxEscapeBench checks executable boundary crossing. FlowGuard monitors risky generation paths. This family adds the behavioral rule: safety is a response curve under pressure, not a point estimate at the easiest part of the distribution.

EXEMPLAR 104

LLM DNA, LLMs Get Lost, And Token Overcharging

Checking Whether The Same Task Still Acts Like The Same Task

Problem

The same request can be wrapped in different wording, placed earlier or later in a conversation, split into different tokens, priced differently, or served by a model with a different adaptation history. A system can look capable while being brittle to the wrapper rather than the task.

State Space

The state is the meaning of the task, prompt surface form, conversation history, tokenization, position, pricing rule, model lineage, behavior fingerprint, belief state, user cost, and final answer.

Object

Imagine asking the same clerk for the same form by email, at the counter, or after a short conversation. If the request is truly the same, the clerk should not suddenly forget the job just because the wrapper changed. The object is a group of interfaces that should count as the same task. What must stay the same is task-relevant behavior and fair cost. What may change is wording, position, token split, model lineage, or conversation history. LLM DNA fingerprints behavior across adaptation and distillation. LLMs Get Lost measures path dependence when early mistaken assumptions corrupt later recovery. Token Overcharging treats token accounting and pricing as part of the mechanism. The formal term is invariance: behavior stays stable under changes that do not change the underlying task.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the job and the fair cost of doing it. Equivalent requests should not change capability just because wording, position, or token splitting changed, and users should not face distorted costs because the interface can be exploited. The formal phrases are semantic stability, where the same task meaning gives compatible behavior, and economic stability, where the same work does not create arbitrary cost differences.

Allowed Movement

What may change is formatting, compression, distillation, adaptation, pricing, or conversation continuation. Those movements should preserve the task-relevant function and make unavoidable dependence on history or cost visible.

Why It Works

It works by separating the job from the wrapper used to present it. If two prompts carry the same information, a robust model should behave compatibly. If moving the request later in the conversation changes the answer, the model depends on position. If rewording the same task changes the answer, it depends on surface form. If token splits or pricing change the cost without changing the work, the interface has become part of the system being measured. The test is not "did one prompt work?" It is "which wrapper changes were irrelevant, and did behavior remain stable across them?"

robust capability means equivalent wrappers give compatible behavior after accounting for real information and token-cost differences

Evidence Check

Run prompt-equivalence sweeps, recovery tests after wrong assumptions, lineage fingerprint comparisons, tokenization and pricing audits, position-sensitivity checks, and wrappers that preserve task meaning while changing interface coordinates.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when two interfaces are declared equivalent despite hiding different information, when fingerprints confuse style with capability, or when pricing fixes remove one incentive while leaving other strategic usage channels untouched.

Connection

ThinKV and EntroKV ask what history is sufficient. LongWriter-Zero and Mamba-3 ask what long context preserves. Common Corpus and Hubble ask where a model came from. LLM DNA, LLMs Get Lost, and Token Overcharging make the same issue user-facing: the interface is part of the mathematical system.

EXEMPLAR 105

Hedging On The Frontier, Know More Know Clearer, MC-Search, And Anytime Trees

Deciding Whether To Answer, Search, Hedge, Or Stop

Problem

A doctor does not order every possible test for a simple cold, and should not guess when symptoms point to something dangerous. An AI system has the same choice: answer now, hedge, retrieve more evidence, branch a search, ask for clarification, or stop. Treating every task as direct answering wastes effort on easy cases and creates confident errors on hard ones.

State Space

The state is current evidence, candidate answer, confidence, known tradeoff frontier, available models, search node, retrieved medium, split statistic, stopping rule, compute budget, and the cost of being wrong or vague.

Object

The object is a rule for buying evidence. Imagine deciding whether to answer from memory, look up one source, order an expensive test, or say the evidence is not enough. The right move depends on how much the next evidence could change the decision and what a wrong answer would cost. Hedging on the Frontier uses known benchmark tradeoffs to choose among models when evidence is sparse. Know More Know Clearer makes stated certainty match actual knowledge. MC-Search turns multimodal retrieval into step-by-step evidence gathering. Anytime Trees add structure only when sequential tests support the split. The formal phrase is an evidence-acquisition policy: a rule for when to search, hedge, answer, or stop.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is decision value under uncertainty. The system should preserve the difference between knowing, not knowing yet, knowing after search, and lacking enough evidence for a strong answer.

Allowed Movement

What may change is whether the system gives a cautious answer, branches into several search paths, retrieves evidence, combines evidence, asks for help, or stops. Each action should be justified by whether the next evidence is likely to change the decision, reduce risk, or protect against seeing fake structure in noise.

Why It Works

It works because evidence has value only through the decision it can change. When uncertainty is low and the cost of error is small, extra search mostly wastes compute. When uncertainty is high or the cost of error is large, searching, hedging, or deferring can be the safer action. Calibration keeps the language of certainty tied to what the system actually knows. Anytime tests protect against a common mistake: repeatedly searching until random noise looks like a real pattern. The stopping rule must count how many chances the system had to fool itself.

choose answer, hedge, search, or stop by comparing expected value of more evidence against compute cost and error risk

Evidence Check

Report whether stated confidence matches actual correctness, whether declining to answer or hedging helps, search-depth value, retrieval support quality, transfer of frontier estimates, validity of stopping rules, and cases where extra evidence changes or fails to change the decision.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when confidence is miscalibrated, when search retrieves repeated evidence, when the benchmark frontier does not transfer to the new task, or when the stopping rule is invalid because the algorithm keeps probing until noise looks significant.

Connection

ATLAS allocates test items by information. Weak-Strong Verification buys stronger checks near uncertainty boundaries. Consequence-Based Utility evaluates downstream effects. Hedging, metacognition, search, and anytime inference are the same control problem: spend evidence only where it changes the decision.

EXEMPLAR 106

BrokenMath, EigenBench, BFTS, 2-SAT Robustness, FPS, LM Geometry Diagnostics, And INDUCTION

Building Tests Where The Error Reveals The Broken Operation

Problem

A student can memorize the shape of a proof without understanding whether the theorem is true. A model can do the same: write a proof for a false statement, exploit the order of logic clauses, hide uncertainty, satisfy a subjective judge for the wrong reason, or show neat internal structure that does not predict actual behavior.

State Space

The state is the formal premise, logic instance, proof trace, subjective-value judgment, revised uncertainty after evidence, exploration context, internal representation shape, family of controlled wording or ordering changes, hidden rule, and observed error pattern.

Object

The object is a test item designed so the error points to a specific operation. A good diagnostic question is like a mechanic's test: it is built so the failure tells you which part broke. BrokenMath uses false statements to test whether proof writing follows truth or merely flatters the premise. EigenBench treats subjective value as hidden agreement to estimate. BFTS tests decisions where uncertainty should change the next move after feedback arrives. 2-SAT Robustness changes logical surface form. FPS and INDUCTION check construction and rule recovery. LM Geometry Diagnostics asks whether internal shape measurements actually predict downstream capability. The formal phrase is a structural probe: a benchmark item built to expose one operation, not broad familiarity.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the intended operation: logical validity, satisfiability structure, constructive correctness, rule recovery, uncertainty-guided action, subjective-value direction, or representation shape that predicts behavior.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the surface form, labels, hidden rule, construction requirement, judge aggregation, or internal measurement. The target operation must stay isolated enough that the failure is interpretable.

Why It Works

It works when the benchmark behaves like an experiment rather than a trivia quiz. A false premise tests whether proof behavior depends on truth. Renaming clauses and adding filler tests whether the model follows logic rather than presentation. Construction tasks force outputs into a checkable set. Induction tasks ask whether the hidden rule was recovered rather than memorized. Subjective and exploration tests ask whether value or uncertainty is actually used to choose. Geometry diagnostics close the loop by asking whether an internal number predicts the operation it claims to explain. The key is controlled change: alter the wrapper, keep the real task fixed, and see which operation fails.

a reasoning test is useful when changing irrelevant surface form keeps the task same and errors reveal the failed operation

Evidence Check

Use controlled wording or ordering changes, false-premise audits, verifier checks, hidden-rule generalization, uncertainty-to-action curves, human validation of subjective values, tests that ask whether internal shape predicts behavior, and error clusters that map back to named operations.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the probe leaks shortcuts, when wording or ordering changes alter the real task, when majority agreement hides minority values, when internal shape numbers describe but do not predict behavior, or when the benchmark labels failure without saying which operation failed.

Connection

Language Generation in the Limit separates formal possibility from learnability. Transformer Circuits maps architecture to algorithms. Finite Test Certification bounds what benchmark evidence proves. This family gives the experimental counterpart: a reasoning benchmark is useful only when it makes a mathematical operation visible.

EXEMPLAR 107

DRPBench, CIRBench, SwingArena, And SparseRL

Finding Bugs That Only Appear In A Certain Order

Problem

Two people can safely use a kitchen if one opens the oven after the other steps away; the same actions become dangerous in the opposite order. Software has the same kind of failure. Code can look fine line by line, yet break when two threads, callbacks, UI events, or sparse updates happen in a particular order.

State Space

The state is program text, shared memory, lock state, event queue, UI state, callback order, compiler or runtime setting, sparse update path, possible orderings of events, and the observed failure trace.

Object

The object is the set of executions the program can actually reach. A program is not only its lines of text; it is all the ways those lines can run when threads, callbacks, UI events, and updates interleave. DRPBench targets data races, where two operations touch shared data in an unsafe order. CIRBench asks whether code reasoning follows runtime behavior rather than syntax alone. SwingArena makes UI behavior depend on state and events. SparseRL exposes how sparse updates change learning or execution paths. The formal phrase is reachable executions under partial-order constraints: all event orders allowed by the program and runtime rules, after respecting facts like "this callback must happen after that click" or "this lock must be acquired before this write."

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is correctness across the relevant event orders, not only the easiest trace. A correct answer should say whether a bad state can be reached and which ordering, event, or update makes it reachable.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the test schedule, simulated UI actions, runtime configuration, or explanation prompt. The score must depend on reachable behavior. A diagnosis that sounds right is weak evidence unless it maps to an executable path.

Why It Works

It works because interactive and concurrent software is a state machine over time. The same line can be safe or unsafe depending on what happened before it and what runs next. The proof of a bug is not that a line looks suspicious. The proof is an allowed path from a safe starting state to a bad state. Schedules, event traces, and UI states make that hidden path visible. If no allowed path reaches the bad state, the warning may be only a pattern match. If one does, the benchmark can point to the exact order that makes the failure real.

a bug exists when an allowed event order reaches a bad state from the starting state

Evidence Check

Report which event orders were tried, which variables were shared, which locks or ordering rules forced one action before another, which UI events happened, which tests reproduce the failure, which warnings were false alarms, and where a surface code pattern guessed the wrong outcome.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the benchmark tries only easy event orders, when its runtime model leaves out real platform behavior, when UI state is flattened into text, or when an explanation names a bug class without showing a path that can actually reach the bad state.

Connection

EditBench and VERINA use checkers for code and proof artifacts; CyberGym executes security claims; WebDevJudge inspects rendered software. DRPBench, CIRBench, SwingArena, and SparseRL sharpen the software side: correctness is a property of reachable state transitions.

EXEMPLAR 108

Prescriptive Scaling, Train-before-Test, CALIPER, And CV Relative Instability

Stating What Was Done Before The Score Was Measured

Problem

A runner's time means different things if they trained for six months, warmed up, ran on a hill, or ran after an injury. A model score is the same. It depends on compute, post-training, allowed adaptation, data drift, and the comparison split. Treating the score as a permanent property of the model hides the recipe that produced it.

State Space

The state is the base model, pretraining compute, post-training rule, adaptation budget, new task data, drift stream, cross-validation folds, instability measure, high-end target performance, and the final selection or retraining decision.

Object

Imagine comparing two runners without saying whether one trained for months, ran downhill, or started closer to the finish. The time is not meaningful without the setup. The object is capability with the full measurement recipe attached. What must stay the same is the meaning of the score. What may change is compute, training before the test, adaptation budget, drift response, or split choice, but those changes must travel with the claim. Prescriptive Scaling estimates how compute maps to downstream capability under a specified training recipe. Train-before-Test measures what a model can do after task-specific training, not only frozen behavior. CALIPER asks whether evidence after drift is enough to retrain. CV Relative Instability asks when cross-validation comparisons have honest uncertainty, meaning the train-test splits may be noisy enough to flip the model ranking. The formal phrase is a conditional capability functional: a score whose meaning depends on both the model and the full evaluation recipe.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the claim. A score should carry the conditions that make it true: what was trained, when adaptation was allowed, what drift was observed, and how stable the comparison was.

Allowed Movement

What may change is compute, adaptation before testing, retraining after drift, or fold comparison. Those changes must be named as part of the claim instead of hidden behind one leaderboard number.

Why It Works

It works because capability is not a bare property floating inside the model. It is affected by what was done before measuring. More compute changes what can be learned. Task-specific training changes what behavior can be extracted. Drift changes whether yesterday's model is still the right object to deploy. Cross-validation helps only when the model ordering is stable enough that the uncertainty around the split is smaller than the claimed gap. The plain rule is: report the score together with the procedure that created it, or the claim cannot be compared fairly.

reported capability = result of model, data, compute, adaptation, drift state, and comparison protocol together

Evidence Check

Report the full evaluation recipe, how uncertain the compute prediction is, how performance changes as more adaptation is allowed, whether new data after drift is enough to justify retraining, whether cross-validation intervals are wide enough to cover ranking noise, and cases where frozen and train-before-test rankings disagree.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the recipe is underspecified, when compute predictions stop holding after a new post-training method appears, when drift detection lacks enough fresh evidence for retraining, or when cross-validation folds are too unstable to support model ranking.

Connection

ATLAS treats items as measurement instruments; Micro-Benchmarking Reliability studies finite ranking noise; Finite Test Certification bounds benchmark claims. Prescriptive Scaling, Train-before-Test, CALIPER, and CV Relative Instability add the missing variable: what the evaluator did to the model and data before declaring capability.

EXEMPLAR 109

PhyWorldBench, AdAEM, WoW!, And ImageDoctor

Checking Whether A Pretty Artifact Still Obeys Its World

Problem

A generated video can look beautiful while a ball changes speed for no reason. A repaired medical image can look cleaner while removing evidence a doctor needed. A world benchmark can reward style while missing that the scene broke its own rules. The artifact must satisfy the domain, not just look plausible.

State Space

The state is the generated artifact, physical scene, force or contact relation, time order, domain rule, corruption process, repaired image, evaluator observation, human or model judgment, and constraint violation.

Object

Imagine repairing a photo of an X-ray. Making it smoother is bad if the repair erases the crack the doctor needed to see. The object is the leftover mismatch after checking the artifact against the rule of its world. What must stay the same is the domain fact: gravity, contact, time order, diagnostic evidence, or one consistent scene. What may change is pixels, frames, denoising, restoration, or the score. If a ball teleports, the mismatch is physical. If a repaired medical image removes a clue, the mismatch is clinical. If a world scene looks good frame by frame but cannot be one consistent world, the mismatch is in the world rule. PhyWorldBench tests whether video obeys physical principles. AdAEM-style evaluation asks whether artifact or anomaly scoring follows the real domain variable. WoW-style benchmarks test world consistency rather than frame quality alone. ImageDoctor treats repair as preserving diagnostic or meaning-bearing content while changing corrupted pixels. The formal phrase is domain constraint residual: the amount of rule-breaking still left after the artifact is compared with the domain rule.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is domain validity: gravity, contact, conservation, time consistency, diagnostic evidence, or artifact identity. The model may change pixels or frames, but the domain relation must remain true.

Allowed Movement

What may change is synthesis, denoising, restoration, simulation, or scoring. Evaluation must happen in the domain's own state, not only through generic visual quality.

Why It Works

It works by checking the rule where the rule lives. A physical video is not just plausible frames; it is a path where nearby moments should obey gravity, contact, object permanence, and time order. An image repair is not just a cleaner picture; it should remove corruption while keeping the evidence a doctor or domain expert needs. A useful benchmark makes the hidden rule violation visible enough to compare systems. If a generic judge only says "looks good," it can reward the exact artifact that the domain would reject.

an artifact is valid when the domain rule error stays small while task evidence is preserved

Evidence Check

Use physical scenes not seen during development, tests for contact and object permanence, checks that adjacent moments agree over time, domain-expert review, repair audits for each corruption type, measured rule mismatch, and examples where visual quality improves while domain validity gets worse.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the rule mismatch is guessed only loosely by a judge, when the benchmark covers too small a part of the physical world, when repair removes rare evidence, or when a world model learns benchmark cues without preserving the underlying law.

Connection

IRNO and RealPDEBench evaluate stable physical operators; VIST3A, TideGS, and RadioGS use renderable native state; Depth Anything 3 recovers coordinate structure. PhyWorldBench, AdAEM, WoW!, and ImageDoctor add the measurement side: domain generation must be judged where the domain constraint lives.

EXEMPLAR 110

TokSuite, Linguistic Nepotism, ATLAS Multilingual Transfer, And Local Redundancy

Seeing How Language Changes The Evidence A Model Can Use

Problem

The same fact can be cheap to write in one language and expensive in another. A search system may prefer familiar-language sources even when another source is stronger. A translation may carry the words but lose the local use. Language is not a neutral wrapper; it changes cost, retrieval, and evidence access.

State Space

The state is language, script, tokenizer, token length, meaning, internal neighborhood of similar texts, retrieval candidates, citation choice, repeated or new support among sources, transfer source, target task, and downstream answer quality.

Object

The object is how language changes access to the same evidence. The same meaning can become long or short, nearby or far away, easy or hard to retrieve, depending on the language interface. TokSuite measures whether tokenizers handle native language changes robustly. Linguistic Nepotism studies citation preference by language instead of evidence relevance. ATLAS multilingual transfer estimates how training languages help target evaluations. Local Redundancy asks when nearby examples add real information rather than repeats. The formal phrase is language geometry: the map of which items become near, far, cheap, costly, easy to retrieve, or easy to ignore after language and token choices reshape the evidence.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is evidence value across language interfaces. The same meaning should not become less useful only because one language costs more tokens, lands farther away internally, or is disfavored by retrieval.

Allowed Movement

What may change is tokenization, internal representation, translation, retrieval, citation, transfer, or duplicate removal. These operations should preserve evidence that carries the meaning needed for the answer, and expose when cost or retrieval bias changes available support.

Why It Works

It works by treating language as a route to evidence, not a harmless wrapper around it. Token length changes how much budget a fact consumes. Internal neighborhoods change which documents get retrieved. Citation habits can favor familiar-language sources over better evidence. Transfer helps only when the source language shares the structure the target task needs, not when it merely shares surface patterns. A second source helps only if it adds new support rather than repeating the same evidence in a favored language. What must stay the same is the evidence needed to answer the question; what may change is the language route used to reach it.

language evidence value depends on meaning, token cost, retrieval rank, shared structure, and nonrepeated support

Evidence Check

Measure how many extra tokens each language costs, whether small native-language changes break the tokenizer, which training languages help which target languages, whether cited sources are relevant rather than merely familiar, how translation changes retrieval rank, whether an added source brings new evidence, and low-resource cases where average multilingual scores hide interface failure.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when translation changes the task, when token tests miss cultural usage, when retrieval relevance is judged only in high-resource languages, or when source-language usefulness is inferred from correlation without a test that varies the source language itself.

Connection

Common Corpus treats source history as part of the data; Token Overcharging makes language cost visible; LIMSSR keeps missing evidence explicit; ATLAS item selection treats tests as information. TokSuite, Linguistic Nepotism, multilingual transfer, and redundancy work show the language version: evidence can become harder to afford, find, cite, or reuse before reasoning begins.

EXEMPLAR 111

AstaBench, SimuHome, MiniAppBench, Vision2Web, And PhotoAgent

Measuring Agents By What They Discover And Change

Problem

A lab assistant may need to search papers, a home assistant may need to schedule devices, an app tester may need to discover bugs, and a photo editor may need several preference-guided edits. These tasks do not have one fixed answer. They are successful when the agent explores, changes state, and produces evidence or an artifact that satisfies the goal.

State Space

The state is the environment, user intent, tool budget, observation history, device or app state, generated artifact, visual feedback, preference reward, exploratory action, discovered failure, and final assessment.

Object

The object is exploration over states and artifacts while only seeing part of the world. The agent cannot know everything up front, so it must act, observe what changed, and choose the next move. AstaBench controls research tools and costs for scientific agents. SimuHome uses a time-aware home simulator. MiniAppBench turns apps into exploratory testing targets. Vision2Web evaluates web-building through visual and functional checks. PhotoAgent treats image editing as step-by-step visual control. The formal phrase is a partially observed exploration process: learning what to do by acting and observing when the full world is not visible at once.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is legitimate progress under the task's own state variables: evidence gathered, device state changed, artifact behavior discovered, web intent satisfied, or image preference improved without breaking earlier constraints.

Allowed Movement

What may change is search, click, query, edit, scheduling, testing, or revision. Every movement should be judged by the state it reaches and the evidence it uncovers, not by whether it sounds like a plausible plan.

Why It Works

It works because open-ended agency is closer to experiment design than classification. The agent should spend actions to reveal hidden state, test a guess, improve an artifact, or decide that a path is not worth continuing. The benchmark needs an environment with visible state changes, a cost model, and a way to score discovered evidence or artifact behavior. The answer is the work path plus the final state, not a sentence describing the work path. A good score therefore asks whether each action bought progress that mattered, not whether the agent looked busy.

agent value = final-state utility plus discovered evidence minus action and tool costs

Evidence Check

Report logs of each state change, tool costs, simulator events, which parts of the app or environment were explored, visual and functional artifact checks, whether preference scores match the user's intent, and cases where final artifacts look acceptable but interaction traces reveal broken behavior.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the simulator leaves out real constraints, when exploratory tests reward shallow coverage, when visual judges miss interaction bugs, when preference scores reward style over task intent, or when tool budgets differ enough that model comparisons no longer mean the same thing.

Connection

Gaia2 and tau2-bench evaluate stateful trajectories; WebDevJudge and OmniVerifier judge native artifacts; ParetoPO turns tool use into a frontier. AstaBench, SimuHome, MiniAppBench, Vision2Web, and PhotoAgent add the open-ended case: capability is measured by exploration under a real state model.

EXEMPLAR 112

TabStruct, Neon, FAC Synthesis, SurvDiff, And PetaGAIL++

Generating Data That Keeps The Relationships The Task Uses

Problem

A fake spreadsheet can match the average age and average income while destroying the relationship between age, income, disease, and treatment. A fake mobility trace can look realistic while exposing private routines or losing traffic patterns. Synthetic data helps only when it preserves the relationships the downstream task needs.

State Space

The state is the real data source, synthetic generator, relationships between table columns, hidden features, generator bias, event time, the rule that decides when observation stops, mobility path, privacy noise, downstream task, and lost usefulness.

Object

The object is a generated data source constrained by the structure the task uses. The fake data does not need to copy real rows. It needs to keep the relationships that make later analysis work: which columns move together, which features are rare, when events happen, why observation stops, and which movement patterns matter. TabStruct measures whether synthetic tables preserve relationships without assuming a known causal graph. Neon uses generator bias as negative evidence about missing regions. FAC Synthesis targets coverage of sparse-autoencoder features. SurvDiff preserves event timing and censoring. PetaGAIL++ generates private mobility paths while keeping movement utility. The formal term is synthetic distribution: the probability source that creates generated examples, like a replacement data generator whose output should support the same analysis as the real source.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the structure the later analysis uses: relationships between variables, features that matter to the model, rules about when observation stops, movement patterns, or privacy-protected behavior patterns that still support the analysis.

Allowed Movement

What may change is sampling, imitation, small controlled changes, censoring, rebalancing, or privacy noise. Those changes should be controlled by what the downstream task needs, not by visible averages alone.

Why It Works

It works when generation is treated as designing a replacement data source for a named use. A table that preserves single-column averages but breaks the relationship between treatment and outcome is not useful for treatment analysis. Bias can reveal missing regions because the generator fails where it has not learned the source well. Feature-targeted synthesis can fill coverage holes when the generated examples still look like valid cases. Survival data must preserve both events and the process that stops observation. Mobility data must preserve aggregate movement while hiding individual routines. The test is downstream: does the analysis reach the same kind of answer on generated data as it would on real data?

synthetic data is useful when the downstream analysis on generated data matches the analysis on real data while privacy and coverage constraints hold

Evidence Check

Compare downstream task performance, recovery of relationships between variables, coverage of important model features, event times and observation-stopping patterns, movement summaries, private-information exposure, how real and generated variables move together, and regions where synthetic samples expose rather than hide generator failure.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when synthetic data preserves easy one-column summaries but breaks interactions, when feature coverage creates unnatural examples, when stopped observation is treated as ordinary missing data, when privacy noise erases rare movement structure, or when downstream validation is too narrow.

Connection

Common Corpus treats source as part of the data distribution; LIMSSR keeps missingness explicit; DiReCT values data by optimizer effect; GoodDiffusion attaches authorization to samples. TabStruct, Neon, FAC Synthesis, SurvDiff, and PetaGAIL++ define the synthetic-data rule: generate the structure the claim uses.

EXEMPLAR 113

Semantic Watermark Fingerprints, Ellipse Signatures, GoodDiffusion, Spherical Watermark, And WaterSIC

Keeping A Source Mark Detectable After Normal Changes

Problem

A receipt is not useful if it vanishes when the paper is folded. A watermark is not useful if it disappears after fine-tuning, quantization, filtering, editing, paraphrase, or platform conversion. Provenance methods have to survive the changes people actually apply while staying hard to forge.

State Space

The state is the original model or sample, secret key, watermark signal, meaning domain, output probabilities, diffusion input signature, transformation process, attacker action, detector statistic, false positive, and false negative.

Object

The object is a test that can still tell marked from unmarked after transformation. A good mark must survive ordinary handling, stay small enough not to damage the artifact, and remain hard for an attacker to forge or remove. Semantic Watermark Fingerprints place marks in broad meaning domains so ownership can survive model changes. Ellipse Signatures verify models from output probabilities. GoodDiffusion conditions generation on valid per-input signatures and shows signature recovery attacks. Spherical Watermark and WaterSIC study watermark shapes that preserve the output distribution while remaining detectable. The formal term is hypothesis test: a decision rule that compares two stories, "unmarked" and "marked," while tracking how often it wrongly flags clean artifacts or misses marked ones.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is a controlled signal: detectable by authorized checking, low-distortion for normal users, low false-positive on unrelated models or artifacts, and resistant to likely transformations.

Allowed Movement

What may change is fine-tuning, quantization, filtering, paraphrase, editing, or generation through another system. The provenance claim must state which transformation path the signal is expected to survive.

Why It Works

It works by placing a small, recoverable pattern where normal transformations do not erase it. The detector then asks which story better explains the observed artifact: it came from an unmarked source, or it came from a marked one that passed through allowed changes. The mark must be strong enough to detect and weak enough not to change normal quality. The allowed transformations must be stated, because a mark that survives paraphrase may not survive fine-tuning or filtering. GoodDiffusion also shows the risk: once a signature controls generation, attackers may try to infer the signature itself.

detect provenance when a statistic separates marked from unmarked after the stated transformation path at controlled error rates

Evidence Check

Report how often unmarked artifacts are wrongly flagged and marked artifacts are missed, how the signal survives each transformation, whether it survives fine-tuning and smaller-number deployment, whether output probabilities verify the source, whether meaning-domain marks transfer, whether quality or probability changes noticeably, whether adaptive attackers can remove the mark, and whether signatures can be recovered.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the deployment channel erases the hidden mark, when the watermark changes user-visible quality, when detector access assumptions are unrealistic, when task-meaning categories are too coarse, or when authorization signatures can be reverse-engineered.

Connection

Catch-22 frames watermarking as a robustness-stealth tradeoff; LLM DNA fingerprints model lineage; Common Corpus and Copyright-Bench name provenance constraints. This family states the statistical core: provenance is useful only when distinguishability survives the transformations people actually apply.

EXEMPLAR 114

Performative Misalignment, LLM Adaptability Limits, MNPO, DPO Unchained, And What Preferences Can And Cannot Predict

Figuring Out Which Failure Story The Behavior Actually Supports

Problem

If a student gives the answer the teacher wants, they might understand the topic, flatter the teacher, copy a pattern, or be confused but lucky. Alignment behavior has the same ambiguity. A model that looks aligned may be deceptive, sycophantic, prompt-brittle, stuck in conflicting preferences, or optimized with the wrong choice rule.

State Space

The state is the prompt, evaluator identity, model belief, preference observation, policy population, choice model, payoff table, adaptation attempt, high-confidence error, candidate mechanism, and observed aligned or misaligned behavior.

Object

The object is the set of failure stories still consistent with the evidence. The evaluator should ask which stories remain possible after the tests: deception, researcher pleasing, task misunderstanding, preference conflict, prompt limits, or a training rule that pushes the wrong way. Performative Misalignment asks whether apparent alignment-faking may instead be pleasing researchers. LLM Adaptability Limits shows many annotation errors cannot be fixed by prompting. MNPO treats training from preferences as a game where changing one side's rule changes how the other sides respond. DPO Unchained checks whether the training rule matches the story it assumes about how people make choices. What Preferences Can and Cannot Predict studies when preference observations remain stable in multi-agent settings. The formal phrase is an identified set: the group of possible mechanisms the evidence has not ruled out yet.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the explanation of behavior. The method should preserve the distinction between deception, evaluator pleasing, task-definition mismatch, prompt-level correction limits, strategic preference cycles, and a wrong choice rule built into the training score.

Allowed Movement

What may change is prompts, evaluator identity, preference populations, adaptation budgets, or assumptions about how choices were made. The evaluator should not collapse all failures into one story unless the evidence rules out the alternatives.

Why It Works

It works by treating behavior as evidence, not as the cause by itself. The same aligned-looking answer can come from understanding, obedience to the evaluator, imitation, confusion, or strategy. To know what to fix, the test must create conditions where those stories predict different behavior. If researcher identity changes the answer, pleasing the evaluator becomes plausible. If prompting cannot fix confident errors, the problem is not only wording. If preferences cycle or differ across groups, one reward climb may not represent the population. If the preference-training rule assumes the wrong choice process, training can move the model in the wrong direction. The method is useful when it narrows the set of live stories rather than forcing one label too early.

diagnose alignment by listing behavior-consistent stories, then using tests that separate those stories

Evidence Check

Run evaluator-identity swaps, prompt variations that try to rescue errors, audits of confident wrong answers, checks for preference cycles across groups, tests for whether the many-player process has actually settled, checks of the choice rule behind the training score, and interventions where deception and evaluator-pleasing predict different behavior.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when tests only rename the same prompt pressure, when preference data lacks the diversity needed to reveal cycles, when prompt failures are mistaken for permanent incapability, or when a training target is judged only by average preference score.

Connection

Pressure Reveals Character exposes behavior under intervention; VALUEFLOW measures value intensity; ParetoPO and SafeDPO make tradeoffs explicit; RLVepsR studies the direction the verifier's score pushes model behavior. Performative Misalignment, adaptability limits, MNPO, DPO Unchained, and preference-predictability work add the diagnostic rule: alignment is not solved until the mechanism behind the observed behavior is identified.

EXEMPLAR 115

PonderLM-2, Reasoning With Sampling, T3, THOR, Rational Agents, And NEXCO

Spending Extra Thinking Only When It Changes The Answer

Problem

A person solving a puzzle can stare longer, try another route, ask a clarifying question, use a calculator, or stop. More time helps only if it removes confusion or opens a route that was blocked. Model reasoning has the same issue. Extra hidden steps, extra samples, longer tool sessions, or more questions are useful only when they buy information, remove a mistake, or make a partial answer possible.

State Space

The state is the prompt, what the model currently thinks, alternative answer attempts, what is still uncertain, the partial solution, tool results, the cost of asking a question, the current work path, verifier feedback, compute budget, and final decision.

Object

The object is a rule for deciding whether another thinking step is worth paying for. Imagine doing a crossword: another minute helps if it rules out a wrong clue, reveals a new crossing word, or shows that you should stop and ask for help. It does not help if you only repeat the same guess. PonderLM-2 spends hidden thinking steps. Reasoning with Sampling tries several answer routes without letting them collapse into copies of one route. T3 cuts off parts of a work path that no longer change the belief. THOR uses immediate tool feedback to correct itself. Rational Agents choose questions or actions by how much useful uncertainty they remove. NEXCO grows partial solutions that still obey the constraints. The formal phrase is a controlled computation policy: a rule for buying more computation only when the extra work can improve the decision.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful progress per unit cost. Each extra thought, sample, question, or tool call should reduce uncertainty, move a partial solution closer to being allowed, or expose evidence that changes the next action.

Allowed Movement

What may change is internal thinking, sampling alternatives, asking questions, calling tools, cutting off weak work paths, or expanding partial plans. Those moves should be controlled by what they add, not by a blanket preference for longer reasoning traces.

Why It Works

It works because reasoning is a series of paid checks. A sample can reveal a different route. A tool call can replace a guess with an observation. A question can remove a missing condition. Stopping can prevent low-value drift. What must stay the same is useful change in belief or action: the next step should be able to change what the model believes, does, or rejects. What may change is extra hidden thought, extra samples, questions, tools, or pruning. The method is trustworthy only when those moves are chosen because the next check might change the answer enough to justify the cost, not because long traces look more serious.

continue reasoning when the expected gain from the next check is larger than its compute, time, and error cost

Evidence Check

Report how accuracy changes as more compute is spent, whether samples are genuinely different and correct, which belief changes caused stopping, whether tool feedback was used, whether questions were worth their cost, what happens when prefixes are cut, and cases where longer reasoning makes answers worse.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when hidden thinking is not grounded in evidence, when samples are copies with different wording, when tool feedback is ignored, when questions are free in the benchmark but costly in deployment, or when truncation removes the one early clue that would have recovered the answer.

Connection

The Tell-Tale Norm estimates where thought is intense; h1 makes long horizons learnable; Weak-Strong Verification buys stronger evidence near uncertainty boundaries; ParetoPO prices tools. This family states the common rule: reasoning compute should be spent where it changes the state of knowledge.

EXEMPLAR 116

SVD As Fast Interpretability, Neuron-Basis Circuits, MDA, AI Engram, FlashTrace, T-SAE, And Temporal Superposition

Showing Which Part Actually Caused The Output

Problem

An explanation can point to a word, neuron, feature, or training example and still not show cause. It is like saying a lamp turned on because the wall is white. The real question is which piece received the evidence, which piece passed it forward, and whether changing that piece would change the answer.

State Space

The state is the training example, internal activation values, neuron or small weight pattern, feature list, memory trace, path from input to output, feature that changes over time, intervention on a circuit, output change, and evidence that the explanation is faithful.

Object

The object is a cause map from source to behavior. A useful explanation should read like a wiring note: this training example helped build this internal part; this text span activated it; this part passed the signal forward; changing it changed this output. SVD as Fast Interpretability splits a weight block into simple directions that detect one thing and write another. Neuron-Basis Circuits asks whether native neurons already form usable causal parts. MDA links influential examples to the later formation of induction heads and in-context learning. AI Engram isolates artificial memory traces by asking what hidden memory could have produced the behavior. FlashTrace follows long-context influence through text spans. T-SAE asks for features that stay consistent over time. Temporal Superposition studies what happens when several time-based signals are packed into the same limited space. The formal phrase is an attributed mechanism graph: a map showing which source, internal part, and output effect are causally linked.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is causal responsibility. The named source, direction, feature, or memory component should be the reason the computation changes, not merely something that appears nearby when the answer is produced.

Allowed Movement

What may change is how the analyst splits weights, traces influence, patches activations, removes examples, recovers memory vectors, or groups text spans. The explanation must survive an intervention: change the alleged cause and the target behavior should change in the predicted way.

Why It Works

It works by turning explanation into a cause-and-effect test. Splitting a weight block helps only if one exposed direction really detects a condition or writes a result. A neuron explanation helps only if changing that neuron changes the relevant behavior. Data attribution matters when training examples helped build the internal procedure now being used. Memory matters when the model carries information from earlier inputs into later answers. Time features matter only if the same feature continues to play the same role as context changes. The plain rule is: an explanation is stronger when changing the named part changes the output, while changing nearby irrelevant parts does not.

an internal-route claim holds when changing the named component changes the target behavior and control changes do not

Evidence Check

Run activation patching, remove or change claimed parts, remove or add source examples, test memory creation and erasure, check whether highlighted spans are truly used, test features across time, and audit unwanted behavior changes outside the target claim.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when a weight split is convenient but not causal, when highlighted tokens are correlated with the answer but unused, when many different hidden memories could explain the same output, or when shared time features make one feature look responsible for another.

Connection

Steer Like the LLM turns directions into control; Causal Route Gating tests modality routes; Neural Effect Search treats features as causal discovery units. SVD, neuron-basis, data-attribution, engram, and temporal-feature papers supply the missing bridge from explanation to mechanism.

EXEMPLAR 117

FALCON, FIDIA, Control Consistency Losses, Dimension-Free Diffusion Sampling, High-Accuracy Sampling, And Manifold Perturbations

Drawing Samples That Preserve The Scientific Quantity

Problem

A scientist may not care whether one generated molecule or state looks plausible. They may care about an average energy, the best candidate in a batch, the chance of a rare event, or whether every sample stays on a valid physical surface. A sampler can make nice-looking examples while breaking the number the scientist wanted to estimate.

State Space

The state is the target source of samples, proposed sampling path, flow map, probability score, energy, valid-surface coordinate, rare-event route, selected sample batch, downstream quantity, estimation bias, and variation across repeated estimates.

Object

The object is movement of probability that preserves the quantity read from the samples. Think of a lab that draws test tubes from a freezer. If the scientist needs the average energy, the rare dangerous reaction, or the best candidate in a small batch, the drawing rule must protect that number. Plausible-looking samples are not enough. FALCON learns fast molecular flows while keeping probability scores accurate for equilibrium estimates. FIDIA trains for the best sample selected from a finite batch. Control Consistency Losses learn routes into rare events. Dimension-Free and High-Accuracy Sampling ask whether guarantees survive higher dimensions or stricter precision. Manifold Perturbations keep movement on the valid surface. The formal phrase is functional-preserving probability movement: the sampler may move probability around, but the downstream quantity read from the samples must stay correct.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the scientific quantity the samples are used to estimate: density, free energy, rare-event chance, best-candidate value, valid-surface average, or coverage of important modes.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the sampling route, bridge into rare states, small controlled nudge, control rule, or batch selection. Those changes are allowed only if they preserve the quantity behind the scientific claim.

Why It Works

It works by matching the sampling method to the number being estimated. If the number is an equilibrium density, probability accounting matters because common and uncommon states must appear in the right proportions. If the number is the best item in a batch, the high end of the distribution matters more than the average. If the number is a rare-event chance, ordinary samples waste effort in common regions and may never visit the important event. If valid states lie on a special surface, moves that leave that surface create invalid samples. The sampler is good when it puts effort where the final scientific quantity is sensitive and can state the remaining error.

a sampler is useful when the scientific quantity estimated from generated samples matches the quantity from the target source within known error

Evidence Check

Report probability-score calibration, free-energy or density checks, best-of-batch calibration, enough effective rare-event samples, valid-surface rule violations, behavior as dimension grows, coverage of important modes, and estimation bias on held-out scientific systems.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when visual diversity hides probability error, when best-of-batch training overfits a proxy score, when rare-event control misses part of the tail, when surface-preserving moves only approximately stay valid, or when high-dimensional guarantees assume a smoother target than the domain provides.

Connection

Safety-Guided Flow and DMPO move distributions under reward or safety pressure; Error Propagation in Quantized Diffusion tracks path error; IRNO and RealPDEBench evaluate physical operators. FALCON, FIDIA, Control Consistency, and sampling-theory papers make the scientific rule explicit: the sample path is valid only relative to the quantity being estimated.

EXEMPLAR 118

Optimal MoE Sparsity, Pre-Training Under Infinite Compute, Scaling Laws Origin, Shallow NN Scaling Laws, And Power-Law Compositional Reasoning

Separating The Different Resources Hidden Inside "Bigger"

Problem

Saying a model improved because it got bigger is like saying a restaurant improved because it spent more money. The money could have gone to a larger kitchen, better ingredients, more cooks, more training, or repeated practice on the same menu. Model scaling has the same ambiguity: parameters, active compute, token budget, repeated data, data structure, memorization, reasoning, and post-training can all change the result.

State Space

The state is stored parameters, active experts, compute used per example, tokens per parameter, uncertainty in the data source, number of repeated passes over data, penalties that prevent fitting noise, ensemble or distillation rule, source generator, strength of learnable directions, compositional depth, and downstream capability.

Object

The object is a map from resources to capability. The map should not say only "bigger helped." It should say what kind of bigger: more stored facts, more active computation per example, more data passes, more varied examples, more useful structure, or more post-training. Optimal MoE Sparsity separates stored capacity from active compute and shows memorization and reasoning respond differently. Pre-Training Under Infinite Compute asks how to get more signal from fixed data when compute is abundant. Scaling Laws Origin studies simple data sources that create power-law curves. Shallow NN Scaling Laws links improvement to which feature directions are available. Power-Law Compositional Reasoning asks how part-whole structure changes the curve. The formal phrase is a resource-allocation surface: a map that keeps each resource axis separate instead of flattening all resources into one size number.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the scaling claim. A curve should say which resource changed, which capability was measured, and what structure in the data made improvement possible.

Allowed Movement

What may change is stored parameter count, active experts, repeated data passes, penalties against fitting noise, combining several models, compressing one model into another, token budget, or curriculum. Those movements should be named separately because they buy different kinds of improvement.

Why It Works

It works because different failures are blocked by different resources. Memorizing facts may need more storage. Reasoning may need examples that show how parts combine. Fixed-data training may need repeated use of the same data, but only with rules that prevent fitting noise. Sparse experts can save compute only if the active route still sees enough evidence for the current task. Power laws need not mean scale has magic in it; they can come from the source structure and from which directions are easy or hard to learn. The useful claim is not "scale works"; it is which resource removed which bottleneck.

capability should be reported as a result of active compute, stored capacity, token budget, repetition, data structure, and post-training together

Evidence Check

Report active versus total parameter tests, token-budget sweeps, repeated-pass tests on fixed data, controls over the data source, measurements of which feature directions are learned, compositional-depth tests, memorization-versus-reasoning splits, and prediction error when post-training changes.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when a fitted power law is extrapolated outside the data regime, when gains are actually post-training effects, when memorization improvements are sold as reasoning improvements, or when sparse expert routing saves compute but starves the active path of needed evidence.

Connection

Prescriptive Scaling attaches scores to the recipe that produced them; Language Generation in the Limit separates what is possible from what evidence can teach; Alignment-Sensitive Rates asks where useful signal sits. Scaling-law and MoE papers add the resource view: progress depends on how compute, data, and structure fit together, not on a single size variable.

EXEMPLAR 119

Linear Causal Representation Learning, LC-PB-SCM, Robust Causal Discovery With Power-Laws, And Unpaired Causal IV

Learning Variables You Can Actually Intervene On

Problem

A model can predict that wet sidewalks go with umbrellas, but that does not tell it what happens if you make people carry umbrellas. Prediction can mix cause, effect, and shared background into one useful-looking feature. Causal representation work asks for internal variables that still mean something when one factor is changed on purpose.

State Space

The state is observed data, hidden factors, targets for intervention, changes across environments, variables that shift one cause without directly shifting the outcome, time-series patterns, structural equations, hidden common causes, limits on how many independent directions the data can support, and the causal effect or graph that can be justified.

Object

The object is a coordinate system whose parts have intervention meaning. A useful coordinate is not just a prediction feature; changing it should correspond to changing one real factor while the causal question remains meaningful. Linear Causal Representation Learning asks when hidden coordinates recover causal variables. LC-PB-SCM studies causal models when some assumptions are partly broken or some structure is hidden. Robust Causal Discovery with Power-Laws extracts stable time-pattern features before graph discovery. Unpaired Causal IV uses special variables, called instruments, when matched "before and after" examples are unavailable. The formal phrase is an identifiable causal coordinate system: a representation where the evidence and assumptions force the same answer about the causal variable of interest, so the coordinate cannot be freely renamed or rotated into a different causal story.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is intervention meaning. A coordinate is useful if changing it corresponds to a stable causal operation, not just a rotated prediction feature that happens to fit the data.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the representation of observations, which environments are compared, which instruments are used, how time-series features are compressed, or whether the answer is a set of still-possible causal stories. The method must state which causal distinctions the evidence fixes and which ones remain unresolved.

Why It Works

It works only when the data contains some difference that prediction alone cannot explain away. An intervention shows what changes when one factor is forced. An instrument helps when it moves a cause without directly moving the outcome. An environment shift helps when the same relation survives some changes and breaks under others. Stable time patterns can help separate causes that move on different schedules. What must stay the same is the meaning of the "what if we changed this?" question. What may change is the representation used to expose the variable. The model does not get causality from low error; it gets causal meaning when every story still compatible with the evidence agrees on the target intervention claim.

a causal claim is justified when all evidence-compatible stories give the same answer to the target "what if we changed this?" question

Evidence Check

Report tests across changed environments, checks that instruments move only what they are supposed to move, interventions on learned coordinates, lists of still-possible causal stories, stress tests for hidden common causes, stability of time-pattern features, and counterexamples where predictive features rotate while causal meaning changes.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when instruments are weak or invalid, when an environment shift changes several mechanisms at once, when stable time features are not causal, when learned coordinates can rotate in ways that change intervention meaning, or when the paper reports one graph even though the evidence supports many.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence and DiCoLa define what passive evidence can identify; Local Covariate Selection targets one adjustment claim; CausalGame adds active experiments. Causal representation papers add the coordinate question: the model must learn variables that interventions can act on.

EXEMPLAR 120

Compositional Generalization Requires Linear Orthogonal Representations, Concept Binding, Emergent Analogical Reasoning, Grammar Substructure, And T-SAE

Reusing Parts In New Combinations

Problem

A child who knows "red square" and "blue circle" should be able to understand "blue square." A model can fail this if it memorized whole phrases instead of learning reusable parts. The same problem appears in grammar, roles, analogies, and relations: the parts must survive when arranged in a new way.

State Space

The state is the concept part, the operation that binds parts together, relation, analogy map, grammar feature, token context, internal direction, separation between directions, compositional task, and generalization error.

Object

The object is a representation where useful parts stay separable and can be joined into a whole. If "blue" and "square" are stored as one memorized phrase, the model may not know what to do with "blue triangle." The parts need their own handles, and the joining rule needs to combine them without erasing either part. Compositional Generalization Requires Linear Orthogonal Representations argues that reusable concepts need directions that do not overwrite each other. Concept Binding studies how parts combine without too much complexity. Emergent Analogical Reasoning tests whether relation structure can move to a new domain. Grammar Substructure and T-SAE recover internal features that line up with reusable grammar and time context. The formal phrase is a factorized representation with controlled binding: the parts remain recoverable, and the joining step records how they fit into a structured whole.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is part identity under recombination. A concept should remain recoverable when paired with a new context, and a relation should remain usable when the objects in the relation change.

Allowed Movement

What may change is encoding, binding, attention, or composition. The representation should keep reusable parts distinct enough that valid new combinations can be reached without learning every pair from scratch.
