Why It Works

It works because compositional tasks are built from pieces. If color and shape are stored in tangled form, changing color can accidentally change shape. If they are stored separately enough, the model can reuse each one. Binding is the step that joins parts without erasing them. Analogies work when the relation, not the surface object, is what transfers. Grammar works when a high-level structure can survive local word changes while still keeping the local words available. What must stay the same is part identity; what may change is the new arrangement.

generalization improves when parts remain recoverable after being combined into a new valid structure

Evidence Check

Use held-out combinations of parts, relation-transfer tests, analogy swaps, grammar changes, probes that recover features, tests that remove or change the binding operation, measurements of whether directions interfere, and cases where high training accuracy hides failure on new combinations.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when apparent parts are dataset artifacts, when separating variables prevents needed interaction, when binding preserves symbols but not relations, or when grammar features are readable to people but not actually used by the model.

Connection

GraphGlue transfers relational coordinates across graph domains; Alignment-Sensitive Rates asks which learned directions carry target signal; Neuron-Basis Circuits tests causal feature bases. Compositional representation papers answer the big generalization question: what must be separated so the system can recombine it later?

EXEMPLAR 121

LVLMs-Saliency, KL-Minimal Visual MI, Visual Symbolic Mechanisms, RFCMH, And AGREE

Checking Which Part Of The Image Supported The Claim

Problem

A model may answer "the dog is wet" because it saw water on the fur, or because captions about dogs often mention baths. The answer can be right for the wrong reason. Visual grounding asks whether the claim is controlled by the relevant part of the current image rather than by language habit or a nearby but irrelevant visual clue.

State Space

The state is image region, where attention goes, how changing pixels would change the answer, visual token, text prior, visual relation such as "on top of" or "inside," generated claim, attribute score, image-text consistency check, and hallucination event.

Object

The object is responsibility for a visual claim. The method should name the patch, object relation, color, layout, or image-text match that actually made the answer possible. LVLMs-Saliency combines attention with change-sensitivity to ask whether answers stay grounded in the current image. KL-Minimal Visual MI asks what visual information is necessary, not merely present: remove image information until removing any more would change the claim. Visual Symbolic Mechanisms test whether visual relations support reasoning. RFCMH uses image-text consistency to repair unsupported generations. AGREE treats subjective visual judgment as several attributes rather than one score. The formal phrase is localized evidence responsibility: naming the region, relation, or attribute that actually supports the claim.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is support. The output claim should trace back to the image region, visual relation, or attribute that justifies it.

Allowed Movement

What may change is attention, image-text fusion, repair, attribution, or aggregation of human judgments. The method should keep the visual evidence route separate from language priors and preserve which attribute or region caused the decision.

Why It Works

It works by asking a simple change test: if the claimed visual evidence were removed or changed, would the answer change? A heatmap helps only if it points to evidence the model actually uses. Minimal visual information helps because it asks how much image evidence is enough for the claim. Symbolic visual relations help when the issue is not a patch of pixels but a relation between things. Multi-attribute judgment helps because one average score can hide whether color, layout, realism, or safety caused the evaluation. The formal term counterfactual means this kind of "what if the evidence were different?" test.

a visual claim is grounded when changing the responsible visual evidence changes the claim more than changing irrelevant image or text cues

Evidence Check

Mask image regions, swap text habits, compare attention with change-sensitivity, make controlled changes to visual relations, test image-text repair, check whether attribute-level judgments are reliable, and trace hallucinations where attention moves away from the image evidence.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the highlight map is an after-the-fact picture rather than a cause, when masking changes the task accidentally, when symbolic labels omit important continuous cues, when repair models smooth away evidence, or when attribute scores are not reliable enough to split the judgment.

Connection

VISUALSWAP and MINT test modality causality; DLMR preserves multimodal memory provenance; Causal Route Gating intervenes on internal routes. LVLMs-Saliency, KL-Minimal Visual MI, symbolic visual mechanisms, RFCMH, and AGREE make grounding local: which visual evidence was responsible?

EXEMPLAR 122

CAT-Q, ETTFS, Rex, Frozen-PINN, S3GNN, And Solving Time-Dependent Differential Equations With Physical Dynamical Systems

Making Sure The Computation That Runs Still Solves The Same Problem

Problem

A formula on paper can be correct while the version that runs on hardware is not. Smaller numbers can erase rare signals. Spike timing can save work but miss needed events. A solver can be reversible in theory but preserve numerical junk. A graph model can pass local messages while missing long-range physics. The computation that actually runs is part of the scientific claim.

State Space

The state is model weights, number format, GPU budget, event time, spike state, ordinary or noisy differential-equation solver state, physical field, frozen feature map, graph mesh, long-range operator, quantity that physics says should stay true, and hardware trace.

Object

The object is a computation over time as realized by a machine. The paper algorithm, the number format, the timing of events, and the hardware path all become part of the same claim because any one of them can change the result. CAT-Q studies practical three-value weight conversion under GPU constraints. ETTFS uses time-to-first-spike as the inference schedule. Rex uses reversible solver structure for ordinary and noisy differential equations. Frozen-PINN fixes feature bases for physical equation solving. S3GNN mixes local graph messages with long-range graph structure. Physical TDDE solvers use physical systems as part of the computation. The formal phrase is dynamical computation realized on a machine: the math, number system, timing, and hardware behavior are one object to check because the running process unfolds through time.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the claim-bearing quantity after it passes through number format, solver steps, hardware timing, graph structure, and physical field structure.

Allowed Movement

What may change is quantization, event scheduling, reversing solver steps, freezing features, mixing local and long-range graph information, or using physical dynamics directly. The deployed computation must preserve the quantity the claim depends on in the arithmetic and timing regime where it actually runs.

Why It Works

It works when the algorithm matches the machine and the physics. Three-value weights help only if the important behavior survives the smaller number alphabet. Event-driven inference helps only if early sparse events carry the needed information. Reversible solvers help only if the kept state is real signal, not accumulated numerical artifact. Long-range graph mixing helps when distant parts of a physical system coordinate. Physical solvers are valid only when the hardware dynamics implement the target equation closely enough. What must stay the same is the quantity the scientific claim depends on; what may change is the cheaper or different way of running the computation.

a deployed scientific computation is valid when the running machine approximates the target operation on the claim-bearing state within the tested tolerance

Evidence Check

Report hardware traces, behavior drift after smaller-number conversion, spike-latency curves, solver reversibility error, physical-equation rule mismatch, graph long-range ablations, tests of quantities physics says should stay true, and sensitivity to production-shaped workloads rather than only idealized runtime or loss.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the smaller number system erases rare directions, when spike timing is sparse but uninformative, when reversible solvers preserve numerical artifacts, when frozen features cannot represent boundary regimes, when long-range graph summaries miss local breaks, or when production hardware differs from the studied machine.

Connection

LiftQuant and TetraJet treat arithmetic as part of the model; TileLang and einx expose how tensor programs fit the machine; IRNO and RealPDEBench require stable physical operators. CAT-Q, ETTFS, Rex, Frozen-PINN, S3GNN, and physical TDDE solvers complete the machine-science bridge: implementation is part of the mathematical claim.

EXEMPLAR 123

Phase Retrieval Dynamics, Low-Rank LM Logits, Token Association Dynamics, And Top-W

Seeing The Smaller Pattern Behind Output Scores

Problem

A menu may have hundreds of dishes, but a customer may really be choosing along a few hidden lines: spicy or mild, cheap or expensive, familiar or new. A language model's next-token scores can look like a giant table, yet the decision may be controlled by a few shared score patterns. If we only inspect the final probabilities, we can miss the smaller pattern that made one token beat another.

State Space

The state is the model's hidden state, the matrix that turns hidden state into token scores, the table of scores for a prompt, links between related tokens, shared score patterns, sign or direction ambiguity, probability after normalization, selection rule, and chosen output token.

Object

Imagine a menu where hundreds of dishes mostly move along a few choices: spicy or mild, cheap or expensive, familiar or new. The object is the smaller pattern inside the model's huge next-word score table. What must stay the same is the score gap that changes the decision: why this token beats that one. What may change is how the score table is split, smoothed, recovered, or used for selection. Low-Rank LM Logits asks whether many prompt-output scores are controlled by a small number of shared factors. Phase Retrieval Dynamics studies how a hidden signal can be recovered when observations show strength but not the full direction. Token Association Dynamics treats token scores as linked choices where one token's score can move related tokens. Top-W studies choosing through a controlled scoring rule rather than always taking raw maximum probability. The formal phrase is recoverable output-score geometry: the structure inside the score table that can be found and used to explain the decision.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the score gap that matters for the decision: which alternatives are separated, which token associations created that separation, and which shared factors keep the separation stable when prompts or decoding rules change.

Allowed Movement

What may change is splitting the score table into factors, recovering hidden directions, smoothing or thresholding scores, following token associations, or adjusting the output rule so its stated confidence matches observed correctness. These moves should preserve the score differences that distinguish correct, plausible, unsafe, or irrelevant tokens.

Why It Works

It works when the huge vocabulary choice is not really using all dimensions independently. If many tokens move together, a few shared factors can explain much of the decision. If the model exposes enough information about how strong a hidden signal is, the missing direction can sometimes be recovered. If token choices are linked, the right question is not one isolated probability but how score changes spread through related tokens. What must stay the same is the score gap that changes the decision. What may change is factorizing, smoothing, or changing the selection rule. Those moves are trustworthy only if the decision-making gap remains larger than noise, confidence-estimation error, and spillover from related tokens.

output behavior is stable when the score gaps that matter remain larger than noise, confidence-estimation error, and spillover from related tokens

Evidence Check

Measure how many shared factors explain the score table, make controlled changes to those factors, test direction recovery on held-out prompts, remove token-association links, compare raw-probability and constrained decoding decisions, and report where small score-gap changes flip behavior.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when important behavior is carried by rare score patterns, when recovered directions are numerically real but meaningless for the task, when token-association graphs encode dataset artifacts, or when confidence rules hide uncertainty by forcing sharp choices.

Connection

Alignment-sensitive rates and local sensitivity studies ask which directions are learnable; SVD interpretability and neuron-basis circuits study which internal directions explain behavior; speculative decoding asks whether output changes preserve a target distribution. These papers bring that question to the final score surface: what smaller structure inside the token scores is the model really using?

EXEMPLAR 124

Persistent Homology LLM Adversarial Influence, Neural Ricci Flow, Wasserstein GPCA, Riemannian Metric Matching, And Diffusion Spacetime

Measuring Distance Along The Space The Data Actually Uses

Problem

Two towns can be close on a map but far apart by road if a mountain blocks the direct route. Model representations have the same issue. Two internal points can look close by ordinary coordinate distance even when no meaningful data path connects them, or look far apart while being neighbors along the real data surface.

State Space

The state is an embedding vector, the path a representation takes across layers, a probability cloud, local distance rule, bending of the space, shortest meaningful path, holes or loops in the data shape, diffusion time, denoising state, adversarial change, and main direction of variation.

Object

Imagine two houses that look close on a paper map but are separated by a river with no bridge. The useful distance is the route people can actually take. The object is distance and shape measured along paths that real data can follow. What must stay the same is neighborhood meaning: points that behave like neighbors should stay near by the data-road ruler, and points separated by a gap should not be treated as close just because a straight line is short. What may change is the ruler, smoothing, denoising stage, or probability-cloud movement being studied. Persistent Homology LLM Adversarial Influence asks whether attacks change holes or connected pieces in activation clouds. Neural Ricci Flow studies smoothing by changing how local distances bend. Wasserstein GPCA searches for main directions of change between probability clouds. Riemannian Metric Matching learns local distance from denoising behavior. Diffusion Spacetime treats sample position and denoising time together. The formal phrase is intrinsic geometry: distances, paths, bends, and holes measured inside the represented data space rather than in arbitrary outside coordinates.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is neighborhood meaning. States that behave like neighbors should stay close along the learned space, and changes that alter behavior should show up as changes in path, bend, shape, or movement of probability.

Allowed Movement

What may change is smoothing, denoising, transporting probability, bending the distance rule, or re-labeling coordinates. The method should preserve meaningful adjacency and should not treat shortcuts outside the valid data space as real semantic movement.

Why It Works

It works because many failures are failures of the measuring ruler. A direct coordinate ruler can cut through invalid space, like measuring straight through a mountain. A path ruler asks whether there is a meaningful route through data-like states. Shape summaries can notice when an attack changes the cloud, not just one point. Local distance learned from denoising can reveal which changes the model treats as natural. Diffusion time matters because early noise and late clean samples are different stages, not one interchangeable coordinate. The method earns trust when its distance predicts behavior better than a straight-line ruler chosen for convenience.

representation distance is useful when it predicts behavior better than straight-line distance in arbitrary coordinates

Evidence Check

Compare road-like neighbors with straight-line neighbors, track shape summaries under ordinary and adversarial changes, measure whether smoothing preserves rare valid states, test probability-cloud directions under distribution shifts, validate learned distances against denoising transitions, and show that diffusion time changes sampling behavior.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when shape summaries are unstable under sampling noise, when learned distances reflect preprocessing rather than meaning, when smoothing erases rare but valid modes, when probability-transport directions are too expensive or not uniquely supported, or when diffusion time is modeled without behavioral evidence.

Connection

Quotient diffusion removes irrelevant symmetries; small valid-surface moves keep samples on allowed spaces; fair transport treats social constraints as allowed matches. These geometry papers ask the broader question underneath them: what space should model movement actually be measured in?

EXEMPLAR 125

NAD, Neural Concept Verifier, CRV, Veritas, And Rashomon Trust

Testing Whether An Explanation Would Fail If It Were Wrong

Problem

An explanation can sound clean and still be weak. A doctor saying "because of fever" is not enough if the fever did not affect the diagnosis. A model explanation has the same problem: it may name a concept, neuron, or graph pattern that is readable to people but not actually responsible for the answer.

State Space

The state is internal activation values, small neuron set, concept encoding, attribution graph, evidence offered by a prover, verifier decision, reasoning trace, another accurate model, explanation set, and output label or answer.

Object

The object is concept evidence that has to pass a check. A concept explanation should not merely be readable; it should give a checker a reason to trust or distrust the answer. NAD turns sparse activations and neuron agreement into evidence about correctness. Neural Concept Verifier makes an explanation persuade a separate verifier. CRV uses structure in attribution graphs to detect reasoning errors. Veritas links generated claims to verification structure. Rashomon Trust asks whether the same explanation holds across many accurate models, not just one. The formal phrase is verifier-tested concept evidence: the named concept must change what an independent check believes about the answer.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is explanatory responsibility. The named concept, neuron set, graph feature, or evidence item should be necessary or sufficient enough to change the verifier's belief about the answer.

Allowed Movement

What may change is compressing activations into concepts, choosing sparse evidence, comparing attribution graphs, or checking other equally accurate models. The explanation must preserve the causal link between explanation and decision, not only human readability.

Why It Works

It works by putting the explanation under pressure. A sparse explanation has less room to hide irrelevant details. A recurring concept is less likely to be a one-off accident. A prover-verifier setup asks whether evidence can convince a separate decision rule. Comparing several accurate models matters because two models can get the same answer for different reasons. What must stay the same is explanatory responsibility. What may change is the concept set, verifier, graph view, or comparison model. A strong explanation is one that would break, or at least lose support, when the named evidence is removed or swapped.

an explanation is valid when changing its concept evidence changes the verifier decision and the explanation remains stable across relevant competing models

Evidence Check

Remove concepts, test sparse neuron sets, measure agreement across examples, swap prover evidence, build attribution-graph counterexamples, check whether verifier confidence matches correctness, and compare several accurate models on the same explanation.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when concepts are chosen after seeing the answer, when the verifier uses the same shortcut as the prover, when sparse neurons are correlated but not responsible, when graph patterns are dataset-specific, or when too few competing models are checked to expose explanation instability.

Connection

FlashTrace and MDA trace responsibility backward to inputs or data; causal route gating tests internal interventions; consequence-based utility verifies by downstream effects. NAD, Neural Concept Verifier, CRV, Veritas, and Rashomon Trust ask the interpretability version of the same question: what evidence would make the explanation false if it were wrong?

EXEMPLAR 126

Time Series Saliency Maps, MemExplainer, Universal Redundancies In TSFMs, And Information Shapes Koopman Representation

Finding Which Past Event Changed The Future Prediction

Problem

A fever today may matter less than a medication taken last week. A traffic jam now may come from an accident that disappeared from the current camera view. In time-series problems, the important evidence is often not the biggest signal now, but the earlier event that changed memory, rhythm, or trajectory.

State Space

The state is observation history, timestamped event, memory attached to a node, recurrent hidden state, time-to-frequency transform, forecast horizon, repeated feature group, simplified evolution operator, hidden dynamics coordinate, and predicted future distribution.

Object

The object is the contribution of history to the state used for prediction. The method asks which past event changed the memory the model is using now, not merely which past event was large or recent. Time Series Saliency Maps follow responsibility through reversible transforms so evidence can be measured in time or frequency and mapped back. MemExplainer traces temporal graph memory back to source events. Universal Redundancies in TSFMs asks which repeated time features can be removed without changing forecasts. Information Shapes Koopman Representation studies coordinates where a messy changing process becomes easier to track over time. The formal phrase is history contribution to predictive state: the part of the past that actually changes the state from which the future is predicted.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is future-relevant information carried by history. An explanation should identify past events, frequencies, memories, or dynamics coordinates whose change would change the forecast.

Allowed Movement

What may change is converting time signals into frequency, compressing repeated history, backtracking graph memory, or lifting the system into easier-to-track coordinates. The method should preserve which historical information is responsible for the future prediction.

Why It Works

It works by treating prediction as keeping a useful state of the past. A heatmap over time helps only if it follows information through transformations and back to the original signal. A memory explanation helps only if the named event changed the memory later used. Redundancy analysis helps because repeated features may carry the same future information, so removing one copy may not change the forecast. Easier-to-track dynamics coordinates help when a messy changing process has a transformed view where tomorrow's state is easier to follow from today's state. The proof is a change test: alter the past event and see whether the future estimate changes through the claimed memory route.

a past event explains a forecast when changing that event changes the future state estimate through the learned memory or transform

Evidence Check

Delete or replace historical windows, compare time-based and frequency-based attributions, trace memory updates back to source events, remove redundant channels, test forecast degradation, and verify whether simplified dynamics coordinates preserve multi-step predictive information.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when transforms create artifacts, when deleting an event changes the whole data setting rather than just the evidence, when memory attribution confuses recency with responsibility, when redundancy changes with the forecast horizon, or when the transformed dynamics fit the data but do not explain it.

Connection

Latent Hawkes and OU identifiability recover temporal mechanisms; generative filtering treats hidden state as sufficient belief; video evidence pages ask which frames control an answer. These temporal explanation papers add the attribution layer: which part of the past changed the state from which the future was predicted?

EXEMPLAR 127

Hallucination Rate-Distortion, RMT Diffusion, Identity Bridge, And Biased Generalization

Knowing When The Model Did Not Store Enough Evidence

Problem

A student may remember the shape of a map but not whether a tiny town is on it. If forced to answer, they may guess a plausible town. Models can do the same with facts. The issue is not only honesty or whether confidence matches correctness; sometimes the model did not store enough evidence, stored it in a hard-to-recover direction, or learned a pattern that reconstructs plausible claims instead of checking membership.

State Space

The state is the fact set, the question being asked, how much storage the model effectively has, the direction where a fact is stored, training split, generated sample, disagreement across outputs, uneven evidence directions, position or reversal bias, memorized example, and generated claim.

Object

The object is an evidence code with limited space and biased directions. A fact has to be stored in enough detail, and in a direction the model can later read, or the model will fill the gap with a plausible guess. Hallucination Rate-Distortion models factual hallucination as what happens when the model stores a compressed, imperfect record of membership in a sparse fact set. RMT Diffusion links output disagreement to finite data and uneven data directions. Identity Bridge shows that reversal failure can come from a bias in how a simple transformer stores direction. Biased Generalization warns that better test loss can still move generated samples closer to training examples. The formal phrase is capacity-limited evidence coding: the model must store enough evidence bits and readable directions to answer a query, rather than reconstruct a plausible guess from an incomplete record.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is evidence relevant to the query. The model should retain the bits or directions needed to distinguish known, unknown, reversed, copied, and merely plausible facts.

Allowed Movement

What may change is compression, generalization, sampling, denoising, or adding training examples. Those changes should not turn missing evidence into confident claims, mistake uneven storage directions for certainty, or improve average loss by copying training examples more closely.

Why It Works

It works by naming the bottleneck. If the fact set is too large or too sparse for the storage budget, some membership questions must be answered with error. If data is finite and uneven, different samples can disagree for structural reasons, not just random noise. If the training setup never teaches reversal symmetry, the model may know "A relates to B" without knowing "B relates to A." If lower loss comes with generated samples closer to training data, factual-looking improvement may be memorization pressure. What must stay the same is evidence relevant to the query; what may change is compression or generalization. The method is honest when it separates stored evidence from plausible reconstruction.

truthful factual recall requires enough stored evidence to separate membership from plausible reconstruction under the model's capacity

Evidence Check

Test sparse membership questions, reversal pairs, generated-sample closeness to training data, disagreement across training splits, uneven representation directions, refusing to answer when evidence is missing, and whether stated confidence matches correctness separately for known facts, unknown facts, and plausible distractors.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the fact world is too dense for the model's storage picture, when disagreement is treated as a truth test by itself, when added examples fix one reversal problem but break another, or when average loss hides a shift toward copying training examples.

Connection

Binary RAR asks for contradiction-free support, evidence-budgeted search asks when to abstain, and provenance pages ask where claims came from. This group explains the deeper limit: a model cannot truthfully answer from evidence it never stored or cannot recover in a usable form.

EXEMPLAR 128

Length Generalization Bounds, Coverage Principle, Intrinsic Entropy, And Noisy Sample Compression

Having Enough Examples To Rule Out The Wrong Rule

Problem

A child may see "2, 4, 6" and guess the rule is "add two," but many other rules also fit those three numbers. A model can be powerful enough to represent the right rule and still not have enough evidence to identify it at longer lengths, rare states, or noisy observations. The issue is not what the model could represent; it is what the data actually proves.

State Space

The state is possible rule family, sequence length, source of examples, covered region, uncertainty in the source, training sample, compressed summary of the sample, added noise, target rule, and target generalization setting.

Object

Imagine seeing three numbers, 2, 4, 6. "Add two" fits, but so do many stranger rules that only reveal themselves later. The object is the part of the evidence that rules out those wrong lookalike rules. What must stay the same is rule-identifying information: examples must eliminate alternatives that would fail in the target setting. What may change is sequence length, sample compression, source uncertainty, or added noise. Length Generalization Bounds asks how short examples constrain longer behavior. Coverage Principle studies whether examples cover the regions needed for reliable extension. Intrinsic Entropy measures how many genuinely different clues the examples can provide, rather than raw dataset size. Noisy Sample Compression asks when a small summary can still preserve the family of rules that can be learned even after independent noise is added. The formal phrase is finite evidence support: the limited examples contain enough rule-identifying clues to rule out the wrong rules for the target setting.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is rule-identifying information. Enough evidence must survive sampling, length shift, uncertainty, and noise to choose the intended rule over other rules that fit the observed data.

Allowed Movement

What may change is compressing samples, extrapolating to longer lengths, ignoring repeated examples, or tolerating noise. The learner cannot claim support for parts of the rule that the training distribution never made distinguishable.

Why It Works

It works by separating "can express the rule" from "has evidence for the rule." A transformer may be able to implement a long rule, but length generalization needs examples that pin down the repeated structure. Coverage matters because missing regions leave many lookalike rules alive. Entropy matters because two same-size datasets can carry very different amounts of rule information. Compression matters because a small summary that preserves the learnable family is a certificate of what the sample really proved. The claim is justified only when the observed examples rule out the competing rules that would behave differently in the target setting.

generalization is justified when observed evidence rules out competing rules in the target setting within the sample, uncertainty, and noise budget

Evidence Check

Report extrapolation by length, holes in coverage, subsets controlled for source uncertainty, noise sensitivity, compression size, counterexamples from competing rules, and sample-size curves showing where the target rule becomes identifiable.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when benchmarks test only familiar-region interpolation, when coverage metrics ignore new combinations of parts, when uncertainty estimates miss hidden structure, when compressed summaries preserve labels but not mechanisms, or when noise changes which rule family can be identified.

Connection

Language Generation in the Limit separates computability from feasible learning; scaling-law papers ask what axis creates evidence; structural reasoning probes expose missing rule support. This group gives the shared first-principles answer: learning needs evidence that distinguishes the intended rule from lookalikes.

EXEMPLAR 129

SoftJAX/SoftTorch, Provable NAM Explanations, Categorical ANOVA, And Optimal Compositional Explanations

Searching For Explanations Without Changing What Counts

Problem

Some explanations are made of hard choices: which feature, which branch, which rule, which ranked item, which subset of concepts. Making those choices soft can help training, but it can also change the question. It is like replacing "which three ingredients caused the flavor?" with "what smooth blend of every ingredient scores well?" The answer may be easier to optimize but no longer the explanation we needed.

State Space

The state is discrete operation, softened operation, yes/no mask, rank or sort variable, feature subset, category support, feature interaction, additive component, number of explanation pieces, and model prediction.

Object

Imagine explaining a soup by naming the few ingredients that actually caused the taste. Replacing that with "a smooth blend of every ingredient" may be easier to optimize, but it no longer answers the original question. The object is a search over allowed explanation pieces. What must stay the same is sufficiency under the original rules: the final explanation should still identify the yes/no choices, ranks, categories, or interacting concepts that make the prediction possible. What may change is the temporary softened version used during optimization. SoftJAX/SoftTorch provides trainable versions of Boolean operations, indexing, ranking, sorting, transport, and straight-through choices. Provable NAM Explanations uses additive model structure to find minimal sufficient explanations. Categorical ANOVA separates one-feature effects from interactions when category features depend on each other. Optimal Compositional Explanations shows that overlapping concepts can make simple greedy search miss the best explanation. The formal phrase is a structured explanation search problem: choosing the smallest or clearest allowed pieces that still explain the prediction under the original hard rules.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is sufficiency under the original structure. The searched or softened explanation should still answer which discrete pieces of the model or input make the prediction possible.

Allowed Movement

What may change is softening hard choices, using additive structure, separating interactions, or searching concept combinations. The method should preserve the allowed explanation set and should not let optimization convenience redefine what counts as an explanation.

Why It Works

It works when structure reduces the search without erasing the question. Soft operators help because they let training signals pass through choices that are otherwise hard to optimize, but the final answer must still map back to the original hard choice. Additive models help because each part can be checked separately. ANOVA-style decomposition helps because it separates one-feature effects from interactions. Compositional explanation theory helps because overlapping concepts mean the best set is not always built by adding the best-looking local piece at each step. What must stay the same is that the explanation still contains enough information under the original explanation rules; optimization convenience is allowed only if it returns to those rules.

a structured explanation is valid when the optimized answer maps back to an allowed discrete explanation that remains sufficient for the prediction

Evidence Check

Compare soft and exact discrete answers on small cases, test whether the explanation is minimal, measure sufficiency after mapping back to hard choices, audit interaction terms under dependence, and build overlapping-concept cases where greedy or beam search fails.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when softening changes the allowed set, when additive assumptions hide interactions, when category dependence is misspecified, when concept libraries are incomplete, or when the explanation is minimal only for a stand-in score instead of the explanation the user needed.

Connection

Concept verification asks whether explanations change a verifier; mechanistic attribution asks where source evidence enters a model. This group supplies the optimization layer: how do we search the space of structured explanations without silently changing what counts as an explanation?

EXEMPLAR 130

EMP, Exact RL Unlearning, CV Relative Instability, TRECA, UP-OCP, And Anytime Trees

Proving The Thing The System Will Actually Do

Problem

A bridge safety proof is not useful if it proves the bridge before trucks, weather, and repairs enter the picture. Deployment guarantees have the same issue. Pruning, unlearning, cross-validation, refusing uncertain cases, online coverage, and streaming tree growth all change the system. The proof has to cover that actual operation, not a cleaner operation nearby.

State Space

The state is model component, score mass kept after pruning, policy trajectory, deleted sample, distance between before-and-after behavior, cross-validation fold, confidence interval, refusal set for uncertain cases, quantile error used for coverage, online coverage state, stream prefix, and candidate tree split.

Object

Imagine a bridge certificate that only applies before trucks, weather, and repairs touch the bridge. That certificate is not enough for the bridge people will use. The object is the condition that stays stable while the real operation happens. What must stay the same is the promised guarantee after the system actually prunes, deletes data, refuses uncertain cases, updates coverage, compares folds, or repeatedly tries tree splits. What may change is the operation itself, as long as the proof follows that operation rather than a cleaner one nearby. EMP bounds pruning by tracking retained score mass and loss change. Exact RL Unlearning checks deletion through behavior-distance stability while keeping missed-opportunity cost low. CV Relative Instability argues that intervals should be stable for comparisons, not just single methods. TRECA protects refusal-risk estimates when helper estimates are imperfect. UP-OCP turns online coverage into controlled accumulated error. Anytime Trees uses tests that remain valid even when the stream keeps offering chances to split. The formal phrase is an operational stability condition: the guarantee remains true while the system performs the actual operation it will use in deployment.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the guarantee's target after the operation occurs: pruning should preserve loss, unlearning should remove dependence on deleted data, validation should preserve interval validity, refusing uncertain cases should preserve risk control, online coverage should preserve how often the true answer falls inside the range, and streaming tests should preserve false-split control.

Allowed Movement

What may change is removing components, deleting data, resampling folds, refusing uncertain cases, updating coverage, or growing a tree. Each operation must satisfy the stability condition that makes the final certificate meaningful.

Why It Works

It works when the proof controls the same movement deployment performs. Pruning bounds matter when they connect removed components to loss change. Unlearning stability matters when deletion is visible as independence from the removed data, not merely good accuracy afterward. Relative cross-validation stability matters because we compare methods, not isolated scores. Abstention estimates need protection against imperfect helper models. Online coverage needs accumulated errors controlled over time. Streaming tree tests need protection because repeated split attempts can otherwise make random noise look significant. The proof is useful only when it covers the actual moving system, not a cleaner system nearby.

a deployment guarantee is valid when every allowed operation keeps the certified quantity inside the stated stability, regret, or testing bound

Evidence Check

Report retained score mass, loss changes after pruning, tests for remaining dependence on deleted data, missed-opportunity and coverage curves, fold-level comparison instability, sensitivity to helper-estimation error, whether refusal confidence matches actual risk, and false-split rates under streams.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the certificate controls a proxy operation, when pruning scores do not match component function, when deletion is judged only by accuracy, when folds are dependent in an unmodeled way, when helper-estimation errors are too large, or when stream adaptation violates the test design.

Connection

Finite test certification, floating-point expressivity, watermark robustness, and low-bit quantization all ask whether a guarantee describes the object actually deployed. This group states the general rule: a proof matters when its stability condition matches the operation the system will perform.

EXEMPLAR 131

PoPE, MrRoPE, Robust Filter Attention, Attention Mean-Field, And Transformer Outputs

Keeping A Fact And Its Address Separate

Problem

A library needs both the book text and the shelf address. If the address is wrong, the right book cannot be found. If the address overwrites the title, the catalog becomes useless. Long-context models face the same problem: they must remember what a token says and where it appeared, especially when the evidence is far away.

State Space

The state is token content, position index, rotation used for position, distance from the current token, attention rule, bias toward recent text, context length, output sequences the model can reach, uncertainty over old evidence, and positions beyond the training length.

Object

The object is addressable evidence: content plus the pointer needed to find it later. The model needs to preserve both "what was said" and "where it was said," because a fact without an address may be impossible to retrieve and an address without content is empty. PoPE separates content from position. MrRoPE treats longer positions like changing the number system used for addresses. Robust Filter Attention treats old evidence as something that becomes more uncertain with distance. Attention Mean-Field studies what happens when attention flows through very long context. Transformer Outputs asks which sequences an architecture can or cannot produce. The formal phrase is position-content factorization: keeping the fact and its address recoverable as separate pieces.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is addressable evidence. When a fact appears far away, the model should preserve both the fact and the address needed to retrieve it, without turning position into a shortcut for content.

Allowed Movement

What may change is position scaling, rotation, filtering old tokens, changing attention flow, or bounding reachable outputs. The method must preserve which content is being addressed and how distance changes uncertainty.

Why It Works

It works by separating the note from the location of the note. Content is the evidence. Position is the pointer. Long-context methods help only when they keep those two jobs distinct outside the lengths seen in training. Filtering helps when old context is treated as uncertain evidence, not as dead text, so far-away facts are discounted only when distance really makes retrieval less reliable. Reachability bounds matter because they show when the model's architecture blocks some outputs no matter how much context is supplied. What must stay the same is evidence that can still be found by its address; what may change is how position and attention are scaled.

long-context use is reliable when the fact and its address remain separately recoverable as context length grows

Evidence Check

Swap content while holding positions fixed, swap positions while holding content fixed, test longer lengths, retrieve under distractors, ablate position encodings, measure uncertainty with distance, and check outputs that should be impossible if the architectural bound is real.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when position encodings memorize training-length patterns, when filtering deletes rare but decisive evidence, when separating position and content helps retrieval but hurts local syntax, or when reachability analysis ignores decoding and tool use around the model.

Connection

ThinKV and EntroKV compress context into sufficient state; LLMs Get Lost tests whether long context remains navigable; token sufficiency pages ask which evidence survives a budget. This group names the coordinate problem underneath them: long context works only when evidence and address do not collapse into one brittle signal.

EXEMPLAR 132

Rational Transductors, DiScoFormer, Exact GNN Algorithms, Which Algorithms Can GNNs Learn, Constrained Transformers, And Deep Flow Networks

Building The Rule Into The Machine

Problem

A calculator that memorizes a few multiplication examples is not doing multiplication. It has to carry the right intermediate state and apply the same rule again and again. Neural models can make the same mistake: they can fit small algorithm examples while failing once the graph, sequence, or problem size changes.

State Space

The state is symbol stream, finite memory state, attention rule, graph node state, how far messages can travel, local instruction, remaining constraint error, flow value, discrete function, and number of repeated steps.

Object

The object is a rule the architecture can actually execute. The model needs an internal state that can be updated again and again in the same way, so the rule still works when the sequence or graph grows. Rational Transductors add finite-state recurrence to attention. DiScoFormer shows attention can recover density and multiscale similarity behavior. Exact GNN Algorithms treats message passing as programmable local instructions. Which Algorithms Can GNNs Learn asks when small-graph training can or cannot extend to larger graphs. Constrained Transformers push intermediate states toward valid descent steps. Deep Flow Networks represent value functions for flow problems on discrete structures. The formal phrase is an executable update rule: the model has the state and step-by-step operation needed to run the algorithm, not only imitate examples.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the computation under scale shift. The same rule should still execute when sequence length, graph size, or problem instance changes.

Allowed Movement

What may change is recurrence, local messages, similarity rules, constraints, or flow structure. The architecture must preserve the algorithmic state transition rather than merely preserve input-output correlations from the training distribution.

Why It Works

It works when the model's basic operations match the computation. Finite-state recurrence helps when the rule needs memory across positions. Attention helps when the task is comparing or aggregating similar items. Message passing helps when a graph algorithm can be made from local instructions. Constraint-shaped layers help when every step should move closer to a valid solution. Flow networks help when the target is about moving limited resources through a discrete structure. The proof step is scale: if the internal update is really the rule, it should keep working on larger states where memorized training examples are no longer enough.

algorithmic generalization holds when the learned step preserves the target rule on larger states, not only observed examples

Evidence Check

Test larger graphs and longer sequences, compare internal states to algorithm variables, run counterexamples outside training size, remove recurrence or constraints, verify required invariances, and report whether errors come from one bad step or accumulated drift.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the computation requires global information outside the architecture's communication radius, when recurrence stores spurious state, when constraints make optimization easier but not algorithmically faithful, or when extrapolation tests stay too close to training templates.

Connection

Transformer Circuits maps clustering into attention blocks; Softmax Turing and HATSolver show formal expressivity; CRAMF and verifier pages check symbolic artifacts. These papers connect the big picture: reasoning is credible only when the system can implement the computation the answer depends on.

EXEMPLAR 133

DFM Bounds, GWF, LoRFS, CoEvol-NO, L2G-Net, And Modified SINNs

Checking The Route, Not Only The Final Answer

Problem

A hiking route can end at the right cabin but still be unsafe if it crossed a cliff. Scientific generators and solvers have the same issue. A final sample, field, or graph result can look plausible even if the probability mass, physical field, mesh, or long-range graph signal took the wrong path to get there.

State Space

The state is probability path, distance between probability clouds, accumulated mismatch, diffusion path, physical field, rule error, frequency coefficient, graph long-range pattern, mesh coordinate, hidden solver state, and predict-then-correct update.

Object

The object is an approximation whose route stays accurate. The method must control what happens at every step, because a small wrong move can be carried forward until the final answer looks plausible but came from the wrong process. DFM Bounds studies whether diffusion flow matching moves probability toward the target correctly. GWF derives generation objectives as movement between probability clouds. LoRFS replaces noisy sample checks with analytic rule errors for physical equations. CoEvol-NO keeps hidden solver state and mesh coordinates evolving together. L2G-Net preserves long-range graph information without computing the full expensive decomposition. Modified SINNs use frequency structure for high-dimensional solvers. The formal phrase is a path-accurate approximation: the accumulated error along the route stays small enough for the final claim.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the rule that lives along the trajectory: mass should move along the intended probability path, physical fields should satisfy their equations, graph signals should preserve long-range structure, and mesh or solver updates should not invent dynamics.

Allowed Movement

What may change is approximating flows, compressing long-range graph structure, replacing sampled checks with closed-form rule checks, or updating mesh coordinates. The method must control error that compounds along the path.

Why It Works

It works by asking where the error lives. A sampler needs distances between probability clouds, not only final visual quality. A physical solver needs equation-rule errors, not only point labels. A graph solver needs long-range patterns that local messages can miss. A changing mesh needs the hidden solver state to stay coupled to the mesh. What must stay the same is the rule along the route. What may change is the cheaper approximation used to follow it. The output is only trustworthy when the accumulated route error stays below what the final claim can tolerate.

an approximation is valid when accumulated path error stays below the tolerance required by the final claim

Evidence Check

Report whether probability paths settle toward the target, rule-error size over time, recovery of long-range graph patterns, mesh-update stability, long-horizon rollout error, analytic versus sampled rule-check ablations, and cases where final metrics hide path violations.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the chosen measure ignores the decisive rule, when compression loses local breaks, when rule objectives are exact for the wrong boundary regime, when mesh updates chase numerical artifacts, or when guarantees about settling toward the target require assumptions not met by the data.

Connection

IRNO treats physical surrogates as stable operators; FALCON and FIDIA preserve scientific quantities through sampling; machine-coupled dynamics asks whether implementation preserves the math. This group gives the shared principle: the output is only as trustworthy as the path used to reach it.

EXEMPLAR 134

SlaClip, PRISM, IHM, LM Memorization Capacity, Empirical Privacy Protection Benchmark, OPL/G-OPL, And PetaGAIL++

Adding Protection Where Private Information Actually Leaks

Problem

Blurring every part of a photo by the same amount may hide a face but leave a unique tattoo visible. Privacy in models is similar. Noise, clipping, projection, and synthetic generation help only if they act on the places where individual information is actually encoded.

State Space

The state is private sample, update size, clipping threshold, noise level, low-rank update factors, local update space, second-order sensitivity sketch, memorized bits, membership attack query, trajectory sensitivity, identity-carrying subspace, and task utility.

Object

Imagine hiding private details in a photo. Blurring the sky does nothing if the face, tattoo, or doorway carries the identity. The object is controlled release in the directions that carry private traces. What must stay the same is population or task usefulness after one person's contribution becomes hard to detect. What may change is clipping, noise, projection, sketching, or synthetic path generation. SlaClip adapts clipping using slack already present in private training. PRISM adds privacy noise to the actual low-rank update rather than to arbitrary factor coordinates. IHM improves private linear regression by sketching sensitive second-order information, meaning how sharply different directions affect the fitted model. LM Memorization Capacity asks how many bits a model can store when generalization is removed. Empirical Privacy Protection Benchmark tests exposure when adaptation data is close to training data. OPL/G-OPL remove identity-bearing video directions while preserving anomaly signals. PetaGAIL++ generates private trajectories with sensitivity in mind. The formal phrase is geometry-sensitive information release: privacy protection is placed where private traces actually live in the model or data representation.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the population or task signal after individual information has been made hard to distinguish along the directions where private exposure can occur.

Allowed Movement

What may change is clipping, sketching, adding noise, projecting away identity, generating synthetic trajectories, or reparameterizing low-rank updates. The method must account for where private information is actually encoded.

Why It Works

It works when the privacy method matches the place information leaks. Adaptive clipping helps because clipping too much wastes usefulness while clipping too little lets one person move the result too much. Low-rank privacy helps only when noise attaches to the actual update, not to a rewrite of the update that can leak the same fact. Second-order sketches help because some directions affect the fitted model more sharply than others. Projection helps when identity lies in a removable direction while the task signal lies elsewhere. Path privacy helps because some routes reveal far more about a person than others. What must stay the same is population usefulness after individual traces become hard to distinguish.

privacy improves when noise or projection targets private-information directions while preserving task-relevant population signal

Evidence Check

Report membership attacks, canary extraction, privacy accounting, utility under clipping changes, tests that low-rank reparameterization does not change private exposure, sensitivity to second-order directions, identity-removal ablations, trajectory-sensitivity curves, and private exposure when adaptation data is close to training data.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the private direction is misidentified, when projection removes the task signal, when clipping statistics leak extra information, when low-rank assumptions break, when synthetic trajectories preserve unique routines, or when privacy tests ignore how close adaptation data is to training data.

Connection

Secret Memory and high-dimensional Gaussian mechanisms give resource and noise limits; certified unlearning asks for deletion indistinguishability; provenance pages ask which data process produced an artifact. This group connects them: privacy is about controlling information flow where data actually leaves traces.

EXEMPLAR 135

Assistant Axis, Robust Harmful Features, Surgery, Suppress And Diversify, Backdoor Self-Awareness, And SVGT

Finding The Hidden Route To Unsafe Behavior

Problem

A machine can look safe while one hidden switch still causes failure. A model can do the same: it may give safe answers on ordinary prompts while keeping an internal route that produces harmful behavior when pressure, triggers, persona drift, or fine-tuning activates it.

State Space

The state is the model before the final answer: internal numbers, attention parts, repeated routing patterns, hidden harmful signs, intended assistant behavior, trigger conditions, fine-tuning changes, bridge tokens, and decoded output. The formal terms are activation for an internal number, attention head for one routing part, and value vector for a direction used to influence generation.

Object

Imagine a machine that works normally until one hidden switch is pressed. The repair question is not "does the machine ever fail?" but "which switch starts the failure?" These papers ask for the model version of that switch: the hidden route where pressure, persona drift, a trigger, or fine-tuning changes the answer before the final words appear. The formal phrase is a localized internal mechanism: a hidden route that changes unsafe behavior when we change that route directly.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is safe useful behavior under pressure. Fluency and helpful capability should remain, while the internal route that causes harmful behavior is detected, bounded, or redirected.

Allowed Movement

What may change is watching internal numbers, keeping the model near the intended assistant behavior, limiting repeated attention traps, steering safety-related directions, diversifying routes, or asking the model to expose triggers. The change must touch the route that actually makes the unsafe output, not merely a surface sign that travels with it.

Why It Works

It works when unsafe behavior has an internal warning sign that appears before the final text. A direction is useful if moving along it changes harmfulness while preserving ordinary capability. A sink signal is useful if harmful fine-tuning repeatedly routes information through the same heads. Trigger recovery helps if the hidden poisoned condition can become inspectable. Value steering helps if safety values can guide decoding without rewriting the whole model. What must stay the same is safe useful behavior; what may change is targeted movement on the suspected route. The test is causal: change the route and harmful output should change in the predicted way.

internal safety control works when changing the named route changes harmful output while preserving useful behavior outside that route

Evidence Check

Patch activations, remove heads, run jailbreak pressure tests, compare traces before and after fine-tuning, measure distance from the intended assistant region, recover triggers, sweep steering strength, and test capability retention on benign prompts.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when unsafe behavior is spread across many weak routes, when a direction detects style rather than harm, when steering pushes the model outside normal behavior, when trigger descriptions are invented, or when benign capability depends on the same route being suppressed.

Connection

Pressure Reveals Character measures hidden tendency under adaptive tests; FlowGuard separates multimodal safety routes; Causal Route Gating tests internal causal interventions. This group adds the safety mechanism layer: the output is downstream of internal state, and safety has to control the state where unsafe behavior is formed.

EXEMPLAR 136

Modern Conservation Laws, Gradient Flow Through Diagram Expansions, Non-Euclidean EoS, WSM, Polar Express, And RACO

Moving The Model Without Breaking What The Path Must Preserve

Problem

Training is not only "make the loss smaller." It is a route through model space. One route may preserve a useful skill, another may erase it. One update may be safe for a smooth direction but too large for a fragile one. Optimizers matter because they decide how the model is allowed to move.

State Space

Imagine moving a heavy table through a narrow room. You care about where the table ends up, but also which walls it scrapes, which leg carries too much weight, and whether the path still fits through the doorway. The state is the model's current knob setting, the proposed push, the rule that resizes the push, memory of earlier pushes, the learning-rate schedule, saved checkpoints, fragile directions, the ruler used to measure a move, quantities the path should keep steady, goals that ask for opposite moves, and the final compromise.

Object

The object is the training route plus the promises the route must keep. Some facts should stay nearly fixed while training moves. Some directions are delicate: a small push can damage a skill. Some goals pull against each other. Modern Conservation Laws names facts that stay fixed under idealized training routes. Gradient flow is the smooth, continuous version of following error downhill. Gradient Flow Through Diagram Expansions studies how update pushes pass through structured expansions. Non-Euclidean EoS says stability depends on the ruler used to measure movement. WSM treats learning-rate decay as weighting past checkpoints. Polar Express shapes matrix updates for the machine operations hardware performs cheaply. RACO trims preference pushes that fight each other. The formal phrase is preserved path geometry: the update route is judged by both what it improves and what it must not disturb.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful movement. The model should get better on the chosen target while keeping the skills, stable margins, saved-checkpoint benefits, and visible compromises that make the final model usable. What may change is the exact step rule used to get there.

Allowed Movement

What may change is shrinking or stretching updates, averaging checkpoints, separating directions, trimming moves that fight each other, normalizing matrices, or changing the movement ruler. Each change must respect what the route has to protect: fragile skills, quantities that should stay steady, competing goals, and machine limits.

Why It Works

It works by replacing "take a step downhill" with "move in the way this model can safely move." A downhill step can still break something if it pushes a delicate part too hard. A different ruler can reveal that two same-sized steps are not equally risky. A checkpoint window can preserve useful past states instead of trusting only the newest state. A hardware-shaped matrix rule helps only if the cheaper machine operation still moves the model accurately. Conflict trimming helps only when two goals really ask for opposite moves and the trimmed move keeps the important parts of both. What must stay the same is useful capability and stability along the path; what may change is the update rule used to move.

an update is justified when it improves the target while preserving protected skills, stable quantities, conflict handling, and machine-realizable movement

Evidence Check

Track the protected quantities, step size in delicate directions, what changes when checkpoint windows are removed, numerical error from hardware-friendly matrix steps, whether two goals ask for opposite moves, remaining compromise error, hardware throughput, and downstream capability drift after optimizer changes.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the chosen distance rule ignores a fragile capability, when preserved quantities describe an idealized flow but not finite-step training, when checkpoint averaging hides rare regressions, when hardware-friendly iterations lose accuracy, or when clipping suppresses the update needed for the most important goal.

Connection

DiReCT chooses data by how strongly it can bend training; Beyond Muon and Adam Degeneracy explain optimizer regimes; operational stability pages ask whether guarantees survive the deployed loop. This group connects them: training recipes work when their movement is shaped by the right path rules, not because a named optimizer is inherently good.

EXEMPLAR 137

KDE Kernel Algebra, FlashSketch, FlashSinkhorn, Matroid Algorithms, IO-Aware GNNs, And TileLang

Counting The Cost Of Getting The Data, Not Just Doing The Math

Problem

A recipe can be simple but slow if every step requires walking to another building for an ingredient. Algorithms have the same issue. The bottleneck may be moving memory, asking expensive queries, building a huge matrix, handling sparse layouts, or synchronizing machines, not the number of arithmetic operations on paper.

State Space

The state is query access, set size, pairwise-similarity matrix, compressed sketch, memory hierarchy, sparse graph layout, tensor tile, bandwidth budget, synchronization point, intermediate tensor, and approximation error.

Object

The object is the real access pattern: what information the algorithm reads, moves, materializes, queries, or approximates at each step. A method is not cheap if it avoids arithmetic but secretly builds a huge table, makes many hidden queries, or moves data through memory in an expensive order. KDE Kernel Algebra sharpens access limits for density operations. FlashSketch and FlashSinkhorn redesign sketching and transport around memory-efficient kernels. Matroid Algorithms charge queries by realistic set access. IO-Aware GNNs reorganize graph computation around memory traffic. TileLang makes tensor layout explicit so computation matches hardware. The formal phrase is an access model: the rules and costs for getting information, not only calculating with it.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the answer the algorithm promised to compute, under the real cost of getting the data. The cheaper version should still preserve the needed similarity, matching, graph update, set choice, or tensor operation while using reads and writes the machine can afford.

Allowed Movement

What may change is sketching, fusing similarity computations, tiling tensors, changing sparse layouts, limiting queries, or approximating transport. The method must account for what information was not accessed and how that omission changes the claim.

Why It Works

It works when the slow part is replaced by work the system can actually do cheaply. A sketch helps if the short note still keeps the fact needed later. Flash-style similarity computations help if they avoid building a giant temporary table while giving the same usable answer. Query costs matter because a lookup can hide real work. IO-aware graph methods help because graph learning is often slowed by moving data, not by arithmetic. Tiling helps because tensor math runs on concrete memory blocks and compute units. What must stay the same is the answer within the stated error; what may change is how the data is reached and moved.

an efficiency claim is valid when the cheaper access pattern preserves the target object within error while reducing reads, writes, queries, or synchronization

Evidence Check

Report memory traffic, materialized intermediates, query size, similarity-approximation error, graph throughput, sparse-layout sensitivity, tile occupancy, synchronization cost, and accuracy under the reduced access pattern.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the cost model omits the real bottleneck, when sketches preserve average behavior but lose rare modes, when fused similarity computations change numerical stability, when queries hide preprocessing cost, or when a layout optimized for one hardware regime becomes bad in another.

Connection

TileLang asks how tensor computation maps to hardware; ParetoPO budgets tool calls; learning-augmented algorithms price advice. This group gives the systems-level first principle: capability expands when the needed object can be accessed at the cost the deployment loop can pay.

EXEMPLAR 138

Axiomatic Value Of Regularization, Beyond Log Likelihood, RACO, AuxDPO, DPO Unchained, And What Preferences Can And Cannot Predict

Remembering That A Loss Function Is A Choice Rule

Problem

When people vote, the counting rule changes the winner. Preference training has the same issue. A loss function is not neutral bookkeeping. It decides what counts as evidence, how conflicts are combined, how much to trust the old action rule, and what kind of social choice the final model inherits.

State Space

Imagine a committee choosing a menu from messy votes. Some people compare two dishes, some votes conflict, the old menu may have traditions worth keeping, and the counting rule can change the winner. The state is the human comparison evidence, the old behavior the model may need to stay near, how strongly that old behavior is protected, supervised examples, model capability level, update pushes that fight each other, helper variables, voting rule, substitute reward, and the final odds of different behaviors. The formal terms are reference policy for the old action rule, likelihood for how much the model already expected the shown tokens, and policy distribution for the final menu of likely behaviors.

Object

The object is the counting rule for preference training. It says whose comparisons count, how conflicts are settled, how much old behavior is protected, and what the data is too weak to decide. Axiomatic Value of Regularization asks which voting-like properties change when training stays close to the old behavior. Beyond Log Likelihood shows that simply rewarding examples the model found likely can work against stronger models. RACO trims preference pushes that fight each other. AuxDPO adds helper quantities to better match the local human-feedback process. DPO Unchained and preference-limit papers ask what comparisons can and cannot reveal. The formal phrase is an alignment objective as decision rule: the training loss is the counting rule that turns imperfect human comparisons into model movement.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the intended value order: the behaviors people truly prefer should still end up ahead after noisy votes, old-behavior protection, model expectations, and conflicting goals have been converted into training movement.

Allowed Movement

What may change is staying closer to the old behavior, changing how much demonstrated text is trusted, adding helper quantities, trimming update pushes that fight each other, or choosing a different preference-counting rule. The method must say what choice rule it is using and what the comparison data can actually distinguish.

Why It Works

It works when the training rule matches the evidence. Staying close to the old model helps only if the old model carries behavior worth preserving. Reweighting demonstrated text helps only when the model's own expectations separate signal from noise instead of amplifying bias. Conflict-aware updates help when two goals genuinely ask for opposite moves. Helper quantities help when a simple comparison leaves out context that human feedback used. Limits on preference learning matter because no counting rule can recover a value order that the labels never separated. What must stay the same is intended value order; what may change is how noisy comparisons are converted into movement.

an alignment update is valid when the training movement preserves the intended preference ordering despite old-behavior constraints, label noise, and conflicting goals

Evidence Check

Report which social-choice rule the loss satisfies, sensitivity to old-behavior weight, comparisons across model capability levels, whether update pushes fight each other, remaining compromise error, preference-noise stress tests, and cases where the labels cannot distinguish two behaviors.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the training rule builds in the wrong social choice, when the old behavior being protected is bad, when demonstrated-text weighting trusts the model's old expectations too much, when trimming conflicting updates suppresses minority constraints, or when preference data is too weak to reveal the desired ordering.

Connection

Omni-Reward and JudgeBench treat reward as a stand-in measuring path; performative misalignment asks how behavior changes under deployment; ParetoPO handles cost-accuracy tradeoffs. This group names the alignment-level principle: a loss is not neutral. It is a decision rule over imperfect human evidence.

EXEMPLAR 139

SleepLM, BioX-Bridge, Seizure-Semiology-Suite, WAVE, And Bio-Signal Foundation Interfaces

Turning Body Signals Into Claims A Clinician Can Check

Problem

A heart trace, sleep trace, or seizure video is not just a sequence of numbers or frames. It is noisy evidence about a body event. A useful model must translate that evidence into language a clinician can read while still pointing back to the measured signal and timing.

State Space

The state is waveform, sensor channel, sampling rate, sleep event, hidden physiological state, clinical vocabulary, report sentence, event timing, bridge between modalities, labeled source signal, weakly labeled target signal, and expert judgment.

Object

The object is an interface between raw body measurements and clinical language. Think of a heart trace as a witness, not as a report. The model may translate the trace into words, but the words must still point back to the measured event, the time it happened, the sensor that saw it, and the uncertainty around it. SleepLM aligns sleep recordings with captions, retrieval, event localization, and insight generation. BioX-Bridge transfers information across biosignal types by aligning middle-layer representations instead of retraining whole models. Seizure-Semiology-Suite checks whether clinical video and report reasoning preserve medically meaningful timing. WAVE-style multimodal systems make audio, video, and text access depend on the user's prompt. In plain terms, the interface must keep the hidden body event recoverable while making it readable.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is clinically relevant signal evidence. The model may compress or translate the measurement, but it must preserve the event, timing, uncertainty, and vocabulary a domain expert needs to verify the claim.

Allowed Movement

What may change is signal-to-text alignment, bridging between sensor types, freezing large backbones, training small adapters, retrieving examples, or localizing events. The method must not turn physiological evidence into free-form text that no longer points back to the measured trace.

Why It Works

It works because different measurements can reflect the same body state. Two sensors may have different noise and format while still carrying related physiology. A bridge works if it connects the layer where that shared body state is present, not a layer that only shares device artifacts. A language interface works if captions and reports preserve event timing and medical meaning. A benchmark works if it tests the variables clinicians actually use, not just whether a sentence sounds medical. What must stay the same is signal evidence; what may change is the format used to make that evidence readable.

a biosignal claim is valid when the body event remains recoverable from sensor data and supports the clinical text under expert-checkable timing

Evidence Check

Report event-localization accuracy, retrieval between signal and report, transfer under sensor shift, bridge-layer ablations, parameter and label efficiency, expert disagreement, timing faithfulness, and fluent clinical text that lacks signal support.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the shared body state is weak, when labels encode clinician habit rather than signal truth, when the bridge aligns sensor artifacts, when text summaries erase timing, or when performance is measured without expert-checkable grounding.

Connection

Mind-Omni and PRISM create interfaces for brain signals; expert evaluation pages measure clinical and legal artifacts; temporal explanation pages ask which past events shape a prediction. This group adds the healthcare version: the model must preserve the measured physiological structure before its language can be trusted.

EXEMPLAR 140

MemoryBench, LLMs Get Lost, DecodeShare, Mamba-3, LongWriter-Zero, And ThinkV

Remembering The Right Thing And Revising It When Evidence Changes

Problem

A notebook is useful only if you can find the right note and cross it out when it becomes false. Model memory has the same problem. A system can store many facts, retrieve the wrong one, commit too early to a false assumption, or keep writing coherently after its internal belief no longer matches the task.

State Space

The state is stored memory, retrieval key, conversational assumption, current belief, compressed cache region, recurrent state, long-form plan, verifier signal, uncertainty, and later action or generated paragraph.

Object

The object is memory that can be retrieved and revised. The model needs a stored belief that can be found later, used for the next decision, and changed when new evidence contradicts it. MemoryBench measures whether memory systems retrieve useful information efficiently. LLMs Get Lost studies how early wrong assumptions corrupt later recovery.
